# Changelog

All notable changes to NILS v1 are recorded here. The format follows [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/) and versions follow [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html). The first release will be 1.0.0; until then pre-releases are tagged `v1.0.0-alpha.N`. The history of v0, the 0.x line, is in [kineuro/nils-legacy](https://github.com/kineuro/nils-legacy).

## [Unreleased]

### Added

- The key store at the door (record 55, ruling 35 (c): every step that can live in the desk gets a place in it; gate T31, steps 8.6, 8.7 and 13.4). `GET /api/keys` lists the keys by name with their length, fingerprint and which one the pseudonyms come from, under `database:see`, never a byte of one. `POST /api/keys {name, passphrase}` makes a key from its passphrase as `nils key add` makes it from its input (one final newline dropped, at most 64 bytes), and `POST /api/keys/{name}/remove {confirm}` takes one away with its name typed again; both under `database:work` and `install:work` at detail sensitive, which no ladder step below admin holds. A key is never replaced, and the registry's pseudonym key, made again after a restore, is first tried on what the linkage store keeps: a passphrase that does not open it is refused with nothing kept. A passphrase is never written to the registry, a job, the audit or a log, and no refusal quotes one; an answer that carries one is sent `Cache-Control: no-store`. Each act is audited by the key's name alone (`key.add`, `key.remove`), and a call that acts as an agent or a model is refused whatever its grants.
- Each handover has a password of its own, and encryption is a choice (record 55, ruling 35 (c), Nima 2026-10-16; gate T31, step 13.4; registry schema 105). A release stays a plain folder and a handover an optional step. Inside it the archives are encrypted by default; `nils handover run --no-encrypt --why TEXT` (or `encrypt: false` with a `why` at `POST /api/handovers`) writes them unencrypted, and the handover, its audit row, `handover list` and `handover verify` say so, with the reason. A password is never shared by every handover under a key any more: NILS makes one per handover, derived from the key (`--key`, or the registry's key when none is named) and the handover's id and never stored, or the person handing over types one (`--password-stdin`, never on the command line; `password: typed` at the door), which is kept sealed under the registry's key and never in clear in the registry, a job, a log, an error or the audit. `nils handover password --handover ID` prints one handover's password; `--key` still prints the old shared one, which opens the archives of a handover made before, and says it is the old scheme. `handover verify` no longer needs `--key`. `GET /api/handovers` lists the handovers with how each is kept closed, and `POST /api/handovers/{id}/password` answers one handover's password, no-store. Who may show it, or type one: two new grants, `handover-password:see` and `handover-password:work` (suite contract version 3, in place), which the admin's set holds and a desk group is given like any other, or the person who made the release, for the handovers of their own release; anyone else is refused whatever release grant they hold, and so is an agent or a model. Every show and every typed password is audited by the handover's id alone (`handover.password`, and the `handover` row with `password: typed`). The jobs door queues only `handover run` and `handover verify`, and never a typed password.
- A subject's identifiers at the door, an admin's (record 55, ruling 35 (c); gate T31, step 8.7): what `nils linkage show`, `link`, `unlink` and `purge` do at the keyboard, under `data:work` and `install:work` at detail sensitive, each a person's act and audited. `POST /api/linkage/show {subject}` answers the subject's identifiers by type, where each came from and whether the store keeps it, with no identifier opened; with `values: true` and a `why` it opens the kept ones, one read audit row each, and answers them, except a value that reads as a personnummer, answered as its shape alone (`withheld`). No personnummer and no reserve number is kept as one, so neither can be shown; one filed under another type is not shown either. The linkages that name the subject come with it. `POST /api/linkage/links {a, b, evidence}` records two subjects as one person and queues the merge of `b` into `a`; `POST /api/linkage/links/{id}/unlink` reverses a linkage; `POST /api/linkage/purge` deletes the identifiers and linkages of one subject, or of every subject, only with the subject's code (or `every subject`) typed in `confirm`, and answers what would go with `dry_run`. The keyboard's purge and the door's are one function.

## [1.0.0-alpha.87] - 2026-10-16

Wave 7a's last engine: hospital reserve numbers read beside the personnummer and never kept, spare numbers through a map, model cards that gain their missing claims, the damaged-header flag's three signs (pack contract 11, MRI pack 1.3.0), the post-contrast label's clock across studies (time-rule-2), kept Query cards, LST-AI's thin image, and fixes from the wave's audit.

### Added

- The install door says when it looked at the releases, and looks again when asked. `GET /api/supervise/install` gains `release.looked_at` (UTC, to the second), and `?fresh=1` makes the supervisor look at the releases again where the look it kept is at least `look_again_seconds` old (60 by default: GitHub keeps its own answer for a minute, and an address has sixty requests an hour that every look spends). Where the look is younger, the answer carries the look it has. The desk's Parts page says how old the look is beside its table and asks for a new one with Look again.
- A person keeps a card, and an assistant's draft is no card until they do (record 55, K6; registry schema 103). `PUT /api/ask/documents/{id}/kept` keeps the card a document is a version of and `DELETE` puts it back among the drafts, under `query:work`. A card is a line of versions, and the mark (`kept_by`, `kept_at` on `ask_document`) sits on the line's first document, so keeping any version keeps the line; a card already kept stays kept by whoever kept it first, when they did. It is a person's act: a caller that acts as an agent or a model is refused with 403, since the assistant proposes and a person keeps. Putting a card back deletes nothing, and the mark writes no audit row and moves no epoch. Migration 103 adds the two columns and holds every line the registry had as kept by whoever made it, when it was made, so no card leaves a list at the upgrade. Tested on both backends (`migration_103_*`, `a_draft_is_a_card_only_when_a_person_keeps_it`, `a_draft_is_listed_as_a_card_only_when_a_person_keeps_it`).
- A hospital's reserve number, the number a hospital gives a person who has no personnummer or whose personnummer it does not know, is a built-in ID type, `reserve-number` (registry schema 104), identifying and never kept: like a personnummer, its identity is its keyed lookup alone, with nothing sealed in the linkage store or on a held file's row, and the linkage store applies that to every row of either type it files, whatever a caller sealed (`linkage::keeps`). A map takes it as `identifier:reserve-number`, beside the person's code or another identifier of theirs, in one written form (spaces and one separator dropped, no date or check digit asked of it); a `canonical:reserve-number` column is refused, since no code is ever made from a reserve number, and no dataset writes one into PatientID. Migration 104 adds the type to a linkage store from before, and drops whatever a type of that name a person had made sealed. `nils custody` and the custody page say that a reserve number is never kept.
- `nils custody` and the custody page (`docs/reference/custody.md`) list the stores Wave 7a added (record 55, T36): where each scan's files are and the hashes of their bytes, the pseudonymise records, System 1's opinions, the review rounds and the audit's draws, the blind-first reads, the post-contrast labels, the main scans and their rules, the rule proposals, the slice cache, the pictures (previews and pyramids) and the places export. The linkage store's entry says a personnummer is never kept, not even sealed, and the quarantine list's that a file the read set aside by policy is a row of it, with no review item.
- A registered model's card gains the claims it lacks (record 55, ruling 31 (a)): the label set it was trained on, its tested claims and its speed. `nils model add-claims MODEL --claims FILE` and `POST /api/models/{id}/claims` (under models:work, in the caller's name) add them; the file is the claims in the card's own shape (`trained_on`, `tested`, and `speed` with `cpu`, `gpu` or both), or holds them as `claims` beside the `model` they are for, its `digest` and `why`. Additions only: a claim the card states already is refused and named, and so is a speed with a GPU on a card whose `gpu` is `none`, and any other field, which stays a new version of the model. Each claim is checked as registration checks it: the label set by its digest, registered in this registry and clear of every sample sealed now (records 40 R3 and 55 E6), the tested claims and the speed as the card's contract has them. The model's `trained_on` follows its card. Each addition is a `claims_added` event, holding the fields filled, the claims as added, what the card lacked before and after and why, and an audit row (`model.add_claims`), both written in the transaction that writes the card. `nils model show` says whether a card is complete or what it lacks, and lists what it gained since it was registered; `GET /api/models/{id}` and `nils model show --json` answer it as `additions`. The model contract v1 and the OpenAPI 7 contract are amended in place, additively; no migration.
- The damaged-header flag reads all three of its signs and is set at ingest (record 56 section 3; record 55, ruling 31 (c)). At the end of every sort, each dataset holding a scan the sort judged has its signs read with the sort's own pack and kept beside its flag: its MR scans with neither a series description nor a protocol name, its named MR scans none of whose words the pack knows (folded as the pack folds a series' names for its rules), and its MR scans by a maker the rules were not built on that no dataset brought in before it holds, or by none. Each share is held to the pack's threshold, and a dataset is flagged when one reaches it, so System 1's gate runs over it and stages its answers there for a person to confirm. The sort's result says what it read (`headers`), and so does its log. `GET /api/datasets/{name}/headers` answers each sign with its count, its share, its threshold and whether it flags, the makers it counted, the pack that read them and when, and in words why the dataset is flagged or not. A person's word still wins: a flag set or cleared with a why, from the Data card or with `PUT`, stands until the person lets the signs decide again, and the signs are kept under it. A dataset no sort has read since the signs were kept is read when asked and kept at its next sort; a root, each folder under it a dataset of its own, is flagged only by a person.
- Pack contract 11: one key under `review`, `damaged_headers`, the thresholds of the three signs, the makers the rules were built on and the derived text a series' names are folded by. Refused in a pack that declares less. A pack before contract 11 flags a dataset by the nameless share at a half, as before, and reads the other two signs without flagging on them.
The MRI pack 1.3.0, the damaged-header flag's thresholds (record 56 section 3; record 55, ruling 31 (c)). It declares pack contract 11, so it needs an engine that implements it, the one these notes' Unreleased section describes: an earlier engine refuses it by its contract, and its update says so and waits. `nils update --pin mri@1.2.3` goes back to the version before.

### Changed

- The pseudonymiser reads a PatientID that is no personnummer, in a dataset whose rule reads PatientID as an ID that is the same everywhere, as a reserve number (above), and looks it up under `reserve-number`: a file whose number a map names is that person's and is written like any other, so one person's main number and spare reserve number land on one subject. A file whose number no map names is held under the number's keyed lookup and shown by its shape, no longer under its study's UID, so a map row naming the number releases it and the next run writes it. No subject is ever made from a reserve number, whatever the dataset says of an identifier no map names, and code anyway (`POST /api/linkage/held/code`) leaves such a file held. A file whose study a map named is still found by the study's UID, and a file an earlier run held under its study's UID is released by a map of that UID as before. A valid personnummer is read exactly as before, and so is every rule a digest reads. Tested on both backends (`reserve_ids.rs`).
- The post-contrast label's check for an earlier injection (C1) reads a second study of the same visit by the clock, as the rule's dated version `time-rule-2` (record 55, ruling 25 of 2026-10-15). Two studies of a subject that started less than 24 hours apart are one visit; such a study with contrast counts for a stack only when the stack's last image was acquired at or after the first image of that study's first post-contrast T1 or first bolus series, so a scan taken before it stays `pre` (flagged `C1_clock_before`), and a study of the visit that started later but was injected before the stack now counts for it. Where the stack's time or the other study's is missing, or either clock fails the trust guard, the study counts by its start time as before (`C1_no_clock`); a study with contrast 24 to 72 hours earlier counts for every stack, as before. The 72-hour flag is the stack's own. A label made before keeps `time-rule-1` as its rule; `nils contrast label` labels again under the new one.
- `GET /api/ask/documents` lists the kept cards by default; `kept=no` lists the drafts nobody kept and `kept=all` both, and a client that lists the documents and wants the assistant's drafts as well asks with `kept=all`. Each row says who kept the line and when (`kept`, null for a draft), and the answer counts the `cards` and the `drafts`, whatever the page and the filter. `GET /api/ask/documents/{id}` says `kept` for the card the document is a version of. Storing a document the request carries at `POST /api/ask/documents` as a person keeps its card, since that is what saving a card is; as an agent or a model, or by naming one already stored (`document_id`, as keeping a draft under a parent does), it keeps nothing. The retention of unused documents (`document::prune`, which nothing calls yet) spares the versions of a kept card. Contract v7 amended in place.

### Fixed

- The desk's Parts page named the release before the newest as the one to take, for up to a quarter of an hour after a release was published, and said "checked just now" beside it. The supervisor looks at the releases once in `poll_seconds` (900) and kept that look with nothing to say how old it was and nothing that could renew it: a minute after 1.0.0-alpha.85 was published, with 1.0.0-alpha.83 installed, the page read "Engine 1.0.0-alpha.84 is out". Pressing Update was never affected, since `nils update` reads the releases itself and installs none that is not newer than what is installed, so it would have taken the newest; the page named a release the update would not have taken. The look now says when it was made and a person can renew it (above). An offer is, and stays, only a release newer than the installed one by the engine's own order of versions, so a look older than what is installed offers nothing.
- The signed channel's door (`GET /api/supervise/capabilities`) offered whatever release the channel named when it differed from the installed one, so a channel that still named an older release after a newer one was installed offered the older one as `newer`, and the supervisor's poll logged it. It offers a release only when it is newer than the installed one by the engine's own order of versions, which `nils update` and a pack's range of engines use too.
- A subject code asked for at `POST /api/linkage/resolve` as a `subject-code` is answered as the subject it is. The door found a code only where a row filed it under that type (a merge files the alias's code, a coded dataset its PatientID), so the code of a subject made from an identifier of another type was answered `known: false`, and the assistant's one chat, which turns a subject named by an ID into its code first, refused to plan the erasure of a subject it was given by its code. The registry itself, which alone knows its own codes, is now asked for a code some subject holds: the answer has the same shape, a merged subject's code answers the subject it was merged into, and a code no subject holds, or none of whose scans the caller may see, is still `known: false`. It opens no identity row, so it writes no read audit row; the audit log holds the lookup and never the value. Tested on both backends (`tests/resolve_id.rs`).
- A question can be started at once from a list of IDs just uploaded. `POST /api/ask/start` with `from.values`, and a document that declares the upload, answered 400, "upload u-... is not known; uploads live only until resolved", for an upload made after the ask doors built their catalog, until some other write moved the registry's epoch. The doors keep a catalog for each handler thread at the epoch and read the uploads when they build it, and between epochs they read again only the datasets; an upload judges nothing and moves no epoch. They now read the uploads again as they read the datasets: a look at the table, its count and its highest id, tells at every request whether one was made since, whichever handler thread or command made it. The epoch is left where it is on purpose, since it is the clock of judgements: moving it for an upload would make every handle of everyone's stale and stop every staged commit. Tested on both backends (`an_upload_made_since_is_known_once_the_catalog_reads_the_uploads_again`, `a_list_uploaded_just_now_is_a_start_the_next_request_can_use`).
- A pseudonymise run that ends on a conflict names the conflict. When the registry failed a batch of the run (an identity collision, or a registry that could not be read or written), the files still to be asked about were told that the run was over, and the run could end with that sentence as its error, in the job and wherever the job is shown, in place of the failure it followed from. Which of the two it kept depended on which worker's answer was counted first; for a registry that failed while recording rows it was the sentence whenever a file was still to be asked about. The run now ends with the first failure the registry met: the collision, with the number of the review item it opened, or the registry's own words. The review item is as it was, and a run that does not fail is unchanged.

## [pack-mri-v1.3.0] - 2026-10-16

The MRI pack 1.3.0, the damaged-header flag's thresholds (record 56 section 3; record 55, ruling 31 (c)). It declares pack contract 11, so it needs an engine that implements it, the one these notes' Unreleased section describes: an earlier engine refuses it by its contract, and its update says so and waits. `nils update --pin mri@1.2.3` goes back to the version before.

### Changed

- The pack states the thresholds at which a dataset's signs flag its headers as damaged (`review.damaged_headers`): 10 % of its MR scans nameless (the engine's half before), 2 % of its named MR scans with no word the pack knows, and 10 % of its MR scans by a maker the rules were not built on (Siemens, GE, Philips) that no dataset brought in before it holds. A series' words are folded as `technique_text` folds them. The numbers were set from measurements over a production archive of 550,680 MR stacks in seven datasets and over the damage study's damaged copies (the pack's PROVENANCE).
- No rule changed, so no stack is sorted differently: the flag decides only where System 1's gate runs.

## [1.0.0-alpha.86] - 2026-10-16

Post-contrast's fallback keeps its card arms inside its image, and a release rebuilds only the model images that changed.

### Changed

- A release builds a model image (body part, post-contrast, System 1) only when its package's files, the release workflow or the descriptors script changed since the previous release's tag. An unchanged image is the one the previous release shipped, by the digest its `IMAGES` names, once the registry says anyone may pull it; the descriptors and `IMAGES` keep their form.
- A release, and a rules release, may take its commit's checks from the pull request it is the squash merge of, where the merge's tree is byte for byte the pull request head's and main's tip before the merge is in that head, so the pull request's ci ran on exactly this tree (`scripts/release-pr-head.sh`). The release then takes the six binaries from that pull request's ci run when main's run has not kept them.

### Fixed

- Post-contrast's fallback (`postcontrast-full`, nils-postcontrast 0.2.2) keeps arms U and V on a card in its image. With the Frangi filter on the card, CuPy writes every kernel it compiles to a cache under the home, and in a container whose home is missing on a read-only root (apptainer's `--no-home`, the image's unprivileged user) every stack that reached the filter lost both arms to an `OSError`: 19 of 93 stacks in the image's card test. The cache now goes in the run's work folder unless `CUPY_CACHE_DIR` names one. The CPU path and the answers elsewhere are unchanged. The descriptor pins the image built from 0.2.2.

## [1.0.0-alpha.85] - 2026-10-16

Post-contrast is sent only the scans that can be T1-type brain.

### Changed

- `nils score --step post_contrast`, and the scoring at the end of a bring-in, queue post-contrast's cascade only over the new stacks that can be T1-type brain by their stored axes (the base is T1w and the technique is not TOF or PC, and the body part says brain, the body region says head, or the body part is not answered yet), the test the cascade itself applies before it scores. A stack left out is not sent and gets no answer; the score's result says how many were left out (`left_out`, "not T1-type brain by the stored axes"), and where none qualifies nothing is queued and the step is skipped with that reason.

## [1.0.0-alpha.84] - 2026-10-15

Ruling 27: a model stages on its admission; certificates serve the paper.

### Changed

- A System 1 model that is admitted and chosen for its task stages its answers without a recorded certificate (record 55, ruling 27). Its admission, and for the rules their replay, is what stands for it in production. On a dataset flagged as damaged, the gate runs and System 1's answer is staged where the rules name nothing and it is confident at its threshold, for a person to commit, as before; the gate no longer waits for a certificate, and Review no longer shows the chosen model's opinion as not confirmed. A model neither admitted nor chosen stages nothing. Certificates stay: recording one works as before, and the model's page, the review settings and the suggestions show one where it exists (the questions door now names it too as `system1.certificate_id`). Seals, the person-only unseal and a person over an agent over a model are unchanged.

## [1.0.0-alpha.83] - 2026-10-15

A fix to 1.0.0-alpha.82 on the night it reached production.

### Fixed

- A pipeline run with a failed unit takes its proposals in (1.0.0-alpha.82). The intake refused a run whose proposals were in already by counting every review item keyed to the run, and a failed unit's `pipeline:qc` item is keyed to it too and is raised before the intake, so such a run took none of its proposals and said they were in already. Only the run's own `<axis>:model` items count now. `nils pipeline ingest <run>` takes in the proposals of a run that closed without them, from the `results.json` files it left, as its intake would have, and records them in the run's summary; taking in again takes nothing and says so.

## [pack-mri-v1.2.3] - 2026-10-15

The MRI pack 1.2.3, a rules release on its own: an install on engine 1.0.0-alpha.82 or later takes it with `nils update --part rules`, or with Update on the MRI rules row of the desk's Settings › Parts, with no engine release and nothing started again. It works with engines `>=1.0.0-alpha.82, <2.0.0` and pack contract 10, since it relies on the engine that reads a value's `stored:` spelling and the acquisition family. `nils update --pin mri@1.2.2` goes back to the version before.

### Changed

- The pack shows and names every technique by a vendor-neutral word from the MR literature, not by one maker's product name (record 55, ruling 23): `SPGRE` for FLASH, `FSPGRE` for TurboFLASH, `VIGRE` for VIBE, `combMEGRE` for MEDIC, `SSFPFID` for FISP, `SSFPecho` for PSIF, `bSSFP` for FIESTA, `pcbSSFP` for CISS, `SSTSE` for HASTE, `IRTSE` for TIRM, `DETSE` for RESTORE, `rsEPI` for RESOLVE and `MSI` for MAVRIC. Rows keep the old word (`stored:`), so no stack moves and a rule, pick or campaign that wrote it still names the value; descriptive names change (`Ax_T2w_2D_FLAIR_IRTSE_ND`), BIDS technique tokens do not. Kept: MPRAGE, MP2RAGE, MEMPRAGE, DESS, GRASE, MDME, QALAS and the words that are generic already, and SPACE by Nima's ruling of 2026-10-15 (very widespread and well known). The table and its sources are in the pack's PROVENANCE.
- The BIDS names of a family's images (SWI, SyMRI, STAGE, a mix sequence) spell the acquisition family before the technique, in both styles.
- An echo Siemens' STAGE acquired (written ORIGINAL) enters the STAGE route, which makes it RawRecon again as before and leaves its other axes alone, so its evidence names the acquisition and its names say STAGE.
- Replayed before release over a production archive of 546,440 MR stacks against 1.2.2: 0 stacks move on any axis; with the STAGE change, still no value moves and 79 acquired STAGE echoes gain an evidence tier on provenance.

## [1.0.0-alpha.82] - 2026-10-15

Wave 7a's remaining work after the beta: the rules engine, Review in bulk, plain admin access, body part and post-contrast as layers of the Main scans map, one lookup from an ID to its subject code, and the acquisition family in names. Goes with desk 1.0.0-alpha.65. The assistant pin moves to 1.0.0-alpha.29; the Kvasir pin is unchanged.

### Security

- A rule that reads subject codes (the one a dataset's declaration writes for its pseudonymised tree, and a rule file's `code: verbatim`) never reads a personnummer as one. The subject code alphabet spells a personnummer's digits, so under a declaration that PatientID holds the subject code, a file whose PatientID held a twelve-digit personnummer became a subject whose code was the number, shown wherever codes are and written into a release's `sub-` names. Such a value does not answer the rule (an `identity_unparsed` diagnostic, shape only), and in a dataset's tree the file is held as above.

### Fixed

- An overlay document posted whole as the body of a proposal (`POST /api/packs/{name}/proposals`, or a preview) is no longer refused for its `scope` (record 56, section 5.4): an overlay document carries its own scope, the overlay's origin, and it is used as such with the document's cases kept; a top-level `scope` is still refused beside operations, a patch or a nested `overlay`, where it would narrow a preview of a card that counts what adoption re-sorts.
- `nils overlay refuse` writes the refusal and the retirement of the overlay's scoped change in one transaction, under the lock the doors adopt with, and also withdraws an adopted overlay: its scoped change retires, its scope is queued for a re-sort and the handles over it stop reproducing, as when a newer version replaces it. A proposed overlay's own proposal is rejected with it.
- Withdrawing an adopted overlay with `nils overlay refuse` is audited as `overlay.withdraw` (with the handles it stops reproducing) in the same transaction, and the command re-reads the overlay inside it so a decision made meanwhile by the server writes nothing. Rejecting the proposal an overlay is refuses the overlay too, and adopting an overlay writes the `handle.invalidate` row again.
- An overlay is adopted through the proposal it is (Nima, 2026-10-15), by the rule change's own decide path and no other: `POST /api/overlays` keeps the scoped change as a proposal rehearsed over the overlay's origin with the scoped changes in force, and `POST /api/overlays/{id}/adopt` is refused as stale, like any rule change, once another was adopted or retired since, until the proposal is rehearsed again. The newer version of an overlay still retires the older, and its origin is re-sorted.
- A decision about one scan is refused when a person decided its group, series, subject or scanner and the decision comes from an agent or a model (C15 across scopes): before, it was written and never read. The refusal names who decided and says to withdraw that decision first if it was wrong.
- A dataset's pseudonymised tree no longer files a file whose PatientID holds a value its declaration cannot read under the file's study (spec §5.4 and §18: an unknown subject's files are held). A de-identified dataset declared to hold subject codes (`--patient-id subject-code`) whose files held another identifier had every file filed under its StudyInstanceUID, the fallback of a rule file, so a person with four studies became four subjects with four codes; on a test archive of 65 studies all 45,179 files fell back and no question was raised. A tree read by what its dataset declares PatientID holds now holds such a file as `identity.unmapped`, asked about once per dataset and shape of what PatientID holds, never its value, whatever the dataset says of an identifier no map names; the next read files it once the declaration is corrected, and a person can still have it coded anyway. A file whose PatientID is empty falls back to its study as before, and a rule file keeps the fallback. `--describe` says what the rule holds, and the batch records `unread: held`.
- The install door no longer answers 404 `no setup is recorded` while an update rewrites the setup record (1.0.0-alpha.80). Every update writes the record again, and the copy the supervisor reads beside it, in place, so a read between the truncation and the write found an empty record, and a desk polling its Parts page during an update was told there was no install. The record and its copy are now written to a file of that write's own beside them, given the mode (and, where the writer may, the owner) of the file they replace, and renamed over it.
- A run the supervisor starts (a restart, a reapply, an update, and the Update of a part's row on the desk's Parts page, the rules' among them) is read back by its id from the moment its 202 is answered (1.0.0-alpha.80). The run's recorder rewrites its document as the run starts and as it ends, and wrote it in place, so a read between the truncation and the write found an empty file and `GET /api/supervise/runs/{id}` answered 404 `no run by that id`, which a desk polling the run showed as an error and which failed a supervisor test once in CI. Every write of a run's document now goes to a file of that write's own beside it (named by its process and a count, so two threads writing one run never share one), renamed over it, so a read finds the document before or the one after; and a run that cannot be put on record is refused with 500 rather than answered with an id the door cannot read back.
- A visit's kinds count an SWI's own image with no weighting, its phase, as SWI, by the acquisition family its route recognised, as the SWI's group holds it, where it counted as Other; its magnitude is still told by its T2*w. Contract v7 amended in place.
- The acquisition family is one reading everywhere (a release's names and sidecars, the scans doors, a visit's kinds, an effect report), as the classification stands: a route whose answer a decision in force contradicts on an axis the route decided, compared as identities (on a multi-valued axis, a decision that drops a value the route wrote, never one that adds others), no longer gives the scan its family, and the provenance in force is read; the family is the route the stack's evidence names first, not the first of a fixed list. The decisions are read once for the stacks asked about, on every axis. A visit's kinds read the same family: a scan with no weighting counts under its family whichever it is (SWI, SyMRI, and the new kinds STAGE and Mix), one with any base never does, where any SWI-family scan that was not T1w, T2w, PDw or T2*w counted as SWI; a decision in force stands over the rules' answer in the kinds; `symri` counts from the same family. Contract v7 amended in place.
- The acquisition family on a call over every stack also covers a stack known only by its provenance (a display composite such as SyMRI or EPIMix), so its release names, sidecars and kinds keep the family; and of two origin decisions of one rank on a scanner and axis the newest stands on either backend (the decisions are read in id order and compared by rank, then id).
- A pipeline whose card is optional runs on the CPU where the lane's card is smaller than a unit's `gpu-memory-gb`, rather than wait for a lease that never comes, and a pipeline that requires a card is refused there with the card's size and the need; the pre-flight says why in `gpu.why`.
- An apptainer container's `/tmp` and `/var/tmp` are a folder on disk beside the unit's log (`--workdir`), removed when the unit ends, where they were a 64 MB tmpfs in a contained run; a tool's temporary files, such as the post-contrast fallback's converted and segmented volumes, no longer fill it. Podman's and docker's were on disk already.
- A unit whose card is optional no longer waits for a card that is large enough but full (one another tenant holds): after 60 s without a lease (`NILS_CARD_GRACE_S`) it runs on the CPU, its log's first line says why, and the pre-flight says so in `gpu.why` while the card is that full.
- A unit that asks the lane's cores (`cores: lane`) takes the cores free at its start, at least one and at most its threads parameter's maximum, rather than wait for the whole lane, and its threads parameter and `NILS_CORES` are set at each start from what it took, a resumed run's included (it kept the threads it first recorded).
- An apptainer unit's work folder is removed when its run stops, is cancelled or is taken up again, and when the pipeline lane starts for every run of its host that ended since its last start (one query, the time kept in the registry per host; the interrupted runs, whose end has no time, are visited once, on the first sweep, and cleared when they are marked); it holds clinical-derived volumes. Only apptainer units get one.
- A pipeline that declares a slice cache is given the files' identities and the cache's mount only on a run that asks for the cache (`[Cache]`, or a parameter given `/cache`); body part's pass gets neither.
- `nils pipeline add --release` reports a descriptors file that does not match the release's checksums as an integrity failure and adds nothing, with no advice to add a descriptor from elsewhere.
- A run whose optional card another tenant keeps too full goes to the CPU as a whole, before its first unit starts, and records the CPU as its device; once a unit runs on the card the run waits up to 30 minutes (`NILS_CARD_STALL_S`) for room another tenant holds and then fails, saying why; waits for this run's own leases never end it, and leases whose memory the card already shows in use are not counted twice. A resumed run goes on where it was: on the CPU, or on the card. The audit's `device` and the summary's `needs` say the CPU when the run went there.
- Threads the caller sets win over the lane's cores and are what the run records; where none is set the run records none and each unit's row keeps the cores it took. A unit that runs alone and asks the lane's cores waits up to 120 s (`NILS_LANE_GRACE_S`) for half of what it may take before it takes what is free.
- Schema 84 no longer removes a dataset that was read when its folder, or its `derivatives/dcm-anon` or `derivatives/dcm-original`, is a symbolic link. The step that makes a root's unread folders plain folders again called a dataset read only when a root a digest kept lay under the dataset's path as stored, and a digest keeps the resolved root, which lies behind the link. It now compares the roots as given and as resolved with the folder and each of its trees, both as stored and resolved, as a place's other path checks do, and keeps a dataset whose folder or tree is a link it cannot resolve now (storage not mounted), since that cannot be shown unread. Only a store a development build left between schemas 80 and 83 could meet it.
- `POST /api/picks/run`, `POST /api/picks` and the three `POST /api/picks/map` doors answer 404 `no scheme called NAME` for a scheme the registry does not keep, where they answered 500 as if the engine had failed. Contract v7 amended in place: `POST /api/picks` lists the 404, and the map doors' 404 names the stored scheme.
- Two quick clicks on a step's run (`POST /api/datasets/{name}/steps/{step}/run` and the cohort's) queue one run, where both passed the check for a run queued or running and both queued one over the same scans. The check and the insert are now one step: the door holds a lock for the scope and the step from its check to its insert, and where another process queued the same step meanwhile, the younger job is withdrawn while it is still queued; the request that loses is refused 409 `running`, naming the job that runs.
- Matching the registry's sources to its datasets resolves each dataset's folder and trees once per call, where it resolved them again for every source row, so a catalog build, a picks map and a release's reading of the picks no longer make source rows times datasets path resolutions on network storage. The answer is the same.
- A release builds the sidecar's `NILS` object of the stacks it writes alone, with the tiers of those stacks read in chunks, where it built one for every stack of the registry and read the whole axis table for them, and the pipeline pre-flight did the same and dropped them. Names are still settled over the whole registry. The cards a release writes are unchanged.
- A release's BIDS name rounds end with a check that every planned name is one stack's: where the rounds run out with a name still shared, each stack of the group takes the plain number in the group's order, and a name shared even then refuses the release before anything is written, naming the stacks, never writing two stacks to one name. No input is known to reach it.
- A release reaches a stack that lives in the later frames of an enhanced file. The digest files such a file as one instance under its first stack, with a frame row for each stack it holds, and every selection of a release joined the instance's stack alone, so the file's other stacks were never planned, not even when named with `--stack`, and the report did not count them. A release now reads a stack's files through its frame rows as well, and writes a file the digest split between stacks as each stack's own: its frames alone (their per-frame groups and their pixels, native or encapsulated, and the frame count), under a SOP Instance UID of its own, the file's remapped with the stack where the release remaps UIDs and under the `2.25` arc otherwise, the same for ever. The reader of frame lists and of a file's fragments by frame is shared with the pyramid.
- `GET /api/cohorts` reads every cohort in a fixed number of queries, where it ran two grouped joins and a count of every cohort's stacks per cohort, retired ones too, and read every release once per cohort: the counts, the members' intervals, the batches behind them, the datasets holding their scans and the releases naming each are read once for all cohorts. `GET /api/cohorts/{name}` reads the same for its one cohort. The answer is unchanged.
- `nils preview build --all` no longer deletes the previews and part files made while it ran. It read the registry's stacks once, built for as long as that took, and then removed every preview of a stack not in that first list and every part file, so the previews of stacks registered meanwhile went, and the part files a sort or a door was still filling, whose rename then failed. Its prune now keeps the stacks the registry holds when it prunes and every preview written since the build began, and removes a part file only when it is older than the build (and, since the later fix below, by a grace period).
- A picture whose build a stop of the engine or a worker cut short is built again when it is next asked for, where it answered 422 "could not be built" for good. The next claim fails such a job for a process gone or a heartbeat gone stale, and the picture doors read that as a build that failed; a job failed in those words, with no reason class of a build's own, is now queued again, once per stack in a process so that a crash loop cannot rebuild forever. A build that failed on the stack's own account (unreadable, compressed, ...) still answers 422 and queues nothing.
- A picture a reader asks for while a background pyramid build runs honours `--force` and its working place. Built between two stacks of the background build, a `pyramid build --stack N --force` of a stack that had its pyramid ended done without building anything, and a build was taken by comparing its place with the default working place while it was written under the background build's own, so with two working places a picture could be written into the other's folder. Such a job is now taken only where its place is the background build's (or it names none and that place is the default), and `--force` builds the stack again.
- `GET /api/places` answers each dataset's `layout` from a kept copy, read again only where its folder or one of its trees changed, its declaration changed, a place or a dataset was written at a door, `?probe=1` was asked, or the copy is five minutes old (thirty seconds when partial), where it listed every dataset's folder and looked into each loose entry for DICOM on every call, which on network storage could take seconds. The looks into loose entries share one budget per request, and a layout whose looks ran out of it says `partial`, counting the entries it did not look into as ones that may hold DICOM. Contract v7 amended in place, additively.
- The contract describes every field `GET /api/places` answers: the three shapes of a source place's `layout` (a root's, a legacy tree's and a dataset's, each field with its values), a root's `datasets` and `probed.folders`, `not_read`, and the dataset fields `patient_id`, `subjects`, `copy_folder`, `kind`, `state`, `root` and `picks`, which the door has answered since Wave 7a and the desk's Data page reads. A test checks that every key the door answers is named there. Contract v7 amended in place, additively.
- `POST /api/packs/{name}/rehearse` over a scope of more than 2,000 stacks is a job, where it ran the effect report inside the request with no bound: answered 202 with the job, queued under the caller's principal, grants and detail as `nils pack rehearse --patch-json`, which beats between its chunks, can be cancelled there, runs one at a time, and keeps the report as its result, scanners shaped below detail quasi. A scope of more than 200,000 stacks is refused (422) with the cap in the words. A small scope is answered at once, as before. `nils pack rehearse` takes `--patch-json` and `--cap`. Contract v7 amended in place, additively.
- A page of scans can carry where each picture is rather than its bytes: with `pictures=1&links=1` each scan's `picture` is `{url, width, height, digest, held, partial}`, `url` the preview door's middle plane named by its digest and held state (immutable for a browser to keep), or for a scan with no preview yet the door's first picture, decoded when it is asked for. A visit's page of fifty is answered without some 800 KB of inline pictures, and reads and audits no pixels: the preview door opens each picture when it is fetched. Without `links` the pictures come inline, as before. Contract v7 amended in place, additively.
- The engine loads every pack, its corpus run, as it starts and before its doors open, where the first request after a start paid for it (1.0 to 1.5 s, on the desk's first capabilities call or the first page of scans). A load of a pack that another thread is loading waits for it and takes its pack rather than building it twice.
- `GET /api/sources` answers a kept document while what it was read from is the same (the registry's epoch, the places, the digests' and the pseudonymised files' counts), for a minute or a few seconds while a digest runs, and any write at a door forgets it; the engine reads it once as it starts. It read every dataset's counts on each call, 400 to 600 ms on a registry of a few hundred scans, which the Data page paid on every visit.
- A file the digest split between stacks is converted and exported as each stack's own frames in the BIDS layout too, where the converter was handed the whole enhanced file and the DICOM export beside it repeated it. The content of a stack now includes the frames it is written from, and the scrub version is 2, so a stack released whole before is written again as its own frames.
- Writing a stack's frames out of an enhanced file rewrites the Extended Offset Table and its lengths for the frames kept (one fragment each) and drops them where a frame is more than one fragment or the data is native. Native 8-bit data with its trailing pad byte and 1-bit packed data keep their frames, which read as a failure before.
- A move whose files are gone, written again at apply time, gets its sidecar's `NILS` card like any stack the release writes.
- A preview and a selection's size count a file split between stacks once, whichever of its stacks the selection takes.
- The rehearse door reads its scope once and replays from it, where it read it again after counting it.
- The sources document and the kept layouts are forgotten by the doors that write a place or a dataset, after they answered with success, and not by a read-only POST or a refused write.
- `nils preview build --all` removes a part file only when it is older than the build by a quarter of an hour, never by a process this machine does not run, so a writer on shared storage keeps its file.
- A run withdrawn as the younger of two for one step and scope deletes the scans it froze, and the lock a door keeps for a scope and step is dropped when nobody holds or waits for it.
- Splitting an enhanced file's frames no longer mis-splits 8-bit or 1-bit pixel data stored as OW. Such data arrived as words and the object's geometry was ignored, so frames of an odd size (two frames of three bytes) were cut by the pad rule at one word each. It is now split by bytes (or bits) from rows, columns and samples, as OB data is, and refused with a clear error when the geometry is missing.

### Added

- `POST /api/linkage/resolve` (record 55, ruling 24): one ID of a known type, as a person names a subject by it (a study's own ID), turned into the subject code it stands for with one lookup: `{id_type, known: true, subject_code, scans, datasets}`, or `{id_type, known: false}` when no subject of a scan the caller may see holds it. It is open to whoever may use the data (`data:see` or `query:see`) at detail quasi, since a subject code is quasi-identifying. The value is looked up by its keyed hash as the digest and a map filed it, so no stored identifier is opened, and the answer holds no identifier it was not given, not even the value back. A merged subject answers the subject it was merged into, a scan of a sample sealed now is not counted without `sealed:see`, and each match writes one read audit row under the caller. Tested on both backends with a ctrl-id map (`tests/resolve_id.rs`).
- A certificate may cite its graded run instead of a sample sealed in the same registry (record 55, ruling 22 b), so a model certified on research evidence is recorded on a site whose registry holds no seal. `POST /api/certificates` takes `evidence` (the run, the sha256 of its `MANIFEST.json`, the commit that registered the plan, the artifact digest of the one model the run graded and, where known, where the plan is written and the run that showed the registered artifact answers as the graded one did) beside the graded result (`bar`, `n` and `errors`, with `bound`, `confidence`, `coverage`, `test` and `claim` where known). The engine works the exact one-sided Clopper-Pearson bound out again from the counts, refuses a stated bound that differs from it, a result above its bar and a run that already certifies the model. It is one person's act under a verified identity and models:work: it unseals nothing, so no second person is needed, and it is audited as `labels.certificate` with the citation and the result. Such a certificate confirms System 1 as one on a sealed sample does and names its task's default model; the unseal door refuses it (409). `nils model certify <model> --server URL --evidence MANIFEST.json ...` posts it under the person's token (`nils login`, `--token` or `NILS_TOKEN`). The model doors, `GET /api/review/settings` and `nils model show` carry the certificate that names a model, with its citation, and `nils labels certificates` lists it. Schema 99 adds the certificate's `evidence` and `model_id`, with a unique key on the sample and the model where it names one, so a run certifies a model once. Contract v7 and the model contract's lifecycle are amended in place.
- System 1 never fills an answer silently (record 55, ruling 20). On a dataset flagged as having damaged headers, once a certificate confirms System 1, where the rules name nothing on a single-valued axis its card gates (no value, or technique's `Unknown`) and no person decided it, and System 1 names a value of the served pack's vocabulary at its λ1 or above, that value, as the axis stores it (System 1's `T2starw` is base `T2*w`), is staged on the gate's `<axis>:disagree` question as a decision of System 1's model, for a person to confirm with one commit or to answer otherwise. Nothing is in force until a person commits it. An answer System 1 no longer gives is withdrawn by its model with an audit row and its question opens again, a gate run again keeps what it staged, an answer of an earlier System 1 model is replaced or withdrawn as System 1's own, an answer a person or another kind of author staged is never withdrawn or replaced (the gate leaves that stack and axis, raises nothing there and counts it as `held`), a decision in force at the stack's series, subject, origin or group counts as decided, and a dataset without the flag stages nothing. Staging reads the round's own rules version from the served pack folder (the run's `--pack-dir` after a System 1 run); where the pack is not served or its version differs, no words are read at all: it stages nothing new, keeps the answers System 1 still gives from the round's model (counted as kept, with the reason in `waiting`), and withdraws, as the model with an audit row, those System 1 no longer gives or an earlier model gave. A held question (a person's answer staged through any item on the stack and axis) is no outcome and leaves no outcome row, and a gate item marked staged whose answer is gone opens again. The gate's answer counts what it staged, kept and withdrew, and why a confident answer was not staged.
- Body part and post-contrast as layers on the Main scans map, for a dataset and for a cohort (record 55, decision 6 of 2026-10-10: "The data map (v0's heatmap, for datasets and cohorts, with layers such as body part and post-contrast) is where the trade-off is seen"; left out of the main-scans study's first round and ruled in on 2026-10-13). `POST /api/picks/map`, `/map/subjects` and `/map/strips` take `layer`: `body_region` or `body_part` (the body-part step's coarse and fine axes) or `post_contrast` (the post-contrast label's state, pre, post, by definition or undetermined, and for a stack no label has reached what the `post_contrast` axis says). Each visit gives its MR scans' counts by value, the value of the scan it takes and, for post-contrast, the scans the 72-hour flag is up on; each candidate its value; the strips by visit the value taken and the value most of its scans carry; the map each value's visits that hold it, visits that take it (under a draft, beside the saved rules) and scans, what carries none, and the step that answers the layer over the scope as the dataset's or cohort's own page carries it, with why a run of it would be refused now. A layer is read from the stored answers and writes nothing, and a stack of a sample sealed now is in no count for a caller who may not read it. The contract v7 amends the three doors in place, additively. The cohort's heatmap door, `GET /api/cohorts/{name}/picks`, takes the same `layer`: each session of the page gives its MR scans by value, each role the value of the scan picked, and the answer the layer's values on the page and its step over the cohort (on the first page alone). A dataset's map (its visits, candidates, scans taken and layer), its layer step and its own pick run read one set of stacks, those its Data page counts (the `source` rows under its folder, resolved once a request or a run, nested folders included, and a failure to read them is reported for that dataset while the run goes on to the next scope), so its saved picks agree with its map, and a run that picked nothing says why from the dataset's own stacks; a page of subjects reads its layer by those subjects, or by the dataset's stacks where they are fewer; `GET /api/picks/layer-step` gives a layer's step alone (a dataset's with data:see or pipelines:see, a cohort's with review:see too), for a page to ask while it runs; an axis with several rows for a stack is chosen the same way on either backend, in Rust and in byte order; the map and the heatmap give a scan of several stacks the value of its lowest stack that carries one; the step says how many model answers are staged and not yet put in force (`staged`); and the subjects door reads the layer for its page alone.
- A release builds every model's image from its tag and ships the descriptors of its model pipelines pinned to them (record 55 A5). `nils-bodypart`, `nils-postcontrast` and `nils-system1` are built and pushed to `ghcr.io/kineuro` by the tag, and the release's `pipelines.tar.gz` (listed in `IMAGES`, checked by `SHA256SUMS`) holds every descriptor of the tree that names one of them, each pinned to the digest the release pushed and taken into a catalog by the tag's own binary before the release goes out. The release waits for both, so a tag's descriptors never name an older image than its own, as 1.0.0-alpha.80's did (its descriptors named 1.0.0-alpha.71's image until a commit after the tag). The descriptors in the tree are development pins. An image job that fails never holds back the binaries and the packs: the release ships the descriptors of the images that pushed and its notes name those it did not build. Post-contrast's fallback image (`Dockerfile.full`) is not built by the release, whose runners lack the disk: `scripts/publish-postcontrast-full.sh` builds it, pushes `ghcr.io/kineuro/nils-postcontrast-full` tagged with the package's version and pins the digest in `postcontrast-full`, which the release's `pipelines.tar.gz` ships with that pin as it is, where anyone may pull it (the registry asked without credentials, tried again on a network error and failing the step rather than guessing; only a clear 401, 403 or 404 says it is not public; else the notes name it). The script refuses, before it builds, a version whose tag is already published, takes the digest from the push and says whether the package is public.
- `nils pipeline add --release <name>` takes a model pipeline's descriptor from the release this engine is, `--release=<version>` from another and `--channel <url>` (or `NILS_RELEASES`) from another place releases come from, checked against the release's sums; a name the release does not ship is refused with what it ships.
- A unit may ask for every core of the lane, `x-nils.needs.cores: lane` (record 55 T24; job contract amended in place): the run is given as many cores as the lane's budget holds, never more than its `cores-input` parameter's maximum, the container is told them as `NILS_CORES`, the parameter is set to them, and the pre-flight shows the number. A descriptor that names a number is as before.
- The Rules page's doors (record 56, sections 6.4 and 6.5, OpenAPI version 7 added in place). `GET /api/packs/{name}/quality` is the pack's quality report for whoever tunes it: the equal-rank disagreements, the overrides by rule pair (who beat whom where the pack's ranking decided, from each stack's classification, with how many stacks, some of them and the datasets they are in), and System 1's confident disagreements with the rules, empty until a System 1 model serves; `dataset` narrows it to one dataset. A proposed rule change is a registry object (`rule_proposal`, schema 100): `POST /api/packs/{name}/proposals` takes typed operations with their reason, evidence and scope, rehearses them and keeps the effect report with an approval card that names every answer they break; `GET` lists and reads them; `POST .../{id}/rehearse` makes the report again against the pack and the registry as they are; `POST .../{id}/decide` adopts or rejects one, a person holding review:work and data:work. A pack edit is adopted as the pack's next version, written into the deployment's pack directory with the version it replaces kept beside it (`<packs>.versions/<pack>/<version>`), recorded as a rules version to release, and the registry is re-sorted by it; a scoped change (a site, a dataset, a scanner, a batch) is kept, and its scope re-sorted. The re-sort writes on the proposal what it did: the stacks it judged and moved, by axis, and the stored answers per axis before and after. The audit records `rules.propose`, `rules.adopt` and `rules.reject`, and an adoption stops the handles over what it re-sorts from reproducing.
- A scoped re-sort: `nils classify --scope SCOPE` (repeated for a union: `site:NAME`, `dataset:NAME[,NAME]`, `scanner:KEY=VALUE[,KEY=VALUE]`, `batch:ID` or `handle:ID`), at the keyboard and at the jobs door, judges the stacks its scope holds and writes nothing about any other: their rows, questions, passes' answers and disposition, and the post-contrast label of their subjects. Its result says its scope.
- Every sort applies the pack's adopted scoped changes, each operation to the stacks its scope holds and to no other (record 56, section 5.6: an overlay never leaves its scope), a bring-in's sort included; a stack so sorted names them on its classification (`overlay`, `proposal:<id>`), and the sort's result lists them (`scoped`) and any that no longer applies to the pack (`scoped_skipped`).
- `GET /api/datasets/{name}/summary` counts the proposed rule changes that touch the dataset (`proposals`), record 56's "3 proposed rule fixes from this read".
- Review round 1's questions (record 56, section 6.3). `GET /api/review/questions` gathers the sort's own questions that wait for a person about a dataset's, a cohort's or the registry's scans into plain questions, each with its distinct scans: an axis the rules found no value for (`base:missing`, "Which base?", "The rules found no base for these scans."), two values in dispute (`base:FLAIR|T2w`, from the gate's disagreement, a person's decision the rules differ from, or an older engine's conflict), or one answer in doubt (`base:T2w?`), and the two answers its keys 1 and 2 give. A question about several axes at once and an item an open campaign holds are counted, never asked there. `GET /api/review/questions/{key}/scans` pages a question's scans, 50 at a time, each with its picture inline, NILS's answer, the rules' reason with the rules it was decided over (`overridden`), System 1's answer and any answer staged for a person; a blind-first question shows none of NILS's answers. `POST /api/review/questions/{key}/answer` answers the scans a person saw with one value and its exceptions (`except`, each its own value, no value, or left waiting): one decision a scan through the review spine, all in one transaction, closing each scan's question, each marked with its mode, its seconds and whether it was given blind.
- An answer re-sorts its scans at once: a `classify` job over those stacks alone (a scoped re-sort) writes their rules' answers with the decisions in force, the passes' answers for them, the questions a sort asks of them, their disposition and their subjects' post-contrast label, and nothing of another stack; it leaves the batch diagnostics as the last whole sort wrote them. A grouped question part of whose scans a re-sort judges again is split: the group is superseded and its members still waiting are asked again as a group of their own (`split_from`), so no question about another scan is lost. The gate runs again over those of a dataset flagged as having damaged headers, and the pick run a sort is followed by is queued where the person may run one.
- `POST /api/linkage/resolve` (record 55, ruling 24): one ID of a known type, as a person names a subject by it (a study's own ID), turned into the subject code it stands for with one lookup: `{id_type, known: true, subject_code, scans, datasets}`, or `{id_type, known: false}` when no subject of a scan the caller may see holds it. A study's own type is open to whoever may use the data (`data:see` or `query:see`) at detail quasi, since a subject code is quasi-identifying; a type flagged identifying is a re-identification and needs `data:work` at detail sensitive. Datasets are named only to a caller holding `data:see` or `places:see`. The value is looked up by its keyed hash as the digest and a map filed it, so no stored identifier is opened, and the answer holds no identifier it was not given, not even the value back. A merged subject answers the subject it was merged into, and a scan of a sample sealed now is not counted without `sealed:see`. Every lookup, found or not, writes a `linkage.resolve` row in the registry's audit log, and a match a read audit row for the identity row it opened. Its policy row is a read (`writes: false`) marked `audited`, with `identifying` naming what such a type needs. Tested on both backends with a ctrl-id map (`tests/resolve_id.rs`).
- An ID type says whether its IDs identify a person wherever they are met (`identifying`, registry schema 102): personnummer, patient-id and study-instance-uid are built in so, `POST /api/linkage/types` and `nils linkage id-type add --identifying` set it (a hospital's record number), and the types door answers it. Changing it on a type that exists, either way, needs `data:work` at detail sensitive; taking it away is asked for with `unflag: true`; a built-in identifying type keeps it; every change writes a `linkage.type.flag` audit row.


### Changed

- One image runs each model on the CPU and on a card (record 55 A5). `nils-bodypart` and `nils-postcontrast` are built by default from the CUDA wheels of the torch each was graded with (2.13.0 for CUDA 12.9, 2.8.0 for CUDA 12.8), which run on the CPU where the runner gives the container no card; a CPU-only image of the same torch is a build argument. Triton, which torch's CUDA wheels pull, is removed in the layer that installs it: it bundles NVIDIA's `ptxas`, `cuobjdump` and `nvdisasm`, which no NVIDIA licence names as redistributable, and nothing in the images compiles a kernel. `bodypart-infer-fusion` declares the card optional with 4 GB of it and runs `--device auto`, so the cascade's student and the lossless JPEG 2000 decoding take the card the lane leases: before, it ran on the CPU on any machine, since its entry point's own default is the CPU and the descriptor asked for no card. `bodypart-embed` and `bodypart-seed` budget 6 and 3 GB of a card, `postcontrast-cascade` keeps 4 and `postcontrast-full` 48, and `bodypart-infer`, `bodypart-train` and `system1-score` say why they stay on the CPU: each is arithmetic per stack.
- The body-part image's build checks the encoder weights it bakes against the build argument `ENCODER_DIGESTS` where it is given; the release gives `bodypart-embed`'s pins.
- Body part's pipeline runs in two modes (record 55 T24 and E5). A pass over stacks the registry holds, such as the step door's run over a dataset's scans, takes the graded fast path: the cascade's stages overlapped in one worker pool (`overlap on`), as many workers as the lane has cores (`cores: lane`; a lane of 48 cores runs 48, at most 64), and no slice cache, which the overlapped pool cannot read. A dataset's bring-in (`x-nils.on-arrival`) runs with the cache: `cache=/cache`, `overlap off`, `cache_fill touched`, so the cache is filled at arrival, for new data only; a re-score that should read the cache asks `cache=/cache`. The descriptor's memory is 16 GB (measured with this image at 48 workers: 8.3 GiB on the CPU and 9.9 GiB with a card for a pass, 8.7 and 9.4 at arrival). Both modes give the same answers (the overlap is class I against the sequential path).
- Post-contrast's fallback, `postcontrast-full` (nils-postcontrast 0.2.0), says "not sure" for a stack left with no image arm, for any reason: no usable volume (no conversion, fewer than 3 slices, 4D), no segmentation after the card, its retry and the CPU, or no arm its features could feed (a failed feature step, too few voxels), with the reason `no-usable-volume`, `no-segmentation` or `no-image-arm`, and `worker-died` or `step-failed` where the run itself failed on the stack rather than the data (nils-postcontrast 0.2.1: a worker that died, a tool the kernel or a signal ended, dcm2niix's included, or a CPU tool step that raised), where model r1 answered it from its header evidence alone, which no test compared with a reference (record 55 E3). The change is class III by construction: on the development draw's full run 11 of 2,020 stacks' answers become "not sure" and nothing else changes, and on the timed run's 377 deferred stacks 18 do. Test A's graded numbers and the cascade's are unchanged: those stacks lie outside test A's 2,003, and the cascade was graded with model r1's own answers in the fallback's place.
- Post-contrast's fallback holds no more of a card than the engine leases it (record 55 T25). A unit on a card is told the card memory it is leased as `NILS_GPU_MEMORY_GB` (job contract text amended in place), and `postcontrast-full` declares one full wave, 60 GB (two SynthSeg processes of 20,000 MiB beside 20,000 for SynthMorph and SynthStrip, as T25 measured), where it declared 48 GB and took three quarters of the card. Within its budget a wave runs as many SynthSeg processes as fit, then SynthSeg runs alone after the wave, then on the CPU; the Frangi filter goes on the card only where every worker's share (2,200 MiB) fits beside a full wave, and otherwise runs on the CPU as model r1 ran it (`--frangi card` keeps the research runs' placement). The run's `results.json` shows the plan (`card_plan`).
- Post-contrast's cascade (`postcontrast-cascade`) reads with every core of the lane, at most 32 workers, the configuration its speed was graded with (record 55 T23), where it read with 8 (`cores: lane`, memory 12 GB).
- Post-contrast's fallback image (`Dockerfile.full`, nils-postcontrast 0.2.0) holds of FreeSurfer 8.2.0 only what the Synth tools run on, copied unchanged from FreeSurfer's pinned image: its Python, `mri_synthseg`, `mri_synthstrip` and `mri_synthmorph` with their networks, the MNI ICBM 2009c template and the colour table, on Rocky Linux 8.10, 34 GB where it was 69. It carries no MATLAB Runtime, FSL or CorTechs tools, no licence key, and neither NVIDIA's `ptxas` nor Triton; `NOTICE` and the licences under `/usr/local/share/licenses/nils-postcontrast-full` say whose terms each part is under (FreeSurfer's agreement with its Part B sentence, CC BY 4.0 for SynthStrip's and SynthMorph's networks, the template's notice, dcm2niix, Apache 2.0). `mri_synthseg`'s one-line NumPy 2 change says so at its top. The driver's kernel compiles for newer cards are cached in the container's `/tmp`. On the CPU it gives the reference's features and answers exactly; its card grade is pending.
- The body-part descriptors pin the `nils-bodypart` 0.3.0 image that 1.0.0-alpha.65 published, so `bodypart-infer-fusion` 0.3.0 runs the code its descriptor describes. Its baked encoder weights verify with the same two digests as before, so those pins stay (kineuro/nils#323).
- The body-part descriptors pin the `nils-bodypart` image that 1.0.0-alpha.62 published, a new build of the same 0.2.0 sources. Its baked encoder weights verify with the same two digests as before, so those pins stay (kineuro/nils#308).
- The rehearse door reads the registry as it is sorted: the adopted scoped changes are applied on both sides, each to its scope, and the report lists them (`adopted`). A dataset holds the stacks its tree holds a file of, as the dataset summary counts them, so a stack with files in two datasets counts in both, in a scope and in a transition's `datasets`.
- A sort deletes and writes the rows of the stacks it judges, by their ids, where it cleared every row in the range of each window: a stack of another modality, or one outside a scoped re-sort, keeps its rows.
- An adopted word overlay is kept as the scoped change it is (record 56, section 5.6): adopting one at `POST /api/overlays/{id}/adopt` makes an adopted proposal of its word edits scoped to its origin, which every sort applies to the stacks of that origin and to no other, and re-sorts that scope (`proposal` in the answer). Before, an adoption sorted the whole registry under the overlay once and the next sort dropped it. Migrating to schema 100 keeps every overlay adopted before the same way. The signals door's text samples read each stack the same way, with the adopted scoped changes whose scope holds it (`scoped`), where they read the last adopted overlay over every stack.
- A proposed rule change is adopted only as it was rehearsed (record 56, sections 5.4 and 6.5). Its report always covers the whole scope its adoption re-sorts: the proposals door refuses a `scope` (400), and `POST .../{id}/rehearse` with one answers a preview it never keeps. A rehearsal marks what it began on, the pack version served and the scoped changes in force (`report.rehearsed_on`), before it reads the registry, and a proposal is stale unless both are still so, where a change adopted within the same second as a rehearsal went unseen. Every check that can refuse an adoption (the proposal is current, its cases hold, a pack edit's next version is written and loads, its scope names what the registry holds) runs before anything changes; then the adoption, its re-sort's job, the audit and the handles it invalidates are written in one transaction, and a pack edit's version is put in place last, taken back where it cannot be. A refused adoption leaves the proposal proposed, where before it could be left adopted with no re-sort, or a new pack version in place unrecorded.
- An adopted overlay replaces its earlier versions (record 56, section 5.6). Adopting a version of an overlay retires the scoped change of every earlier version of the same overlay adopted before (a fourth status, `retired`, which no sort applies), and re-sorts their origins with its own; before, both stayed in force, their words collided (`already holds`), the stacks of both fell back to the bare pack, and a word the newer version dropped stayed. Migrating to schema 100 does the same in the order the overlays were adopted, each keeping its own adoption time. The overlays door takes for its own only the scoped change it made (marked `made_by: overlay.adopt`), never a person's proposal citing the overlay; the overlay's scoped change, its job, its decision and its audit are written in one transaction; and `nils overlay refuse` retires a scoped change made of the overlay. A proposal keeps an overlay document's own cases, given whole or under `overlay`, so a rehearsal again judges them.
- A sort with adopted scoped changes runs the rules of every group of stacks first and then each group's passes, the vote reading one pool as every group's rules left it, as the rehearsal counts it; before, a group's vote read the stacks of a group sorted after it as they stood before the sort, so the result hung on the order of the groups and differed from the rehearsal. A scoped re-sort reads its stacks' sessions whole for the post-contrast label and writes the label of its own stacks alone, where it relabelled every stack of their subjects.
- A person's decision is final (record 56, section 5.2). A decision made with the rules' answer in view (in Review's grid, or at the apply door where the question's suggestions showed) keeps that answer, and a sort asks about the decision again (`<axis>:decision`) only when a newer rules version moves the rules' answer from it. Before, every sort asked again about every decision that differed from the rules. Registry schema 101 adds `decision.rules_in_view`.
- Review round 1's questions keep blind answers blind (spec 11.4): a scan's tile is decided once from every question waiting on it, and every decision its answer writes carries that one mark, counted once a scan. An answer is blind only where the tile hid NILS's answers and every question on the scan was read through a door that hid them, or where it is the provisional answer to each. A pair or a value in doubt names the rules' answer in its words, so it is never read blind. A blind-first question's keys are the axis's first values, never a staged, System 1's or the rules' answer. Read for one scan (`stack`, the dataset viewer, whose names say NILS's answer), the questions list gives the scan's `mode` and notes its questions shown, so an answer there is never a blind label.
- The rules' answer kept with a decision (`rules_in_view`) is the one the question showed: the stored classification's value, as the last sort wrote it with its overlay and adopted scoped changes, where it was the served pack's own answer before. A sort compares a multi-valued axis's values in any order.
- An answer's re-sort uses the pack, the pack version and the overlay its scans were last sorted with; scans sorted by another version, or under an overlay the registry does not keep, are left to the next sort and `resort.state` is `refused`, with why. On Postgres the count of the questions a re-sort asked is a whole number again, and a count that fails is said in `resort.why`.

## [pack-mri-v1.2.2] - 2026-10-15

The MRI pack 1.2.2, a rules fix released on its own: an install on engine 1.0.0-alpha.80 or later takes it with `nils update --part rules`, or with Update on the MRI rules row of the desk's Settings › Parts, with no engine release and nothing started again. It works with engines `>=1.0.0-alpha.80, <2.0.0` and pack contract 10. `nils update --pin mri@1.2.1` goes back to the version before.

### Fixed

- Seven values are no longer read from the words of a gradient-echo scout (an image typed LOCALIZER or SCOUT, or a Siemens position display, on a gradient echo), whose name is that of the series it plans: STIR, FatSat, FLAIR and DIR on the modifier axis, and Trace, ADC and Velocity on the construct axis. A value the header states still stands (ImageType, ScanOptions, an inversion time, a diffusion private element, GE's coil label), and Velocity also stands where the scout's own sequence name states a phase contrast. Replayed before release over a production archive of 546,440 MR stacks against 1.2.1, with engine 1.0.0-alpha.81: 210 stacks move, 208 explained by the registered check and 2 missed by it and judged right by reading their headers; the values lost are Trace 157, ADC 32, STIR 37 and FLAIR 15 (above its sized 13, reported as a miss of the estimate), and 140 of the stacks also move the base's basis from physics to name beside a lost Trace or ADC, the base itself unchanged; none newly breaks a pack constraint. Four corpus cases (`packs/mri/corpus/axes-122.yml`).

## [pack-mri-v1.2.1] - 2026-10-15

The MRI pack 1.2.1, a rules fix released on its own: an install on engine 1.0.0-alpha.80 or later takes it with `nils update --part rules`, or with Update on the MRI rules row of the desk's Settings › Parts, with no engine release and nothing started again. It works with engines `>=1.0.0-alpha.80, <2.0.0` and pack contract 10. `nils update --pin mri@1.2.0` goes back to the version before.

### Fixed

- The Dixon modifier is no longer read from the words of a gradient-echo scout (an image typed LOCALIZER or SCOUT, or a Siemens position display, on a gradient echo), whose name is that of the series it plans: a Philips survey named for the mDixon TSE it plans was given Dixon (record 55, ruling 12 (c), an error System 1 had learnt from the rules). Nor is a water or fat construct read from such a scout's words, so Dixon does not reach it that way either; a Dixon, water or fat part the header states (ImageType or the per-frame image type, Philips' Dixon part, ScanOptions DIXW, DIXF or GE's FLEX, GE's `WATER:` or `FAT:` prefix) still stands, and the Dixon modifier also stands where the header states an in-phase and an opposed-phase part together. Replayed before release over a production archive of 546,440 MR stacks against 1.2.0, with engine 1.0.0-alpha.81: 6 stacks move, every one a Philips gradient-echo survey of that kind, from `Dixon,FlowComp` to `FlowComp`, and nothing else moves; none newly breaks a pack constraint. Thirteen corpus cases (`packs/mri/corpus/axes-121.yml`). Other values such scouts still take from the words of the series they plan (STIR, FatSat, FLAIR, DIR, and the Trace, ADC and Velocity constructs) are the next rules item.

### Changed

- Names say the acquisition (record 55, ruling 23: "on SWI we need the SWI in the name too ... if we can do this on systematic level (like adding the acquistion type) it would be good too"). Every image of a family's acquisition (SyMRI, an SWI, STAGE, a mix sequence) carries its acquisition family, the one the scans doors carry since alpha.81, in its descriptive name before the technique (`Ax_T2starw_3D_SWI_GRE_Magnitude-ND`), where nothing in the name says it already: never on a product of the family's route, whose `rec-` names it (`rec-SyMRI`, `rec-SWIRecon`, `rec-STAGE`, `rec-EPIMix`), in a folder of its own (`anat/SyMRI/`), or beside a value that names it (an SWI's minIP, base SWI). Every sidecar's `NILS` object says `AcquisitionFamily` on each image of one. The engine reads the family into a BIDS name where a pack spells it, with an `acq` group `from: acquisition_family`, a field it gives beside `orientation` and `acquisition_type`; the MRI pack 1.2.0 spells none, so BIDS names do not change with this engine. A re-run of a release renames the files it moves.
- A value's word can change without moving a stack (record 55, ruling 23). An axis value of an axis that stores labels may say `stored:`, the spelling rows keep, beside a new `label:`: the rules write the old spelling, every reader reads either as the value, and the scans doors' `axes`, the descriptive names and the packs door read the new word. Contract v7 amended in place.
