- A pack file names a value by the spelling its rows keep too (`stored:`), in a rule's condition and what it sets, an exclusion, the pick model and an overlay, so a rule written before a value's word changed still loads and names it. An axis condition that names a value by a label that differs from what rows keep reads as that value, where it compared the label as text no row holds.
- The Query page reads a value in the word the pack shows (record 55, ruling 23): a group's key and the value sampler say `SPGRE` where rows keep `FLASH`, and a clause on such a key takes either word. A level's signature keeps the stored spelling.
- A rule change's effect report names each side's stacks with that side's own acquisition family, from the routes its rules decided by and the decisions in force, where both sides were named with none: a rule that brings a stack into a family, or a value's new word, moves its name although no axis moves, and the report counts that rename. Its axis moves read in each side's words.
- A value's old spelling (`stored:`) is read as the value by the contrast rules, an effect report's settled answers, `nils explain` and a pack's adopted word lists, and every name field (base, modifier, construct, acceleration, technique) reads in the pack's word.

## [pack-mri-v1.2.0] - 2026-10-14

The MRI pack 1.2.0 on a tag of its own, the first rules release (record 55 B5, spec section 9.2): from now on a rules fix reaches an install with no engine release beside it. It is the pack 1.0.0-alpha.80 carries, file for file, so an install on that engine has it already and an update takes nothing. It works with engines `>=1.0.0-alpha.80, <2.0.0` and pack contract 10.

- An install takes a rules release with `nils update --part rules`, with `nils update --all`, or with Update on the pack's row of the desk's Settings › Parts, and `nils update --check` names it first. The engine's binary stays as it is and nothing is started again: the engine reads a pack again once its files change. A plain `nils update` takes no rules, and an engine in a container gets its rules with the engine's next image.
- A rules release whose pack contract or engine range leaves the running engine out waits, says why in the check, the update and the pack's row, and is never put in place. `nils update --pin mri@<version>` keeps one version through every update until `nils update --unpin mri`; pinning the version before is also how an install goes back.
- What 1.2.0 holds over 1.0.1, and how each step was replayed before release over a production archive of 546,440 MR stacks, is in the notes of engine 1.0.0-alpha.80. In short: 1.0.2, 1.0.3 and 1.1.0 move 7,967 stacks, each explained by the rule meant to move it, and 1.1.0's 1,234 colour SyMRI stacks miss its estimate of about 1,117, reported as a miss; 1.2.0 moves 20,483 stacks, of which the registered check explained 20,145 and missed 338, each read from its header and found right, recorded as a miss of the check and not as a pass. None newly breaks a pack constraint, and the protocol truth table (version 5) follows every ruling to this release.

## [1.0.0-alpha.81] - 2026-10-14

Fixes found on the first dataset read on a site after the beta. A dataset's Main scans page works on a registry on Postgres again: builds of the session cache take turns, and a read that changes nothing writes nothing. SyMRI's acquisition stays in SyMRI's folder; each scan carries the acquisition family its route recognised, so an acquisition's own images are grouped together; a visit's kinds count SyMRI's acquisition with SyMRI; and a dataset's file count says when it stopped short. It goes with desk 1.0.0-alpha.64. The installs it sets up take Kvasir 1.0.0-alpha.10 and the assistant 1.0.0-alpha.28, as before.

### Added

- Each scan of the scans doors (a dataset's, a cohort's, Browse's and a session's stacks) carries `acquisition_family`: the family of the acquisition the pack's routes recognised in it, `symri`, `swi`, `mix` (EPIMix or NeuroMix) or `stage`, from the route whose rules decided one of its axes, as its evidence names the rule set, else from the provenance a route enters on; null where none entered. `family` takes it first, so an acquisition's own images are grouped with what was made of them: SyMRI's acquisition with its maps and synthetic contrasts, an SWI's magnitude, phase and minIP with its processed image, and a projection or a reformat of SyMRI, an SWI or STAGE with its acquisition rather than among the derived images. Nothing is classified again: the evidence a registry holds already says it. Contract v7 amended in place, additively.

### Changed

- The five body-part descriptors pin `nils-bodypart` 0.6.0, the image the 1.0.0-alpha.80 release published (`sha256:06b7ab6b141e3b56e0a26c5d6cfb65a0e8797c76e70b3d70519f37c2e8183a7c`), where they pinned 1.0.0-alpha.71's. Its two encoders bake the same weights as before (`nils_bodypart.bake verify`, offline), so `bodypart-embed`'s encoder digests stand.

### Fixed

- A visit's kinds count SyMRI's multi-dynamic multi-echo or QALAS acquisition with SyMRI, as its folder holds it (record 55 C4), where it counted as Other; the visit's `symri` counts it too. Contract v7 amended in place.
- A dataset's found count says when it stopped short, and a read counts the tree whole. The probe that counts a dataset's tree reads a page's worth of entries and stops short of a large one, and its count was shown as final, so a large dataset's card showed a small part of its files as the whole. `GET /api/datasets/{name}/summary` now says `files.found_partial` and the found step's `partial`, and once a read of the tree NILS reads has finished (for an identified dataset, a pseudonymise run of its originals), the found step and `files.found` count the files it saw, with their bytes where it measured every one (else the probe's where the probe counted the tree whole), and the found step's `counted_by` says which. Contract v7 amended in place, additively.
- SyMRI's multi-dynamic multi-echo or QALAS acquisition stays with the rest of SyMRI (record 55 C4). The MRI pack makes it a working scan, which a BIDS release routed to `sourcedata` before it asked for SyMRI's folder: it now takes the folder's route, still DICOM and never converted, its slices in SyMRI's folder of the DICOM export (`sourcedata/dicom/sub-*/ses-*/anat/SyMRI/<name>/`) as before, so a release's record and a pipeline's plan say it is in the folder. The scans doors showed it under other: its datatype is `anat` and its folder `anat/SyMRI`. The descriptive layout keeps it in `anat/SyMRI` with SyMRI's other images, as v0 did, where it had been in `anat` since the pack called what the scanner acquired RawRecon (MRI pack 0.9.0); a re-run of a descriptive release moves it there. A BIDS release made before writes nothing again, and its record names the folder. Under `--synthetic anat` or `derivatives` it stays in `sourcedata/` under its datatype, in no folder, as SyMRI's other stacks use none there.

- A dataset's Main scans page in Review no longer fails on a registry on Postgres with `duplicate key value violates unique constraint "uq_session_label_session_id_scheme_digest"` (1.0.0-alpha.80). Each of the page's requests brings the session cache up to date for the dataset's subjects before it reads their visits, and the page sends several at once. Each build deleted every label row of those subjects and wrote it again, so under read committed the second of two builds side by side, whose delete could not see the rows the first had just written, failed on them. A build of the cache now holds a transaction lock (`pg_advisory_xact_lock` on a key of the registry's schema; nothing on SQLite, whose writers already take turns), reads the timelines under it, and writes only the labels that changed, as an insert that updates the row its unique key finds: a read whose timelines and labels did not change writes nothing, not even the open review item of an empty study it raised before. Builds that found the cache cold or a span moved at the same time failed the same way, on the session rows' unique key as well as the labels'. A merge of two subjects, which drops their sessions for the next build to make again, takes the same lock from its first write, so a build beside it no longer leaves a label of a session the merge dropped.

## [1.0.0-alpha.80] - 2026-10-14

Wave 7a's beta candidate. Rules ship on their own tag from now on, models are parts a site chooses per task, System 1 stands beside the rules in Review, post-contrast is served through its cascade, and a dataset is scored as it arrives. The installs it sets up take Kvasir 1.0.0-alpha.10 and the assistant 1.0.0-alpha.28. The `system1-score` and `postcontrast-cascade` images, like `postcontrast-full`'s, are built by each site from their folders, which pins its own digest; no release publishes them.

The MRI pack is 1.2.0 and names this engine as its floor (`engine: ">=1.0.0-alpha.80, <2.0.0"`). Every rules change it carries over 1.0.1 was replayed over a production archive of 546,440 MR stacks before release (record 55 C2, gate test T16; the design record's studies `2026-10-13-pack-110-replay` and `2026-10-14-pack-120-replay`):

- 1.0.2, 1.0.3 and 1.1.0: 7,967 stacks move, each explained by the rule meant to move it; none newly breaks a pack constraint and none moves on an axis of the protocol truth table. 1.1.0 moves 1,234 colour SyMRI stacks to the display composite, where its plan estimated about 1,117: a miss of the estimate, reported as one.
- 1.2.0, the stored image component (record 55, rulings 17 and 18): 20,483 stacks move, on construct (20,454, Magnitude added), base (275, PWI to ASL), modifier (29, FlowComp to none) and directory type (48, misc to anat). None newly breaks a constraint. The registered check explained 20,145 of them and missed 338 (290 Philips M-FFE magnitude images and 48 Philips `MDME_TSE` magnitudes); reading their headers found each one right, no rule was changed, and the step is recorded as a miss of its check, not a pass.

The protocol truth table (version 5) follows every ruling to this release. A replay writes to no registry: after updating, a reclassify applies the pack.

This page lists the security changes. Every other change of the wave (changed, added and fixed) is in `CHANGELOG.md` under *1.0.0-alpha.80 in full*, since the whole list is longer than a release page holds.

### Security

- At detail plain a list of the rows a quasi-identifying field chose is held back whole where it holds 1 to 4 subjects, since a list's length is a count (record 55 K7, small lists, the ruling of 2026-10-13). It completes small counts at plain, whose stated limit it was. The rows are chosen by such a field as small counts reads it (a `where` on an age, a sex or a date, a group keyed by one, a `has` within a window of days, a near, and every set that narrows, lies under, combines, groups or keeps such a set). An answer at `level: record` or `aggregate` over such a set, or over a group set of such rows, counts the set's subjects over the whole set, before an `after`, a limit or a page and in the same read, and where they are 1 to 4 answers no row: every column is named in `held_back` with why `a list of fewer than 5 subjects that a quasi identifying field chose; its length is a count, so no row is shown`, `row_count` is null and `truncated` false, on a run and its cached answer, a preview, a handle, its pages and the list of handles, a kept set, a job's result, the CSV export (the header alone, and the why on stderr) and MCP. The handle holds no row and no key, so no later page reads one; a set read from it, a start from it and its promotion into a cohort are refused (409, `handle N holds no rows`), since each would answer none where there were some. The diagnosis's funnel with keys holds such a stage as a held count, and a pick's ties over it are null. None and 5 subjects or more are listed as before, a list that technical and clinical fields alone chose (the sixth visits, 3 subjects) is listed whatever its length, and detail quasi and above see every list. A handle records `quasi_lists` in its suppression, so no answer cached while such a list held its rows is served again. The gate adds `quasi-list-few` (2 rows), `quasi-list-few-plain` (held) and `quasi-list-many-plain` (36 rows). Spec section 7.1 states the rule and no longer names the list as a limit; contract v7 amended in place.
- At detail plain a count under 5 subjects of rows a quasi-identifying field chose or grouped is held back (record 55 K7, small counts, the ruling of 2026-10-12). A field chooses the rows where a `where` reads it through any comparison or function (an age, a date, the sex), where a group is keyed by it or by a value made from it (an age band, a year), where a `has` counts within a window of days or a near finds a partner, and in every set that narrows, lies under, combines, groups or keeps the rows of such a set; a `pick` chooses none. A count of such a set (`level: count`, its rows and subjects) is null for 1 to 4 subjects, whether any (`level: boolean`) is null there, and a group's `_rows`, `_subjects`, `count` and `distinct` are null for a group of 1 to 4 subjects; a group's count of its own by such a field (a `count` of a value made from one, a `sum` or `avg` of a comparison such as the women of a cohort) is held where it, or the group's other subjects, are 1 to 4. So that no held count is a total less the counts shown, every line of groups that holds a count holds one more, the group with the fewest subjects shown, where the line held one alone or only counts under 5; the groups held are decided over every group, before a page or a limit. What is made of a held count, and a share of it or over such a set of 1 to 4 subjects, is held with it. The answer names the columns in `held_back` with why `fewer than 5, or held beside a count of fewer than 5 so that it is not found by subtraction`, on a run, its cached answer, a preview, a handle and its pages, and a job's result. The diagnosis's funnel holds a stage's rows, subjects and keys the same way, and where its clause dropped 1 to 4 subjects from the stage before it: `rows`, `subjects`, a drop's `before`, `after` and `nulls`, a clause group's `kept` and `lost`, and the counts of `ties` and `coarse` may now be null, and a held stage carries `held_back`; the opening door's `count`, `subjects` and `sessions` and the profile door's counts may be null too. Counts grouped and filtered by technical or clinical fields stay exact, and detail quasi sees every count. A handle records `quasi_counts` in its suppression, so no answer cached while such counts were numbers is served again. At plain, gate C's count and share of women per cohort are now held, since one cohort's women leave 3 men; the gate adds `quasi-age-bands` and `quasi-count-few`, each at the gate's detail and at plain. Spec section 7.1 says what is held and why, and names as a limit that a list of the rows such a field chose is still answered at plain.
- At detail plain a group's mean and spread of a quasi-identifying field, an age for one, are answered when the group holds 5 subjects or more with a value, as a pipeline's measures are under record 49 R4, and held back as null below (record 55 K7, the ruling of 2026-10-12). Subjects are counted, never rows: one subject's five visits are one subject. The mean is a group set's `avg` over its child, and what the group's other bindings and the answer's columns make of it alone; the spread is the post pass's `stddev` of a field of the group's child, which was refused at plain, so gold C's standard deviation of age is answered at plain again. A group's count of distinct values of such a field (`distinct`) is held back the same way. A group's min and max, a median and a percentile are one person's own value and stay shapes or refusals, and a sum and a list stay shapes; a mean or a count of distinct values made anywhere but as a group set's own binding reads one subject's rows and is a shape. Every answer that holds rows names the columns with a value held back and why, beside `shaped`: `held_back`, `[{column, why}]` with why `a group under 5 subjects`, on a run and its cached answer, a preview, a handle and a page of it (`held_back` on a handle's column), and a job's result. The engine's own reads for a group's spread are no longer shaped, so a group keyed by a quasi-identifying field matches its rows. A handle records `quasi_totals` in its suppression, so no answer cached while a group's mean was a shape is served again. The gate adds `gold-c-demographics-plain`, `quasi-by-visit` and `quasi-by-visit-plain`, and `quasi-aggregates-plain` now answers the mean. Plain detail still allows filters, orders and counts, so repeated questions can narrow a value; the specification states it as a limit. Contract v7 amended in place, additively.
- A check never holds a sealed scan's result (record 55, the rulings of 2026-10-12, item 6; record 48, sealed means sealed). A check made from a run could hold the mask of a stack of a sample sealed now, and its raters then read what a system said of a sealed stack: the parts and layer doors let a rater of an open campaign that holds a result read it even while it was withheld from everyone else. Now New check's preview and its make leave out every result made from a stack sealed now, read as the derivative doors read what it was made from, before anything else is counted, for any caller, and say how many in `left_out_sealed` (kept in the made check's `source.counts`), never which; a source that leaves nothing else is refused saying so. A check made before that holds such a result withholds its item from every rater and adjudicator without `sealed:see`: a claim never offers it, and the item door, an answer and a correction refuse it with 403 and `sealed: true` (at the keyboard, `nils campaign claim` and `answer` withhold it the same unless `--unsealed-access` says why); a holder of `sealed:see` reads it, audited as `sealed.read`. Its close writes nothing of it, whoever closes, and counts it in `left_out_sealed`. The parts and layer doors no longer let a rater of a campaign that holds such a result read it.
- An aggregate of a quasi-identifying field follows the field, and every answer names the columns it shaped (record 55 K7, 2026-10-12). A chat bench at detail plain wrote the column of `min`, `max` and `avg` as an option (`["min", {set: firsts, column: [...]}]`), which was read as nothing, so each totalled the rows' own keys and the preview named a youngest and an oldest age that were row numbers; no age was shown. An aggregate now takes one option, `set`, and reads one column, its argument: a column written as an option, a `min`, `max`, `sum`, `avg` or `list` without one, a tuple or a literal is refused at every detail with the form to write; `count` and `distinct` still read the rows without one. Below detail quasi the `min`, `max`, `sum`, `avg` and `list` of a quasi-identifying field, and a group's key made from it, are its shapes at every size of the group, as before, and a count of rows and of distinct values is answered; a group's `median`, `percentile` or `stddev` of such a field of its child, which was answered as null, is refused with the reason, so gold C's standard deviation of age is refused at plain. A `share` of such a field was answered raw beside the field's own shape, and a share of an age over a set of 38 is the age over 38: it is now a shape as the field is, and what a share divides is held to the caller's classes as a field is. Validation also checks every clause beside an aggregate: it stopped at the first aggregate of a clause, so a field after it went unchecked, an unknown name or a sensitive field, which detail quasi then projected raw. Every answer that holds rows says which columns it answered as shapes: `shaped` on a run and its cached answer, a preview, a handle and a page of it, and a job's result, and `shaped: true` on a handle's column; a handle cached before this is not answered again from the cache. The gate runs a fixture at the detail it names (`detail`), and `quasi-aggregates-plain` holds the shapes of the bench's question on both backends.
- Sealed means sealed in a run's QC summary (record 48, 2026-10-12). `GET /api/pipeline-runs`, one run, and a run job's result at the jobs doors showed every unit's breaches at detail quasi, a sealed stack's unit and its checks' values among them. For every caller but a holder of `sealed:see`, the breaches of a unit holding a stack of a sample sealed now (by the stacks the run gave the unit, else by the list the run was frozen over) are now left out, and `breaches_withheld_sealed` says how many units went, never which; below detail quasi that count is held as any count of scans is. A holder of `sealed:see` reads them all, audited as `sealed.read`; `nils pipeline runs` and `nils jobs` withhold the same unless `--unsealed-access` says why. A session's or a subject's `pipeline:qc` item, which names no stack, was listed by the review doors whatever its session held; it is now withheld by the stacks its run gave the unit, as a stack's item already was.
- The review of Wave 7a's merge (2026-10-10), privacy and security: a pseudonymised copy's marks tell the truth (the pseudonymiser also removes the `ids` category, the descriptions the rules read are cleaned of the file's own identifiers and claimed as the Clean Descriptors Option, the basic profile is claimed only while no direct identifier is left, and a dataset's `keep` never retains one); a folder's loose entries are named only to Data work at detail sensitive and never in an audit row; exploring the sources is an audited POST and no longer a read; the folders doors decode their names and refuse a symbolic link out of the root; held/code queues the pseudonymiser only at detail sensitive; a preview is cached as immutable only under an address that names its held state; a cohort's steps leave a sealed sample out for a caller without its grant; a personnummer that is not ASCII is refused rather than crashing the read; and a personnummer rule or generated codes are refused on a registry whose codes are not the subject code generator's.
- Sealed means sealed at the derivative doors (record 48, 2026-10-12): `GET /api/derivatives/{id}/content` served a pipeline's table whole, and a pipeline may score a sealed stack into it. For every caller but a holder of `sealed:see`, a table whose rows name their stacks (a run's table by its declared unit column, another by a column of stack ids) is now answered without the rows of a stack of a sample sealed now, with `X-Nils-Withheld-Sealed` saying how many went and never which; anything else made from such a stack, as its row and its run's units say (a stack's own table or mask, a session's file whose unit was given one, a run's own file), is refused whole with the reason, and `transport=share` is refused where rows would be withheld. The parts and layer doors refuse such a result to `pipelines:see` alone, a check's raters reading it as before; its row keeps the file's facts without its parts' counts and its check, and is not listed by a check's status; `nils derivative list`, `show`, `parts` and `layer` withhold the same at the keyboard unless `--unsealed-access` says why. A holder of `sealed:see` reads all of it, audited as `sealed.read`. The ask reads a pipeline's measure of a session or a subject that holds a sealed stack as null; a stack's measure already left with its stack. A stack's pictures and a model's artifact are never withheld.

## [1.0.0-alpha.80 in full] - 2026-10-14

The rest of 1.0.0-alpha.80's changes, kept apart from its section above only because a release page holds 125,000 characters.

### Changed

- The MRI pack 1.2.0 records the stored image component for six researched cases (record 55, rulings 17 and 18): a MEDIC (or Philips M-FFE) image as acquired, an acquired QALAS or SyMRI (MDME) image, an SWI-type EPI with SWI processing off, and an ASL label or control image are construct `Magnitude` (an acquired SyMRI image its component as stored, and one that names none `Magnitude`), even as the raw inputs of a quantitative method; every other plain acquisition keeps no `Magnitude`. The base axis gains `ASL` for the label and control images of an arterial spin labelling acquisition, whose difference stays `PWI` and whose M0 scan stays `PDw`; an ASL image's directory type stays `perf`. An acquired MDME image takes no modifier, GE MAGiC's flow compensation included. Replayed over a production archive of 546,440 stacks before it ships: 20,483 move, on the construct, the base of 275 ASL images, the modifier of 29 MAGiC images and the directory type of 48 unrouted MDME images, and none breaks a constraint. A release's names follow: the stored component is the `part` entity where BIDS allows it (`part-mag` on a MEDIC's `T2starw`) and is spelled into `acq-` where it does not (an acquired SyMRI series' folder `...MDMEMagnitudeND`, an ASL label or control image's `acq-`).
- A pseudonymised tree's series folders carry the series description (record 55 K9, ruling 18): `<SeriesNumber>-<description>`, the number padded as before and the description, as the copy keeps it, cleaned of the file's own identifiers, reduced to letters, digits and hyphens (accented Latin letters folded to their base letter) and cut to 40 characters; the number alone where the description is empty, removed by the dataset or reduced to nothing. Only a new tree is written so: a tree begun before keeps its numbered folders, and a resumed run keeps the scheme its tree was begun with, which a run records in its job's and batch's `config` (`series_folders`: `description` or `number`). Existing trees are never renamed.
- The public code names no cohort and no internal path (record 55, ruling 18): comments, tests and pack text name the cohort by its letter, the Wave 4a cohort gate takes `--cohort NAME` instead of a cohort written into it, and the setup tests' site places are generic.
- A promotion retires no model (record 55 D4): several promoted models stay in a task and slot, and the site chooses the one that answers, so an earlier version stays runnable. A retired model may be run again by name (`nils run --model`), from the file kept while it served, to reproduce a past result; what it proposes is asked, never staged. The promote door's `retired` is always null and `retired_models` empty.

- The SAMSEG with lesions starter separates the pallidum from the white matter only where it reads the FLAIR, and says which it did (record 57 P11, accepted 2026-10-12; research 02, SAMSEG's wiki). The earlier versions passed `--pallidum-separate` for a T1w alone too (lesions off), where SAMSEG cannot tell the two apart; now a session read from the T1w alone is never separated, and one with its FLAIR is separated where the setting `pallidum_separate` is on (the default). Separating it changes the pallidum and white-matter volumes, so each session's table gains `pallidum_separate`, 1 or 0, for a result's key to carry. The new version replaces the earlier ones (`x-nils.replaces`), and the engine adds it when it starts.
- The segcsvd starter names the SynthSeg each session read (record 57 P11; research 04): robust and not are different networks, and segcsvd's WMH model was trained with a SynthSeg of the FLAIR while this reads the T1w's. Each session's table gains `synthseg_run`, `synthseg_pipeline` (name@version), `synthseg_robust` (1 or 0) and `synthseg_contrast` (the suffix of the file of the scan the label map is tied to, FLAIR for a T2w with the FLAIR token; left out where the map is tied to no scan). The new version replaces the earlier ones, and the engine adds it when it starts. For it, the manifest a run's container reads (`/inputs/manifest.json`) lists with each derivative it takes `made_by`: the run that made it, its pipeline's name and version, the parameters it ran with, and the scan the derivative is tied to (`grid_stack_id`) with the file name the release that run read gave it (`grid_stem`); null for a derivative no run made. The job contract v1 takes it in place, additively.
- A pipeline's version is chosen, and a version that fixes the earlier ones replaces them (Nima's ruling of 2026-10-12: "if it fixes any bug then we simply dont show previous version"). A descriptor says it with `x-nils.replaces` (`why`, and `versions` where not every earlier one), which the job contract v1 takes in place, additively. The catalog answers each version's `standing`: `current` (the newest active version, which a name alone runs), `selectable` (an older version nothing replaces) or `replaced` (with `replaced_by` and `replaced_why`), and, beside the entries, `names`, each name with its current version and the versions a new run may take, newest first (`GET /api/pipelines`, `GET /api/pipelines/{id}`, `nils pipeline list` and `show`). A new run of a replaced version is refused by `nils run`, the pre-flight and the jobs door (409, with `replaced`), naming the version that replaces it, why, and the version a new run takes; `nils run <name>@<version> --replaced` at the keyboard runs it still, and no door takes that word. Its runs, derivatives and checks read and name it as before, and a run taken up again goes on with the version it recorded. The starters whose new versions fix the old replace every earlier version: freesurfer-recon-all (its memory), mriqc (`--notrack`), samseg-lesions and segcsvd (the FLAIR alignment check). N4's new version changes what it makes, so its earlier version stays selectable. A catalog that already holds the new versions of those four gets one more, the same with `replaces`. The OpenAPI 7 contract is amended in place, additively.
- The N4 starter sets its B-spline distance, convergence and histogram sharpening explicitly and writes the bias field it estimated (the review of the starters' settings, 2026-10-12). The ANTs wiki says the spline spacing "should always be set explicitly", between 100 and 200 mm for a human brain: the new parameter `spline_distance` (180 mm, the wiki's example, 100 to 200) is recorded with every run like the others, as `-b [180,3]`, and `-c [50x50x50x50,0.0]` and `-t [0.15,0.01,200]`, ANTs' own defaults, are written out. Each session gains a second output, `bias_field` (`*_desc-n4_biasfield.nii.gz`), on the corrected image's grid. A brain mask (`-x`) needs a SynthStrip run first, so it is left to a pack rule. Corrected images are not comparable with those of the older version, which fitted one mesh element over the whole image. The engine adds the starter's new version when it starts.
- The segcsvd starter says its WMH model is validated for white-matter hyperintensities of vascular origin at 3 T and not for MS lesions, in its description and on its `wmh_volume` column (the review, 2026-10-12).
- A dataset is a folder that holds stacks and files, never subjects, and only a cohort has members (Nima's model of 2026-10-12, which corrects the ruling of 2026-10-11 recorded here before: "subject only belong to cohorts. its the stack that belong to dataset"). The subjects counted in a dataset are those with a stack its tree holds a file of, and one place in the registry counts them (`dataset_subjects`): the sources door's `totals.subjects`, the `subjects` of `GET /api/datasets/{name}/summary`, `GET /api/picks/summary` and `pick run --dataset`, and `GET /api/datasets/{name}/subjects` and its visits door. A subject whose files in a dataset were all set aside by the read (none a scan) is not counted in it, and the `no_scans` the earlier ruling added is gone: the summary's `files.set_aside_no_scans` says instead how many subjects with no stack in the dataset its set-aside files are of, and how many of those files are theirs, where its pseudonymise run recorded whose a file is. A stack with files in two datasets is one stack with a path in each (record 55): each dataset counts it and its subject once. The ask's `dataset` of a stack, a subject or a session names every dataset holding a file of the stack or of one of its stacks, not only the one that read it first, so a subject is counted in a dataset when some of its stacks are there, and a subject with no stack reads null; `<>` and `not_in` on a stack's `dataset` take a stack of no dataset as they do a subject's. An answer that reads a dataset says so in its conventions: datasets are folders that hold stacks, and only cohorts have members.
- A model server named to setup gives Kvasir every model it offers (2026-10-11, Nima: "if several llm exist on them then all should be available not just one"). On the first add, `nils setup --model-server URL --model-key-file FILE` admits all of the server's models in one `POST /v1/servers`, the stations' model asked last so it stays loaded on a server with one card, and maps the stations to that one. `--model-server-model ID` names the stations' model; with none named and nobody to ask, the server's default is taken, else its only model, and otherwise the run stops as before and names what the server offers. Only the stations' model failing stops an install; another that does not answer or does not pass admission is said in one line. A server offering more than eight models has the stations' model alone admitted, and setup says to add more from Settings > Kvasir. A server Kvasir holds already, as on an update or a rerun, is sent the stations' model alone, so a model removed in the desk stays removed.
- The main scans have one place of truth (Nima's ruling of 2026-10-11: "chat and the page should not disagree and we have to have one place of truth"). One resolver says which picks are the main scans of a stack and a role, and every reader asks it: the ask's `picked` and its role sets, the viewer, releases and the pipeline runs that read them, the pipelines' pre-flight, the Main scans page's doors, the picks summary and the dataset summary, the timeline and `nils pick list`. A reader about a dataset or a cohort reads its own picks once a run of its saved rules finished, else the pack's; a reader about no scope reads each subject's own dataset's by the same rule, else the pack's (a subject's dataset is one whose tree holds a file of its stacks; of two with rules, the lower id). A reader about a scope reads its subjects alone. An ask that reads picks is never answered from the cache, since a pick run moves no epoch. The ask's `picked` takes `{dataset: NAME}` or `{cohort: NAME}`, and a document's description says which picks it read. The Main scans page's saved side shows the picks stored, as every other reader reads them, and a draft beside them. A release of no scope records the datasets whose own picks it read (`pick_rules.datasets`). `nils pick list` takes `--dataset` and `--cohort`. Before, the ask and every reader but a scope's own read the pack's picks, so the chat counted other main scans than the page showed for a dataset with rules of its own.
- A subject code made from an ID is final (Nima's ruling of 2026-10-10, found trying the desk: "the subject code should be created from their ID"). A dataset that codes what no map names (`unmapped: code`) and a person's "code anyway" (`POST /api/linkage/held/code`, the desk's Generate subject codes) make each subject's code from its ID under the registry's key, as before, but the subject is no longer provisional and no `identity.provisional` question is raised: the person chose it, and the same ID gives the same subject code wherever it is read. A map that later names such an ID under another subject is refused as a conflict, which a merge answers. Subjects an older engine marked provisional keep their mark and their open items until a merge closes them.
- A dataset's jobs are its own (2026-10-10, found trying the desk): a job made for another dataset is never its, and where its folder or name was a removed dataset's, only the jobs since it was added are, so a folder added again no longer shows the removed dataset's failed run as "stopped".
- A removed place's name is free again (2026-10-10): the retired row keeps its id, path and history under `<name>.retired-<id>`, and a folder added again takes its own name, not the root's before it.
- An identified dataset whose PatientID holds an ID that is the same everywhere never writes PatientID as written into its pseudonymised copy (2026-10-10): `patient_id: id-type:patient-id` is refused there, since that is the ID itself.
- A changed identity rule or unmapped setting has the dataset's held files read again on the next pseudonymise run (2026-10-10): a held file was filed under the setting it was held under, and an unchanged file was never read again, so every run asked for the same IDs' subject codes.
- nils-bodypart's cascade may overlap its stages (record 55 E2): with `--overlap on` one worker pool, started once the student has loaded, reads stage 1's slices and the student's tokens, and climbs the 24-frame and touched stages of every stack the moment stage 1 defers it, while the student scores the next batch and a second thread runs the head, the calibration and the deferral model. The student's batches are the sequential path's, and every stack is scored by the same functions, so the answers are the same; `--overlap off`, the default, is the sequential path.
- nils-bodypart's cascade honours `--gpu-workers` (record 55 E2): with `--device cuda` the planes stages decoded on the card in two workers whatever the option said, since the cascade did not pass it on. Now 0 keeps every decode on the CPU and puts only the student on the card, and the run's parameters name the decode device and the card's workers.
- nils-bodypart's image takes torch 2.13.0 and torchvision 0.28.0, where it took 2.5.1 and 0.20.1, in both its builds (record 55 E2): the CPU build as released, and the card build, whose `TORCH_INDEX` build argument now names the `cu129` wheels. torch 2.5.1's card wheels had no kernels for Blackwell cards (sm_120), so the cascade's student could not run on one. 2.13.0 is the newest torch with a CUDA 12 build, which the image's CUDA 12 nvImageCodec wheels need. No model, code path or other pin changes. A study found the CPU build's answers unchanged on 19,275 stacks (every stage, value and probability), and the card build's equal to the CPU build's on 3,213 stacks, with the student on a Blackwell card.
- nils-bodypart 0.5.0 (record 55 E2, P1b): `bodypart-infer-fusion` runs body part's cascade where the run is given one (ruling R1 (a): one model whose file names its parts), with the optional model inputs `student`, `deferral` and `cascade`. Each stack is answered by the first stage that may: the few-slice student over one native slice (the few-slice study's slot-0 rule, 192 x 192 at 1.5 mm, its tokens, the encoder's 15 geometry numbers and the 44 header features, all from the manifest alone; the stack's height against its session's top over the study's unsealed MR stacks, the run's and those the engine lists as the session's context, so a stack's input is the same in any run that holds it), accepted where the deferral model D3 is at least 0.99; then the planes of at most 24 frames, accepted at a log-odds margin of 0.5 from both thresholds; then the touched frames, reusing what the 24-frame stage decoded. The scores table gains `stage`, `files_read`, `reason` (`sealed`, `not-mr`, `no-geometry`, `not-sure`, `unreadable`) and the cascade's, student's and deferral model's digests, so every stack carries a body region or a stated reason; each `body_part` decision names the cascade. Each table also says how the first stage ended for the stack (`stage1`: `answered`, `deferred`, or why it went straight to the planes: `no-study`, `no-file-geometry`, `multi-frame`, `no-slice`, `slice-unreadable`), and a stack with no answer counts the files its reads opened. The student runs on the CPU with threads of its own (`--student-threads`, 32 by default, the count the graded answers were made with), not the run's `--threads`, since its numbers depend on them; its last batch is partial and not padded, as in the graded runs. `--cache` keeps the inputs each stage read per session, keyed by each file's SOP Instance UID, the sha256 of its bytes and the stack's frames of it, and a digest of the frames the input was made of (so the stacks of one enhanced file are entries of their own), never a registry id, written after the run's answers and on a best-effort basis (a session's file that cannot be read is replaced, one that cannot be written is counted, and neither fails the run; each file is synced before it is renamed into place), and `--cache-fill touched` builds the touched planes of stacks an earlier stage answered; each unit reports the sha256 of the files it read whole. The research cascade's rules are reproduced on synthetic stacks (the slot-0 file, the session's top, the tokens, Postgres's rounding of positions, the stage's features and D3's 62); the student runs in float32 on the CPU, new numerics that the speed study's amendment 5 grades (A5.2) before the cascade is used.
- nils-bodypart 0.4.0 (record 55 E2): `bodypart-infer-fusion` reads, by default, only the frames the certified encoder's six planes interpolate from (`--reader reduced:touched`, about 39 files a stack where the full reader read about 78), chosen from each file's geometry in `stacks.json`, which its descriptor now asks for (`x-nils.input.geometry`), and reads four stacks ahead. A study found it gives the certified answers within its registered equivalence bar (fine 5,998 of 6,000, coarse 6,000 of 6,000), and reading every kept frame from the geometry (`reduced:full`) gives the full reader's planes bit for bit. `--reader full` reads every file as the model was certified. A file the manifest gives no geometry for, a multi-frame file and a stack of mixed orientations have their headers read, and a file decoded on a card has its header read too, so the card's answers are the CPU's. The descriptor's `reader` and `readahead` parameters choose; the image reads the engine's geometry and the earlier short names alike.
- A dataset's chain says where it stopped (2026-10-10, found trying the desk): `GET /api/datasets/{name}/summary` marks its `pseudonymised`, `read`, `sorted` and `main_scans` steps `failed` where the newest job of the step failed and none of them runs, so a run that stopped at its first step no longer looks as if nothing happened. The job's own words stay the jobs door's.
- An identifying ID that is the same everywhere (a personnummer, a national ID) has an ID type in every registry (2026-10-10, found trying the desk: a dataset whose PatientID held one failed at its first run on a registry without the type, and the page said nothing). Registry schema 86 adds it where it is missing; `nils init` seeds it; it is never a namespace an answer reveals, since NILS keeps no such value. A dataset that names an ID type the registry lacks is refused when it is declared (Data's doors, `nils place add`, `nils place set`, `nils place add-dataset`), in words that name the types there are. `GET /api/linkage/types` gives each type a `label`, what a person reads. The messages a person reads say "an ID that is the same everywhere", "hospital or study ID" and "subject code" where they said personnummer or a bare code.
- A read that brings nothing new ends its chain before the sort (2026-10-10, found trying the Data page): a digest that added, changed and removed no file and made, emptied or folded no stack leaves the chain's fingerprint, classify, pick and picture steps unqueued, and its result says which as `chain_ended {why, skipped}`. A sort judges every stack and its pick every subject it judged, so a dataset brought in again with nothing new, or one whose files the registry holds already through another dataset, no longer sorts and picks the whole registry again.
- The MRI pack is 1.0.2 and declares pack contract 9 (review of 2026-10-10). Its content had changed since 1.0.1 under the same version, so `mri@1.0.1` named two packs in classification rows, releases and the disagreements door. Since 1.0.1: its review block names `by_model` (body part, body region and post-contrast, each answered by an operation of its own) and is silent on a localizer as on an excluded stack (record 56 section 2); its BIDS mapping writes FLAIR as a modifier with no `_FLAIR` suffix, fills `acq-` slot by slot in v0's order in the full and minimal naming modes, and places derivatives and SyMRI's folders (record 55 C4). No verdict of the rules changed: an axis value read under 1.0.1 reads the same under 1.0.2, so a registry needs no re-sort. 1.0.0 stays the certified pack. The patch contract's example now patches 1.0.2 into 1.0.3.
- A pseudonymise run counts its dataset's trees again when it ends, as a probe does (2026-10-10, found trying the Data page): the copy it wrote is counted at once, so the sources door and `GET /api/datasets/{name}/summary` show the pseudonymised files and the step reads done without waiting for the next probe. A dry run counts nothing again.
- A file whose subject, study, series and instance UIDs are an instance the registry holds is a location of that instance, not a file to skip (record 55, Nima's duplicate policy of 2026-10-10). Registry schema 87: `source_file` says when a file was first seen, and `source_stack` which sources hold a file of each stack, filled for a registry from before. A dataset holds every scan its tree has a file of, whoever read it first: the sources door, `GET /api/datasets/{name}/summary`, the scans and viewer doors, a step's Run and a dataset's pictures count and list them. A read counts its copies as `known` (another dataset read the instance first) or `twice` (the dataset holds another file of it). A file whose instance UID the registry holds under another subject, study or series is neither: it is held, quarantined as `identity.same_instance`, read again by every run and asked about once per source and pair of subjects, and a merge of the two subjects answers it. A copy a registry from before filed by its instance UID alone is marked `unchecked` and compared on the next read of its source.
- A main-scan pick takes one acquisition time as one scan (the 2026-10-10 study of the pick borders, R6, after record 38's ruling on what a rescan is). A retake is full stacks taken at different times. Series of one candidate acquired at one moment are one acquisition stored twice, a second reconstruction or a re-send: the candidate keeps the scanner's original image over a derived one and then the series it wrote first, and raises no retake. A stack of two images or fewer is a fragment, no candidate and no part of one, so a spin echo split into stacks of 2, 19 and 1 images is a take of 19, and a session whose role holds fragments alone has nothing eligible, which asks as before. A stack whose images carry no acquisition time is a moment of its own, and a family's retake counts takes, not stacks. The near-tie order between two candidates is not part of this (a ruling still owed). On a real study corpus, with the frames counted, the open pick borders go from 59 to 56: a 3D FLAIR stored twice settles with its pick narrowed to one series, a split spin echo keeps only its near tie, and a session of single-image planning pieces now says nothing eligible.
- A sort asks a person only where it is truly necessary (record 55 H3, Nima's ruling of 2026-10-09: "the pack decides"). A conflict between the pack's rules is no longer a review item: where one rule decided an axis over another (an earlier rule set, an earlier rule of the same set, a lower priority in an exclusion group), who beat whom, what each cited, their tiers and confidences and what ranked them are kept on the stack's classification. An answer below the pack's threshold is no longer a review item either; it is noted beside them, and only a person's own `nils classify --review-below` still asks about one. The sort raises no question about the body part or the post-contrast, each answered by its own operation in its own run, a step of a dataset and of a cohort (record 56 section 2; the MRI pack's new `review.by_model: [body_part, body_region, post_contrast]`); what the rules state of either is kept as they state it. The split note (`split:one_image_per_stack`, a series split into stacks of one image each) is information on the stack, no longer a question. What is still asked is `<axis>:missing`, now for every axis that matters and that no rule answered and no pass filled: the engine works out which axes matter from what reads them (the release's descriptive name and layout, the pack's BIDS mapping, its pick models, and what a release does with a stack), and asks where such an axis holds one value, has no default and is not an image model's, which for the MRI pack is `base`; an axis a rule decided to be nothing is answered, and a scout is not asked about at all: the MRI pack's `review.silent_when` now holds for a stack whose directory type is `localizer` as for an `excluded` one, since a scout has no base contrast by definition. Asked after the passes, so a value the physics vote or a session pass fills is not asked about. A classify of stacks that raised the old questions supersedes the open ones. Registry schema 85: a classification keeps `notes`, what the sort decided without asking, and `disagreements`, how many answers on it nothing in the pack ranks.
- Answers on one axis that nothing in the pack's ranking puts one above the other (one rule whose two values both hold on an axis that holds one, a set that collects writing two values on such an axis, two values of one exclusion group at the same priority) are a defect of the pack, never a question: the classify report counts them by rule pair, the stack's notes keep them, and `GET /api/packs/{name}/disagreements` (under `review:see`) gathers them by rule pair with how many stacks and up to ten of their ids, for whoever tunes the pack. The classify report also counts the overrides by what ranked them, the answers below a threshold and the axes no rule answered per axis, the missing questions per axis and the axes they are asked on, and the split notes.
- `GET /api/explain/{stack}` and `nils explain` show the notes (`notes`: `overrides`, `below`, `unresolved`, `split`, `equal_rank`; the words a rule matched at detail quasi and above, as for the evidence); `GET /api/stacks/{stack}/why` gives each axis the rules it was decided over (`overridden`) and a weak answer's threshold (`below`), and the axes no rule answered (`unresolved`); each scan of `GET /api/datasets/{name}/scans` carries the split note in `notes`, and a source's `totals.noted` on `GET /api/sources` counts it, outside `to_sort` and `need_a_look`. `GET /api/packs/{name}` and `nils pack show` say which axes matter and why (`review.matters`), where a missing answer is asked (`review.asks_missing`, and each axis's `asks_when_missing`) and which axes are an image model's (`review.by_model`); the pack's own `review.missing` list is still accepted and no longer decides. An overlay's rehearsal predicts the questions by the same rule.
- "Need a look" means one thing wherever it is shown (record 56, 2026-10-09): a scan needs a look while a question the sort itself asks about it waits for a person, open or staged: an axis that matters with no answer, a person's own `--review-below`, a person's decision the rules disagree with, an older engine's conflict, a broken constraint of the pack and System 1's question. The sources door's `to_sort`, `sure` and `need_a_look` and each recent digest's `to_sort` (the card), the Data page's summary, a cohort's sorted step, the scans doors' `questions` (the Grid's marks) and the Grid's `look` on subjects and visits all count by that one definition, so they agree. A question about body part or post-contrast, whoever asked it, is that step's to look at; a pass's question about another axis (a vote, a session answer) is the sorted step's new `passes`; a pick border is the main scans' `borders`; a model's question about another axis and an identity question are Review's alone. None of them is a look any more, where the sources door, the scans doors and the Grid counted every open question before. Contract v7 amended in place.
- A new registry is made with the subject code generator's scheme, `blake2b-8`, by default, by `nils setup` and by `nils init`: a person's code is the keyed 8-byte BLAKE2b of their identifier (of a personnummer, its twelve digits) under the site's own constant key, as 16 hex characters, which is the function a site's existing codes were made with. `blake2b-32` stays selectable with `--scheme blake2b-32`. A registry keeps the scheme it was made with; a setup rerun that names another is refused.
- An identifier filed under the id type `personnummer` is read as its twelve digits before its code or its lookup is derived: separators and spaces are dropped, a ten digit number gets its century (a `+` meaning a hundred years or more), and a coordination number keeps its day as written. So one person derives one code however a source wrote the number. A value of that type that is no personnummer (another shape, a date that is no date, a wrong check digit) does not answer the identity rule, which goes on to its next source with an `identity_unparsed` diagnostic, and refuses its row in a map import, which names the column and never the value. Identifiers of every other type are hashed as read. A registry that filed personnummer under their written forms before this release finds them under the twelve digits now, so a map of such numbers is best imported again.
- A personnummer is its own map: where a dataset's identity rule files personnummer, the subject code generator codes every valid number, and its subject is a subject, never a provisional one, also where the dataset holds what no map named. A file whose PatientID is no valid personnummer is held or coded as the dataset says, as before.
- A source place is read from its structure, never from a declaration (Nima's ruling of 2026-10-08: the declaration comes from the structure). A source place is a root, added alone (Nima's ruling of 2026-10-08: adding data is the one moment its structure is expected): a folder under it becomes a dataset, a source place of its own under the root, only when a person adds it, with `nils place add-dataset ROOT FOLDER` or `POST /api/places` with `{role: "source", root, folder}` (or a `path` under a root), and only then is its structure read. `nils place folders ROOT [--search Q] [--limit N] [--after NAME]` and `GET /api/places/{id}/folders?q=&limit=&after=` list a page of a root's folders found by name, in one read of the root's listing and never looking inside a folder: `{root, root_id, path, q, matching, count, folders: [{name, path, added, dataset_id, dataset}], next}`, 50 a page by default and 200 at most, `next` the name to page on from. `nils place folder ROOT NAME` and `GET /api/places/{id}/folders/{name}` look at one folder before it is added, changing nothing: `{name, path, added, dataset_id, dataset, holds_dicom: yes|no|unknown, has_derivatives, layout}`, `layout` the structure it would have as a dataset. A dataset's state is what its folder holds: only `derivatives/dcm-original` is `identified` (the pseudonymiser reads it and writes `dcm-anon`); only `derivatives/dcm-anon`, or `dcm-raw`, renamed and shown, is `anonymised` (the registry reads it); both is `both`, identified with its anonymised copy; anything else is `unknown`: entries holding DICOM beside `derivatives/`, or no tree at all. A folder the place names that holds `derivatives/` is one dataset; one that names a dataset's `derivatives/dcm-raw` or `dcm-anon` itself is `legacy`, read as that tree. `arrives` is no longer declared: a declaration naming it is refused in words saying what decides it. Nothing of an unknown dataset, of a root, of a folder holding a place's folder, or of any `derivatives/dcm-original` is digested, brought in or pseudonymised, and the refusal says why. The digest's walk never goes into a `derivatives/dcm-original` below the folder it reads.
- An unknown dataset's entries holding DICOM go into the tree a person names, and only on the person's word: `--move-into originals|anon` with `--confirm-move` on `nils place add` and `nils place set`, or `move_into` with `confirm_move: true` on `POST /api/places` and `PUT /api/places/{id}`; without the word the answer is a 409 carrying `layout` and `confirm: "confirm_move"`, and nothing is written. Then the structure says what the dataset is. Entries holding no DICOM stay beside `derivatives/` and are not read. `--move-into` on a folder of loose DICOM makes it one dataset.
- Registry schema 80: a source place without a whole, current declaration is undeclared until its folder is explored again: one `nils setup` made with no dataset, one the old default had read its folder itself, and an anonymised one that does not say what PatientID holds and how its subjects are found. A place that names a dataset's `derivatives/dcm-raw` or `dcm-anon` is `legacy`. Each keeps its rule, cohort and tag lists. No file is touched. Schema 83 takes the same step again for a store a development build migrated under schema 80's earlier form; on any other it changes nothing. Schema 84 removes the datasets a development build made of every folder under a root that nothing has read (no digest of its tree, nothing pseudonymised); their folders are plain folders again.
- A personnummer is never kept, not even sealed (Nima's ruling of 2026-10-08: a personnummer is saved nowhere in the registry or the audit): its identity is its keyed lookup alone, a held file's row keeps its shape and lookup, showing a subject's identifiers says the subject's code stands for it, and a map with a personnummer column is imported at the imports door from the request itself, never written to a file for a job. Registry schema 82 drops what a store from before sealed of one.
- Anonymised data must say what its PatientID holds (`patient_id`) and how its subjects are found (`subjects`): `map`, through a map of subject codes to its ids, given or already in the registry, a file whose id no map names held; or `generated`, the subject code generator making each code from the id as from a personnummer, every subject a subject, never provisional. It is not read before it says. An identified dataset's tree is read only as the pseudonymiser resolved it: a value there no subject holds is held, never made a subject.
- "Code them anyway" (`POST /api/linkage/held/code`) queues the run that codes the held files and answers with its job, `{place, files, job, state, command}` (202): the pseudonymise of the held files of an identified dataset, the digest of a dataset read in place. A second press with nothing new to code queues nothing and answers `job: null`. A file held for want of its subject's id type value (below) is not coded anyway.
- Three ways to name stacks, chosen per release (record 55 C4, Nima's ruling of 2026-10-08): the descriptive layout, v0's grammar as before, and two BIDS naming styles, `--naming full|minimal` (the release form's naming choice), which replace the `bids` and `informative` pair (a release recorded under either re-runs as `full`). In both BIDS styles the suffix is the base contrast, or a construct suffix BIDS defines (`ADC`, `FA`, `trace`, `T1map`, `PDmap`, `UNIT1`, `MP2RAGE`, `MEGRE`, `angio`, `asl`); `FLAIR`, like STIR, DIR, PSIR and FatSat, is always a modifier in `acq-` and never the suffix, so a T2 FLAIR is `acq-...+FLAIR+..._T2w` and a T1 FLAIR `..._T1w`. What has an entity goes to it: `ce-`, `rec-` (the provenance, and a Dixon image as `rec-water`, `rec-fat`, `rec-inphase` or `rec-outphase`), `part-`, `echo-`, `inv-`. **Full**, the default, spells every axis without an entity in `acq-` in v0's slot order, joined by `+` (body part with the brain implicit and the spine `SC`, orientation, 2D or 3D, modifiers, technique, construct): `acq-BrainAx2DIRTSEFLAIR_FLAIR` is now `acq-Ax+2D+FLAIR+IRTSE_T2w`. **Minimal** spells only 2D or 3D, the modifiers and the technique, always: `acq-2D+FLAIR+IRTSE_T2w`. A token that is the suffix itself is not repeated (`MEGRE`, `ASL`); `TOFMRA` and `PCMRA` stay on `angio`. A scout or a working scan, which never takes its BIDS name, no longer takes part in a name conflict, and a derivative shares names only with derivatives. A re-run of an existing BIDS release renames its files.
- A pipeline finds a FLAIR by the token those names give it. A bids input names each session's FLAIR pick a T2w with `FLAIR` among the `+` tokens of its `acq-`, so the pre-flight and the run count a `flair` pick by that token, as well as by a `_FLAIR` suffix, and the segcsvd and SAMSEG with lesions starters take the T2w whose `acq-` holds it, or the `_FLAIR` of a tree an older release wrote. segcsvd names the hyperintensities for the FLAIR's name without its suffix (`..._acq-Ax+2D+FLAIR+IRTSE_label-WMH_desc-segcsvd_mask.nii.gz`). The engine adds the two starters' new versions when it starts. A site's own descriptor that looks for `*_FLAIR.nii.gz` finds no FLAIR in a new release, as an older version of either starter does (segcsvd's then makes the perivascular spaces alone): look for the token instead, as the starters now do.
- Raw BIDS keeps only what the standard admits among derived images (after the 2026-10-08 naming research): MIP, MinIP, MPR and standard-deviation projections, every reformat, the ASL and DSC perfusion maps (CBF, CBV, MTT, Tmax, TTP, PBP, K2) go to `derivatives/nils/` under the name the raw tree would give them plus `desc-<construct>` (`acq-Ax+3D+TOFMRA_desc-MIP_angio`), or, where the standard has no name, `desc-<descriptive name>_<base>`. The MRI pack's BIDS mapping lists these constructs under `derivatives`.
- SyMRI is anatomical and stays together (record 55 C4, Nima's ruling of 2026-10-09, as v0 kept it): its quantitative maps and synthetic contrasts are NIfTI under `sub-*/ses-*/anat/SyMRI/` with their BIDS names (`anat/SyMRI/sub-01_ses-01_acq-Ax+2D+MDME_rec-SyMRI_T1w.nii.gz`), and its multi-dynamic multi-echo or QALAS acquisition, which is not an image to convert, is DICOM in SyMRI's folder of the DICOM export. `.bidsignore` lists the folder. `--synthetic folder|anat|derivatives` defaults to `folder`; `anat` and `derivatives` place the synthetic contrasts as before, without a folder. The MRI pack's BIDS mapping names the folder and what goes in it under `folders`.
- A BIDS release also writes a DICOM export inside the BIDS folder, as v0's DICOM tree did: each converted stack's de-identified slices under `sourcedata/dicom/`, at the path its NIfTI has, in a folder named after the NIfTI file without its extension (`sourcedata/dicom/sub-01/ses-01/anat/SyMRI/sub-01_ses-01_acq-Ax+2D+MDME_rec-SyMRI_T1w/00000001.dcm`; a derivative's without `derivatives/nils/`). `--dicom all|folders|none` chooses which stacks: all converted stacks (the default, as v0 exported every stack), only the stacks in a folder of their own (SyMRI), or none. The export moves with its file when a re-run renames it, and a handover carries it. `sourcedata/` is where BIDS keeps data before conversion, so the validator does not read it. Releases are larger by the DICOM of every converted stack unless `--dicom` says otherwise. Every DICOM a BIDS release writes is now under `sourcedata/dicom/`: scouts, working scans and stacks the converter refused move from `sourcedata/sub-*/ses-*/<folder>/<name>/` to `sourcedata/dicom/sub-*/ses-*/<folder>/<name>/`, so `sourcedata/` can hold other kinds of source later; a re-run of an existing release moves them.
- Every BIDS sidecar carries a `NILS` object: every classification axis with the tier and confidence that decided it, the descriptive name, and the acquisition as the registry measured it (manufacturer and model, field strength, TR, TE, TI, flip angle, slice thickness and spacing, pixel spacing, voxel size, matrix, field of view, number of slices and images, b values and directions, echo train, averages, bandwidth). No station, coil name or free text. Every `scans.tsv` gains a `nils_name` column with the descriptive name, described in a root `scans.json`.
- A BIDS release is never refused for a name conflict. Where two or more stacks of one session build one BIDS name and are not one acquisition made again, each now carries what separates it instead of going to `sourcedata/`: the value of a classification axis where they differ on one, in that axis's own slot, otherwise the measured property and its value (the slice thickness, the repetition time, the matrix and so on) after the pack's slots, and as the last fallback a plain number, last, which is never a `run-`. In a BIDS name all of it is in `acq-` (`acq-Ax+2D+FLAIR+IRTSE+3mm_T2w` in the full style, `acq-2D+FLAIR+IRTSE+3mm_T2w` in the minimal one); in the descriptive layout it is a `_` slot of its own before v0's counter (`Ax_T2w_2D_FLAIR_TIRM_ND_3mm`). True repeats keep `run-` as before. No text, such as a protocol name or a series description, ever makes a name. The release's report lists every BIDS name a difference or a number decided, with the property and the values, and the `README` says how many there are. A `release.shared_name` review item is raised only where the stacks differ in nothing the engine can see (no fingerprint, or one moment written twice) and are not a repeat.
- The MRI pack 1.1.0 is the first rules release that ships on its own (record 55 B5, Nima's decision of 2026-10-10): it declares pack contract 10 and the engines it works with, `engine: ">=1.0.0-alpha.79, <2.0.0"`. An engine before contract 10 refuses it by its contract, so it reaches an install with the first engine that reads rules releases, and every rules release after it reaches that install with no engine release. Released under the tag `pack-mri-v1.1.0` by `rules-release.yml`.
- The MRI pack is 1.1.0 (record 55 C3, the ruling of 2026-10-08; a minor version, since adding to the vocabulary is a minor and removing or renaming a major, the rule as amended on 2026-10-10): a SyMRI map, segmentation or synthetic image the viewer saved in colour (PhotometricInterpretation RGB or a YBR form, or three samples a pixel) is a display composite, a picture made to be looked at. Until now it was read as a screenshot: excluded, every axis empty, and no ask could find it. It now has a value of its own on the axis that says what it is and on the one that says what to do with it, as a scout and a derived map have: construct `DisplayComposite`, with no base and no modifier, the technique of its SyMRI acquisition (MDME or QALAS), directory type anat and the provenance, body part, post-contrast and quality its own rules read; and disposition `display_composite`, which no role takes. An ask finds it by either value, and nobody is asked about one, as before. Corpus cases: `packs/mri/corpus/axes-110.yml`. After updating, a reclassify applies the pack; nothing needs to be read again, since the fields it reads are the fingerprint's of revision 7. The rule for vessel pictures made from a TWIST-VIBE run in the same session waits for a real case, and the pack says so where it reads those runs.
- A release leaves display composites out by default, as it leaves out what the pack ruled out, and takes them when its selection names their disposition: `--disposition display_composite`, alone or beside the others, or an axis item `disposition=display_composite` (`--axis`, a door's `axes`), which now names the dispositions a release takes as `--disposition` does instead of narrowing the default. Its report counts what the default left out, by disposition (`left_out_unless_asked`, `not asked for` at the command line), so a composite missing from a tree is never a silent absence. `nils select` and `POST /api/select` count by the same rule.

### Added

- No command line inside a desk flow for a pipeline or a session scheme (record 55 H2). `POST /api/pipelines` adds a pipeline from its descriptor's text, as `nils pipeline add --json` does and with its checks, under install:work since it brings a container image onto the site; `GET` and `PUT /api/pipelines/lane` read and set the lane's cores, memory, card and places; `GET /api/pipelines/secrets` lists each secret the pipelines declare and whether it is set, never its bytes, and `PUT` and `DELETE /api/pipelines/secrets/{id}` keep a secret's text (a licence) as a file of the engine's own, mode 0600 under its home, or forget it. `GET` and `POST /api/session-schemes` list the kept session schemes with the default and keep a new one, as `nils session scheme add` does, under release:see and release:work, audited as `session.scheme` and never replacing one. The command line and the doors share one code path for each. Contract v7 amended in place, additively.
- Scoring at arrival and the slice cache (record 55 E5, spec section 10.4; gate test T26). A bring-in ends with a last step, `nils score @dataset --name <batch>`, which queues the body-part step's run, then post-contrast's once the body part's run ends done, over the stacks the batch brought, with the model the site chose for each task, where its pipeline declares `x-nils.on-arrival` (`true`, or `{params: {...}}` for such a run's parameters). The runs go to the pipeline lane under the grants and detail the bring-in was queued with; a step that cannot run is skipped and the score's result says why (`not_on_arrival`, `no_pipeline`, `no_model`, `no_runtime`, `no_place`, `requirements`, `no_grant`), and a bring-in that read nothing new queues no score. A pipeline that declares `x-nils.cache` is given the working place's `slice-cache/` at `/cache` (`[Cache]`) and a `stacks.json` naming each file's SOP Instance UID and, where known, the sha256 of its bytes, learnt when a run reports reading the file whole (`read` in `results.json`, ruling R3: no pass over the archive) and kept while the file keeps its size and time (`file_content`, migration 97). An input version no admitted model reads is dropped 30 days after it went unread (ruling R4), after each run and with `nils cache prune`; `nils cache show` reads the cache. The job contract v1 is amended in place, additively. `pipelines/nils-bodypart` 0.6.0 reads the cache before it reads a file: the student's slice, and each planes stage's planes found by its stack's key and checked against each file's sha256, the encoder reading the cached planes again only where another encoder made them; at arrival it fills the touched planes (ruling R5), so a head or calibration update re-scores from the cache with no file read and the same answers a read gives.
- System 1 runs beside the rules as a pipeline (record 55 D3 with record 56; spec 11.1). `pipelines/nils-system1` serves a site's System 1 model, the one chosen for the task `system1`, from one bundle file whose `system1.json` names every file by sha256 (code it brings runs only with `trust_code` true, after its bytes match); `system1-score` reads the registry's rows a header model needs (`x-nils.input.rows`: each stack's whole fingerprint row and votes, each series's frame of reference as a digest and private elements, for the run's stacks and the other MR stacks of their sessions) and gives per stack and axis its answer, its calibrated probability, the rules' reliability on the header and the fields that mattered, each measured by leaving it out (`results.json` `opinions`, `contracts/job/v1/opinions.schema.json`, declared by `x-nils.opinions`). The engine keeps them as a second opinion (`opinion`), never a decision or a review item. It runs over a dataset's or a cohort's scans at the steps door (`system1`) and over every scan at `POST /api/review/system1/run`. A model answers in its own vocabulary and says, per opinion, how it read the rules' answer (`rules_read`): the gate and the suggestions compare its answer with that reading, the one its certificate measured agreement with, so a vendor's name the pack stores (SPACE, T2*w) agrees with the model's word for it (3D-TSE, T2starw). A model card says its review gate thresholds per axis (`gate.axes.<axis>.lambda1`, `lambda2`).
- The review rule's rounds and gate (record 55 D3, record 56 section 3; spec 11.2). A round is a pair of a rules version and the chosen System 1 model, with the card's thresholds frozen with it; `GET /api/review/rounds` reports each round's scans, the accepted and review shares, review by kind, the zero counts of what is never asked (a rule conflict, a low confidence, body part, post-contrast), the gate's outcomes, System 1's coverage, the answers given blind and not, and its audits. On a dataset flagged as having damaged headers (`GET`/`PUT /api/datasets/{name}/headers`: a person's flag with why, else the signs, at least half of its MR scans with neither a series description nor a protocol name), the gate accepts the rules' answer only where System 1 agrees with probability at least lambda1 and reliability at least lambda2, and asks `<axis>:disagree` or `<axis>:unsure` about the rest; it raises nothing until a recorded certificate names the model, and runs after every System 1 run and at `POST /api/review/gate`.
- Both suggestions and both reasons, and blind first (record 55 D6; spec 11.3, 11.4). `GET /api/review/{id}/suggestions` shows per stack and axis the rules' answer with the rule, the clause, the fields read and the text matched, and System 1's with its probability, reliability, the fields that mattered and up to five similar settled stacks, never of a sealed sample and shown for display only, marked not confirmed until a certificate names the model. Every question about an axis carries a mode drawn once from a site share (`PUT /api/settings` `review.blind_share`, one in five by default): a blind-first question is read without NILS's answers until the person's provisional answer (`POST /api/review/{id}/provisional`), and the suggestions door refuses before it. Every answer records whether it was given blind, the seconds it took and the mode (`decision.blind`, `seconds`, `mode`; `campaign_answer.blind`); only blind answers become training labels, so the training labels leave out decisions given after the suggestions showed, and a label set of a campaign with an answer that was not blind is not training data. A campaign says its `purpose` (decide, train, measure, audit); one that trains or measures is read blind first always, shows a rater an item's suggestion only after their answer, and serves no batch or gallery.
- The blind audit of accepted scans (record 55 D7 and its ruling 13; spec 11.5). `POST /api/audit/draws` draws a seeded share (1 % by default) of a round's accepted stacks by the sha256 of the seed and each stack's DICOM identity, records the round, the seed, the frame's size and digest and the rules' answer each drawn stack was accepted with, and makes the audit campaign that reads them blind (purpose audit, suggesting none, closing into none, its reads never training); its raters are agents outside the engine through the campaign doors (`docs/guides/review-rule-and-audit.md`). `POST /api/audit/draws/{id}/grade` computes the accepted error from the settled answers with its exact one-sided Clopper-Pearson bound at 95 %, kept beside the round. Registry schema 98.

- The gate's T4 and T7 in CI (Wave 7a section 17). T4 for `nils setup`: a site's source place over a folder of loose DICOM beside a study's `derivatives/dcm-original` is read by nothing before the person's answer; a digest of the folder, its parts and the originals is refused, and the registry holds 0 files, no batch and no job. T4 for `POST /api/places`: no batch and no job between the first look and the adds of a root and its unknown folders. T7 end to end: every file of a pseudonymised dataset carries the four marks with the pseudonymiser's options, and every file of a descriptive release of it carries them with the release's own, one option list for the release.
- The logs of jobs and of a run's units are read at the doors (record 55 H2, round 5, A23). The worker now writes what a job's verb prints, both streams, to `logs/jobs/<id>.log` beside the registry, cut past 32 MiB with a line that says so, and `nils jobs prune` removes the logs of the jobs it prunes. `GET /api/jobs/{id}/log` answers a slice of it, `{job, state, size, from, to, text, earlier, over}`: the last 64 KiB from the start of a line by default, or from the byte `from` names (`limit` bytes, 64 KiB by default and 1 MiB at most, a page that stops short ending after a whole line), so a page following a running job asks only for what is new. It is read by whoever may run the job's verb, at the verb's detail and never below quasi, since a verb prints what it reads; a job with no log says why (not started, run on another machine whose disk keeps it, or run before logs were kept or from a terminal). `GET /api/pipeline-runs/{id}/units/{unit}/log` answers a unit's `log.txt` from the run's scratch place the same way, `{run, unit, shared, size, from, to, text, earlier, over}`, `shared` where the run's units ran together and its one log is answered, and `GET /api/pipeline-runs/{id}/log` that one log; both at pipelines:see and detail quasi, and a log with the words of a unit holding a stack of a sample sealed now only with `sealed:see`, that read audited as `sealed.read`. The spec named the unit door `/api/runs/...`; it sits with the runs at `/api/pipeline-runs`. Contract v7 amended in place, additively.
- The post-contrast label (record 55 F2 and F5, spec §12.3): every MR stack carries a post-contrast state (`pre`, `post`, `by_definition` or `undetermined`), the minutes since injection and what they are counted from (the bolus start where a DSC, DCE or contrast MRA series comes before the first post T1, else the first post T1's first image, as ruled on 2026-10-11), where the label came from (`t1_anchor_time_rule`, `header`, `image` or `reader`), the 72-hour flag, and for an ASL stack whether it was acquired after contrast (`contrast_label`, migration 95). The time rule runs as a session pass over a study's clock, its bracket set by the last pre and the first post T1 whose answer is certified (the image model at or above its card's threshold, or a person's read), and its checks run as code with a reason code each against the time-label study's eight failure modes: an earlier injection within 72 hours (`C1_prior_72h`, in a subject-level step after the session pass), two exams in one session (`C2_exam`), a bolus before the last pre T1 (`C3_bolus_before_L`, and the stacks after a bolus are post), a split dose (`C4_split`, a flag), clock errors (`C5_midnight`, `C5_no_time`, `C5_content`, `C5_straddle`, `C5_sn_guard`), derived series (`C6_derived`), sessions without a usable T1 (`C7_no_t1`, `C7_post_only_before_F`, never `pre`) and a post T1 that rests on one model answer alone (`C8_weak_F`, a flag). DSC, DCE and contrast MRA are contrast by definition; PSIR, vessel-wall T1 and PD stacks count as the T1 family. A classification labels every session at its end, and so do a run that proposes the post-contrast axis, a campaign close and a person's decision on it; `nils contrast label` labels again and `nils contrast show <stack>` reads one. A stack's evidence line (`GET /api/stacks/{id}/why`) carries its label, and the post-contrast step of a dataset or a cohort counts the labels by state. The rule's numbers are the time-label study's registered ones (`nils_pack::contrast::Config`).
