- The full post-contrast pipeline, `postcontrast-full` (record 55 E3, spec 12.2): model r1 itself on the T1-type brain stacks the cascade defers, `nils-postcontrast full` in its own image (`pipelines/nils-postcontrast/Dockerfile.full`, FreeSurfer 8.2.0's image pinned by digest with the card's runtimes beside it). Per stack dcm2niix (retried once), SynthMorph's affine carrying the template and venous regions, SynthSeg, SynthStrip, the regions' and structures' intensities with a Frangi vessel filter, the stripped stack's intensity tail without N4, the three frozen arms (a new model part, `arms`, with the atlases they were fitted on) and model r1's fusion with the header evidence. With a card the tools run in waves on it, several SynthSeg processes each held to a share; a card's failed segmentation is tried once more on the card, the CPU's tools run at most one SynthSeg per 96 GB of the memory given, and a worker that dies has its pool made again and its stacks given again, so one stack never ends the run. The image carries third-party components whose terms are not ours to pass on, so a site builds it and it is never published.
- Post-contrast served (record 55 E3): the post-contrast step runs `postcontrast-cascade` given the promoted post-contrast cascade and its parts, each model input filled by the part of its name, and a run of the cascade queues `postcontrast-full` over the stacks its table says it deferred, where that pipeline is installed, under the grants of the cascade's job. A descriptor may ask for the session facts the post-contrast model reads (`x-nils.input.contrast`, with the header and the geometry): each stack and context entry of `stacks.json` carries its series' contrast tags, its clock, its physics and the post-contrast rules that fired, each context entry its series and header, and each header the datasets holding a file of the stack (the job contract v1, amended in place, additively).
- Models as parts, chosen per task (record 55 D4, E6, A5; spec 10.2, 10.3, 10.5; the wave's tests T22 and T23). A model's card gains `tested` (each claim with its value, its bound, its count and the evidence run it comes from), `speed` (stacks a second on a CPU and with a GPU, each with the hardware and the evidence run), `gpu` (none, optional or required) and `equivalence` (the class, I, II or III, of the difference between its CPU and GPU answers, with its tolerance, the set and the evidence run); the model contract v1 is amended additively, and every model answers `complete` and `missing`, what its card lacks of the label set it was trained on, its tested claims, its speeds, its GPU need, its equivalence, its intended use and its limits. Several admitted or promoted models now answer one task and slot, and the one that answers is the chosen one: a site's admin chooses it (`POST /api/models/choose`, `nils model choose`, audited as `model.choose` and kept on the models' history as `chosen` and `unchosen`), and where nobody chose, the model the newest recorded certificate names, else the one promoted last, else the newest admitted (a cascade only once chosen, certified or promoted). `GET /api/models/tasks` and `nils model chosen` list each task and slot with its models and the one chosen and why, and `GET /api/models` says which model is chosen. Body part's step runs the chosen model. The admission suite measures the CPU and GPU equivalence from two runs of a fixed set (`nils model equivalence MODEL --cpu run:ID|results.json --gpu ...`, and `nils model admit --cpu --gpu`, which keeps it with the check), each answer read at its model's threshold: class I where no answer and no most probable value changed and the probabilities stay within the card's tolerance (0.001 by default), II within the tolerance of a class II card, III where every change is between an answer and not sure, and none where a value turned into another. A model whose card's `gpu` is optional or required is admitted only with a measured class no worse than its card's, on its own file. A label set made outside the engine is registered (`POST /api/label-sets/register`, `nils labels register DIR`) with its labels.tsv, its provenance and the sha256 of every file: its rows are keyed by DICOM identity (study, series, stack key and SOP Instance UID), never by registry ids, it is identified by its name, version and the digest of its canonical labels.tsv, a set with a row of a sample sealed now (by DICOM identity) is refused, and a card's `trained_on` may name it; such a set is held to later seals as a written one is. A schema migration adds `model_choice`. The OpenAPI 7 contract is amended in place, additively.

- The create door draws a pair, an anchored and an A/B campaign's items from saved selections (record 55 H3, nothing typed by hand), where they were made only at the keyboard from files. A pair source takes a selection and, optionally, a second one: every two stacks of one session, or each stack of the first beside each of the second in its session. An anchored source takes the candidates, the pre anchors and the post anchors as three selections: each candidate's nearest anchor by series number in its own session, else the subject's nearest session's, flagged. An A/B source takes a selection, the voters' readings in the body and the rules as a voter; the rules vote on a stack of a sample sealed now only for a holder of `sealed:see`, audited as `sealed.read`. `POST /api/campaigns/preview` answers each one's counts and makes nothing, and the capabilities' `campaigns.draws` names the three. The door and `nils campaign pair | anchored | ab` make them through one function each, so they check, draw and audit alike.
- Browse's doors (record 55 H3): `GET /api/subjects`, `GET /api/subjects/{id}/sessions`, `GET /api/sessions/{id}/stacks` and `GET /api/scans`, the whole registry's subjects, one subject's sessions, one session's stacks and every scan a page at a time, answered as the dataset viewer's doors answer a dataset's, with the same filters, shaping below detail quasi and sealed rule, under `data:see`.
- A cohort's picks for its heatmap (record 55 H3): `GET /api/cohorts/{name}/picks` answers a page of the cohort's members, each session the cache holds and, per role the pack's picks declare, whether it is picked, doubted (an open `pick.border` item) or missing, with the stack and who picked it, read through the one pick resolver so it agrees with the Main scans map, and totals per role over the whole cohort.
- A run reads as what its units made, the pre-flight says which units cannot run and why, and a run taken up again is checked as a new run is (2026-10-12, found trying the desk on a laptop install: a run of samseg-lesions over T1 scans alone, taken up again with Run again once dcm2niix was installed, skipped all four of its sessions for want of a FLAIR and read as done; Nima: "done-fail should be per unit of job not the whole thing"). A run, its listing and its job answer `made`, its units summed (made, failed, skipped, left), each reason with its count in a few words, whether it made nothing, and the line a person reads beside its status, `3 of 4 made · 1 skipped: no FLAIR picked` or `nothing made · 4 of 4 skipped: no FLAIR picked`, which `nils pipeline runs` and `nils jobs show` print; a run's summary keeps `skipped_by_why`, a run from before has it read from its units, and each unit answers `lacks`. The pre-flight answers `cannot`, the units that cannot run one entry a reason in a few words with the units under it, and `lacks` on each of `missing`; a run none of whose units could run is not ready, and `nils run` and the jobs door refuse it before anything is made or queued, with why (failure `input`). A run taken up again is checked for the units it still has to run, of the bids input it released before where it did, the units it is over with kept: the pre-flight takes `{resume: <run>}` and `nils run --resume <run> --preflight`, and `nils run --resume` and the jobs door refuse one whose checks fail (the converter only where its input is still to be released), one that could run none of its units and made nothing, and one that cannot be taken up, where the door queued every resume unchecked before; the containers its engine left running are stopped before it is checked, as taking it up always did first, and a run whose input release's kept picks are gone (a release from before) is refused saying so, rather than taken up to skip every unit. A pipeline job whose run a later job took up again answers `taken_up` {job, state, run}, and the pipelines capability says `preflight.resume`.
- A run over a dataset's or a cohort's main scans takes every role its pipeline needs (2026-10-12). `--select main@dataset:<name>` (or `main@cohort:<name>`), written without a role, resolves to the main scans of each role the pipeline's descriptor names (`x-nils.input.roles`: samseg-lesions and segcsvd need a T1w and a FLAIR), one pick a visit a role, and takes a visit only where it has a main scan of every one; `main:<role>@...` is as it was, a pipeline of one role takes its role this way too, and one that names none refuses the spec with the form that names one. The list is frozen into one handle whose entry records each role's own stacks, so the run's bids input and its units name each stack by the role it was taken for, where a list of one role took every stack as that role; the input's release keeps each stack of the list as that role (`release_pick`) and records the roles (`pick_rules.main` "t1w+flair", `roles`), and its history says "main t1w and flair scans". The pre-flight over a scope with no main scan of the role (a dataset with no picks) says there is nothing to run over and counts its visits by why, where it panicked; and of picked stacks the registry holds no file of, it says that ("the release of the input would hold no subject's images: 4 of the selection's 4 stacks: the registry holds no file of it to read"), where it said none was picked. The pre-flight's `main` names the roles, counts the visits with every one and those that lack one by role and by why, names them at detail quasi with their role, and gives each role's own count; a run's `main` says the same. `--chunk` splits such a list by whole visits.
- The catalog says when each cohort was made (2026-10-12, found by the chat bench: the catalog listed cohorts by name and size only, so "which cohort was made third" had no answer). Each cohort of the catalog's `cohorts` (`GET /api/ask/catalog`, which the chat's search reads) carries `created_at`, and the cohort level carries the fields `created_at` and `retired_at`, null while a cohort is in use, so a question at the cohort grain orders the cohorts by when they were made. Each is `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ` on either backend, technical at every detail and never federated, since a cohort's dates are its own and not a subject's. A retired cohort still leaves the catalog's list (record 26) and is read with its retired date at the cohort grain; `GET /api/cohorts` said both dates already. The OpenAPI 7 contract is amended in place, additively.
- A release keeps the main scans it read (record 55, rulings of 2026-10-12, item 6, carried to releases). A scope's newer rules replace the picks of the version before as their run ends, and the pack's run makes its own again, so whatever read "the picks of the version a release was made with" after it answered another list, or none: a run taken up again over its input skipped every unit as having no T1w picked, a mask's grid by its output's role was found on no scan, and a check's panels lost the visit's main scans. A release of picked stacks now keeps what it read before it takes a stack: of each visit it takes a stack of, every pick of what it read (a dataset's or a cohort's rules at their version, each subject's own dataset's, or the pack's defaults), with every stack the pick names and the scope and version of the rules it was made with, one row a visit, role and stack in `release_pick` (registry schema 94); and it takes its stacks by that list. A pipeline run's input released from a frozen list of a scope's main scans also keeps each stack of the list as the main scan of its role, whatever the picks say by then. Every reader of a release's picks after it was made reads what it kept: a run's units over its input (as a run taken up again with `--resume` judges them), a mask's grid by its output's role (`nils derivative parts`, a layer's build) and a check's panels. `GET /api/releases` and `nils release --history` say what each release read and kept (`picks`, with `frozen: {visits, picks, stacks}`). A release from before reads the live picks of what it recorded while they name the stacks it holds; once they do not, or a newer version of the rules it read has replaced them, a unit says which of its stacks stands for a role is not known and why, a check's preview counts its results under `panels_gone` with why and shows their made-on scan alone, and a mask's grid is the input whose grid its file's matches. A subject merge re-points what a release kept, and an empty stack a release kept a pick of is kept. The OpenAPI 7 contract is amended in place, additively.
- SAMSEG with lesions and segcsvd check that each session's FLAIR sits on its T1w (the review of the starters' settings, 2026-10-12). Both place the FLAIR on the T1w by the image headers alone, with no registration, and both tools expect their inputs aligned. Until NILS has a registration step, each unit with a FLAIR compares a brain mask of each image on the T1w's grid, inside the FLAIR's field of view, and writes their Dice to its table as `flair_t1_brain_dice`: SAMSEG's from `mri_synthstrip` on both images, segcsvd's from the T1w's SynthSeg labels and a threshold mask of the FLAIR, since its image has no skull stripper. The check `flair_t1_brain_dice >= 0.85` (a NILS policy) flags a session below it with a `pipeline:qc` item, and never fails its unit; the unit's log says "FLAIR and T1 may not be aligned (brain masks agree at 0.xx)". On three open sessions (OpenNeuro ds005602 and ds004884) the masks agreed at 0.91 to 0.99, and with the FLAIR moved 15 mm at 0.77 to 0.86. The engine adds the two starters' new versions when it starts.
- A run goes straight over a dataset's or a cohort's main scans of a role (record 55, rulings of 2026-10-12, item 6: "run SynthSeg on study-anon's main T1 scans", without making a selection first). `nils run <pipeline> --select main:<role>@dataset:<name>` (or `main:<role>@cohort:<name>`), the pre-flight door's `select` and a run queued at the jobs door take it beside `selection:<name>@<v>` and a handle. The role is one the pack's picks name (t1w, flair, t2w). The list is resolved when the run is made, through the one resolver of the main scans: of the scope's scans (a dataset's, the stacks its folder holds a file of; a cohort's, every stack of its open members), those the live pick of their visit names, the scope's own picks once a run of its saved rules finished, else the pack's, one pick a visit with every stack it names. It is frozen into a handle as a selection is, and the handle records where it came from: the spec, the scope, and the picks read with the rules' version, digest and run, or the pack's defaults. A run's bids input is released from the list as it was frozen, each stack the main scan of its role, so a run over a list frozen before the scope's rules changed takes the same stacks after (a scope's newer rules replace the picks of the version before); the input release records the picks the list was read under. The pre-flight answers `main`: what was read, the visits, those with a main scan, and the visits with none counted by why and named (it holds no scan of the role; it holds one and none is picked; a person said none stands for it; of a dataset, its main scan is one only another dataset's folder holds), and a run says it as `main` too. A campaign or a label set is still made from a saved selection. The OpenAPI 7 contract is amended in place, additively.
- The stations think on a model server, and `nils setup --model-thinking on|off` says whether (2026-10-12, Nima's direction of 2026-10-01: "run local models with maximum thinking mode"). The assistant's one-chat bench measured qwen3.8-flash-next with its thinking on at 79 % pass^3 against 71 % with thinking off and a check for words before tools, with no words before its tools at all, for about 1.3 seconds a turn at the median and 8 at the 90th percentile. A backend setup gave Kvasir for a model server named no thinking, so a model that reasons thought as its server's default had it, with the longest answer the server lists, and nothing could turn that off. Now `nils setup --model-server` asks Kvasir for thinking `on` by default, which Kvasir sends as the chat template's `enable_thinking` to every model of the server that reasons, or `off` with `--model-thinking off`, and holds the stations' model with answers of up to 16,384 tokens, the thinking included, as the bench did, never more than the server allows. The setup record keeps `thinking` under `[model_server]`; a record from before reads as on. `nils setup --update` and a repair send it again, and `--model-thinking` with `--update` alone changes the server on record. Kvasir holds a server it holds already again in place, its other models, key and admission records kept, without asking any model, so an update loads nothing on the server. Setup says what Kvasir holds (the stations think before they answer, with up to 16,384 tokens an answer, and what the server allows), and where a Kvasir from before per-backend thinking takes neither, that the stations think as the server has it by default.
- A failure says why, and a run is checked before it is queued (2026-10-12, Nima: "if the failure is package dependency or resource then it should let user know"). A run, each of its units and its job answer `failure` {kind, cause, advice, fix} beside their error, which is kept as it was written: `dependency` (dcm2niix, the container runtime, the subordinate ids rootless podman unpacks an image with, an image that will not pull, the GPU driver), `resource` (memory, a container killed with 137; disk; a card's memory; the lane; time), `input` or `pipeline`, with one sentence of what to do; a run's summary counts its units' failures by cause (registry schema 93). The pre-flight answers `requirements` (the converter, the runtime and its image, memory, a GPU, and the disk a unit's outputs take where the descriptor says, `x-nils.needs.disk-gb`), and `nils run`, the jobs door and a step's run door refuse a run a check fails, by its kind, before anything is queued. dcm2niix is provided: the engine looks for it beside the running `nils` and in the registry's `tools/` folder as well as on its search path, `nils setup` places the pinned build (rordenlab v1.0.20260724, its sha256 checked) beside `nils` on an install on this machine or takes a site's own with `--dcm2niix`, and an admin installs it with `nils tools install dcm2niix` or `POST /api/tools/dcm2niix/install` (install:work, audited); `GET /api/tools` and `capabilities.tools` say whether it is here.
- A site starts again from an empty registry under the same setup (Wave 7a §15.3, slice A27). `nils setup --new-registry` stops the engine, sets the registry aside without deleting it (on Postgres its schemas are renamed `<schema>_before_<stamp>` and `<schema>_before_<stamp>_linkage`; its `nils.toml` and key store, and on SQLite its databases, move into `before-new-registry-<stamp>/` in the registry's folder, where it still opens), makes a new registry on the key `--reg-key-file` or `--key-file` gives under the subject code generator's scheme, declares the setup record's places, carries the timezone and week start, and writes and starts every part again; the configuration, the desk's store and Kvasir's state are kept. `nils place export` writes every place in force, a dataset with its declaration, to one file, and `nils place import` declares them in another registry as written, without looking into a folder, moving or probing; a place already there by name, role and path is kept, `--skip` leaves one out, and a place it cannot take stops the whole import before anything is declared. On an install of the machine's services the registry is set aside and made as the engine's account.
- A cascade is one model that names its parts (record 55 E2, the ruling of 2026-10-10): its card's `parts` names, under the pipeline input each fills (`encoder`, `head`, `coarse`, `student`, `deferral`), a registered model by digest, none retired, and promoting the cascade retires none of its parts, nor a part's promotion a cascade that reads it. A promotion or a retirement that would take a part from an admitted or promoted cascade is refused, naming the cascade, and a cascade with a retired part is neither admitted nor promoted. A promotion names every model it retired: `nils model promote` prints each, and its `--json` and the promote door add `retired_models`, the list, beside `retired`, the first (the HTTP contract v7 amended in place, additively). Body part's step takes a cascade once a person has promoted it (an admitted cascade waits, and the promoted or admitted single model answers until then), and gives its run each part in the descriptor's order, the coarse part its card names and no other mode (a cascade naming none is refused, `no_model`), and the cascade itself as the input `cascade`; a plain head leaves those optional inputs out. At plan time a cascade is refused (`no_model`, with the reason) unless every part it names is registered, not retired, and admitted or promoted, and its parts fill every model input of the descriptor but `cascade`: a cascade is never given as its own head or encoder. A descriptor that takes no `cascade` input, or not each of the cascade's parts (one older than the cascade), is refused for it (`no_pipeline`), never run on the parts alone. The model contract v1 is amended in place, additively.
- A stack's geometry in `stacks.json` also carries the facts body part's few-slice student reads (record 55 E2, P1b): `study`, an id to group a session's stacks by, `image_type`, the fingerprint's `sop_class_uid`, `n_instances`, and `sealed`, whether the stack is in a sample sealed now, so an image leaves it out of its session's top and answers it as sealed. The job contract v1 is amended in place, additively.
- A pipeline of the stacks layout may ask for each file's geometry (record 55 E2): with `x-nils.input.geometry: true` each stack's entry of `stacks.json` carries `geometry`, the orientation of its images (`image_orientation_patient`, six numbers) and its fingerprint's `slice_thickness` and `spacing_between_slices`, and each of its files their own `geometry`: `image_position_patient` (three numbers), `pixel_spacing` (two), `rows`, `columns`, `instance_number` and `number_of_frames`, as the registry read them from the headers, each null where it holds none. So an image chooses which frames to read without reading a header first, as body part's reduced reader and its few-slice student do; a multi-frame file's position is its first frame's, and its other frames are read from the file. It goes beside the header (`x-nils.input.header`) or alone, together and apart; without it `stacks.json` is as it was, and a bids input is refused it. With it `stacks.json` also lists, under `context`, the other MR stacks of each session the run holds part of that are not sealed now, each with its modality and geometry and its files' geometry and no path, so a stack is read against its whole session in any run (a `--chunk` page, a selection that is not whole studies); a run of whole sessions lists none, and a unit run apart is given the rest of its session the same way. With the geometry `stacks.json` is written compact, since the files' geometry makes it three to four times the size, and from the entries without a copy of them. The job contract v1 is amended in place, additively.
- A dataset is probed by its name: `POST /api/ingest/probe` takes `dataset` instead of `location` (one of the two, never both) and `nils ingest probe --dataset NAME` reads the dataset's originals, the same bounded sample and the same shapes-only answer. Its originals are identified files, so the door needs detail quasi besides `data:work`. What the dataset's structure says decides whether there is anything to read: an identified dataset, and one with its anonymised copy beside, is probed; a name that is no dataset, a root among them, is refused naming the datasets (404); a dataset whose structure is unknown, an anonymised one, one whose originals were vaulted or purged and one with no originals tree on disk are refused with 409, before anything is queued. A dataset's probe also reads each file's birth date, sex and study date, in memory only, and answers per candidate `alike`: `{pairs: [{subjects: [code, code], agree: [birth_date, sex], visits: {shared, of}}], linked, unmapped}`, the subjects whose birth date and sex agree and whose visits overlap, by the codes the linkage store files their identifiers under (record 55 K9). It proposes nothing and merges nothing; a merge stays a person's act at `POST /api/linkage/merge`. Below detail quasi, where a subject's code is only ever a shape (K7), the jobs doors answer `alike` with no pairs and `withheld`, their count.
- Rules releases (record 55 B5): a first-party pack is released on its own, under the tag `pack-mri-v1.0.2`, with no engine release beside it. The release (`rules-release.yml`, on a commit main's checks passed, the pack's `pack.yml` stating the tag's version) carries `pack-mri.tar.gz`, holding `packs/mri/` as the engine's `packs.tar.gz` holds every pack, and `pack-mri.VERSION`, both named in its `SHA256SUMS`, and is never the repository's latest release. `nils update --check` says each pack beside its own newest release, `nils update --part rules` takes the newest one the engine reads and nothing else, with nothing started again, and `--all`, `nils setup` and the supervisor's update door take it with the rest. What a release works with is read from its `pack.yml`: its pack contract, and from contract 10 the engines it names; one the engine would refuse is never installed, and the check says it waits and why. The engine's own release is the floor: a pack's own release goes in where it is at least as new as the copy the engine's release carries, an engine update never puts an older copy over it, and a pack in place the engine would refuse is replaced by the release's whatever its version. The pack replaced is kept in `<dir>.previous`, one update deep, in the same single swap, and `.nils-packs.json` names the release each pack came from. The setup record notes the rules in use (`[rules.mri]`, the version and the release), and an uninstall that keeps the data keeps the note with it. The install door gives a row per such pack among `release.parts` (`part: rules`), `release.rules`, and `ahead`, `rules` and `refused` on `release.packs`. On GitHub the releases are found in the listing by their tags, and the engine's own lookups take only its own tags; a channel of a deployment's own names the newest in `latest/download/pack-mri.VERSION`, and `NILS_RULES_RELEASES` names a channel for the rules alone. Where the engine's own release cannot be read, as on a development channel that keeps its newest build alone, a pack's own releases are measured against the pack in place, never below it. An engine in a container reads the packs its image carries.
- A rules version can be pinned (record 55 B5, Nima's decision of 2026-10-10): `nils update --pin mri@1.0.0` puts that version in place from its own release, or the engine's copy where that is the version, whatever is newer, and every update after it keeps it, an engine update included; `--check` says what the pin holds back, the supervisor's rules rows say `pinned`, the setup record notes it under `[rules.<pack>]`, and `nils update --unpin mri` lifts it and takes the newest release the engine reads. A pin of a version that is neither in place nor a release this install can read is refused, and nothing is written. The pin lives in the pack directory's manifest, `.nils-packs.json`, so every planner reads it.
- Pack contract 10 (record 55 B5): a pack may name the engines it works with, `engine: ">=1.0.0-alpha.80, <2.0.0"`, ordered as releases are, a development build after the release it was built from. The loader refuses a pack whose range the engine is outside, with the range and the engine named, and refuses the key in a pack that declares less than 10, so an engine at 9 refuses such a pack by its contract. The contract stays the key every engine checks. An engine at 10 loads a contract-9 pack unchanged.
- A rehearsed rule change scores the main-scan picks of each side by its own pack's pick (the 2026-10-10 study of the pick borders): before, the effect report scored both sides with the pack's pick as it was, so no change to the pick file could be rehearsed. The picks section counts, per pick and role and in all, the picks kept, changed (a pick both ways naming other stacks), removed and added, `moved` (the three together) and `held_by_a_person`, and the occasions whose borders are raised, settled or still there; each example carries its borders before and after. The names `appear` and `disappear` are gone. `nils pack rehearse` prints the same. On a planted registry a pick change's counts equal a pick run by each pack, occasion by occasion.
- A pick change is a typed operation too (the 2026-10-10 study of the pick borders; `patch/v1` amended in place, additively). Four operations reach the pick file, each a pack edit only: `set_candidates` (which stacks holding a role compete for it, as `when` and `unless` conditions `{axis, is}` or `{tag, is | lt | le | gt | ge}`; both empty lets every stack holding the role compete again), `remove_border` (a border kind the pick raises no more, by its reason or its key; `too_close` is the margin, set rather than removed), `set_near_tie` (the order a near tie is decided by; empty takes it out) and `set_runner_up_within` (the margin of a near tie). `set_candidates` and `set_near_tie` raise the pack to declare pack contract 9 where it declares less, and say so. `add_rule` may set `nothing` on an axis that holds several values, such as `role`: the stack holds none of its values where the conditions hold, the rule placed first in the first set that decides the axis, and a place other than first is refused. A rule a later one stopped this way is ranked against it in the notes, where it was ranked against a person's answer before.
- Pack contract 9 (the 2026-10-10 study of the pick borders): a pick file may say which stacks holding a role compete for it, `candidates` per role with `when` and `unless` lists of conditions over the names the pick reads (`{of, any: [values]}`, an axis value by its identity, label or alias, or `{of, lt | le | gt | ge: number}`), and a stack it does not admit is no candidate and no part of the population the role is scored against; and how a near tie is decided, `near_tie`, an order of steps (`prefer` values, `avoid` them, the `lowest` or the `highest` number, for every role or the `roles` a step names): among the candidates within `runner_up_within` of the best, the first step that tells the first two apart decides, the pick's notes say which (`near_tie`), and a near tie it decides raises no `too_close` and is counted as no tie. A near tie no step decides is a border as before. An engine at 9 loads a contract-8 pack unchanged and refuses these keys in a pack that declares less than 9. The MRI pack declares neither.
- The pseudonymise step of a dataset (2026-10-09): its held identifiers one row each, `GET /api/linkage/held/ids?place=` under `data:see` at plain, each by the first held row that carries it, with its shape, its type, its files, when it was first seen and how it stands (`held` with no code yet, `mapped` once a map named it, `generated` to be coded anyway at the next run, `waits` for its subject's value of the type PatientID gets), a code with the datasets its subject is in already (`also_in`) and below detail quasi as its shape, and the subjects the dataset's pseudonymised files carry with how many were coded without a map. A map rehearsed for a dataset answers the code it gives each of the dataset's held identifiers (`held_ids` on the dry run of `POST /api/linkage/imports`). `POST /api/linkage/held/code` codes only the identifiers `ids` names, and with `run: false` marks them and queues nothing, for the dataset's next run to code them. The held reveal names each identifier by its row (`id`). Never a value. Contract v7 amended in place, additively.
- The text each axis the rules resolved was matched against (kineuro/nils#94, the part that remained): `GET /api/classify/signals` carries `resolved_texts` beside `unresolved_texts`, from the same sample, with the same overlay and under the same showing threshold (five stacks of three subjects): per axis the stacks the rules gave a value, a default not counted, and per value the pack's search texts of those stacks, the most common first and at most ten, each with the words the rules cited in it for that value. A person tuning a value's words sees how the site writes it, also where the header decided it and no word did.
- The dataset viewer's Grid (2026-10-09): a dataset's subjects a page at a time, `GET /api/datasets/{name}/subjects`, each with its visits, scans, scans to look at, body regions, makers and main roles, filtered (`filter=look,visits2,main:<role>,region:<part>,maker:<name>`, kinds all, values of one kind any), searched by code (`q`, and an identifier given whole for a caller who may read identifiers, found by its keyed lookup), ordered (`order=look|code|visits|scans`) and shown by the code or an id type's value (`show`, opened for the page's subjects only and audited, as it is only to data:work at detail sensitive, an alias also at detail quasi, as its shape otherwise), with the whole dataset's totals and facets; one subject's visits, `GET /api/datasets/{name}/subjects/{subject}/visits`, each with its session or its studies, a label by date, number or days from the first visit (`name`), its number in the subject's whole timeline, its scan kinds in words, whether a contrast agent was given, its SyMRI scans and its main scans, filtered by `look`, `contrast`, `symri` and `region:<part>`; and the same three over a cohort's current members across datasets (`GET /api/cohorts/{name}/subjects`, `.../subjects/{subject}/visits` and `GET /api/cohorts/{name}/scans`). Both scans doors narrow a page to one visit (`session` or `studies`), and each scan carries `scope`, `study`, `family` (symri, mix, stage, swi, derived, body or plain), `te`, `tr`, `ti`, `fa` and `main`, its live picks' roles. Below detail quasi the code, every date and the days worked out from dates are answered as their shapes (record 55 K7), and a stack of a sample sealed now is in no number (record 48). A scan needs a look while an open or staged review item asks about it, everywhere it is counted, so the scans door's `questions` now names a staged question too, as the card's `need_a_look` counts it. Contract v7 amended in place, additively.
- A question can name a dataset (2026-10-09): the ask's catalog has a `dataset` field. On a stack it is the name of the dataset the stack came from, the source place whose digest first read it (the stacks a dataset's `totals.stacks` counts on the Data page); a root is never one, and a stack no dataset holds has none. On a session and a subject it is the datasets their stacks came from, as one sorted list joined by `, `, and `=`, `<>`, `in`, `not_in` and `has` ask whether a name is among them: a subject or a session is in a dataset when one of its stacks is. So "T1 scans in study-big" is a stack set with `["=", {}, ["field", {}, "dataset"], "study-big"]` beside its axes, and "subjects in study-big" a subject set with the same clause. A group by a stack's dataset counts each stack once; a group by a subject's or a session's counts it once under each of its datasets. It filters, groups and counts in a run, a preview, a queued job, a handle read again and MCP, on SQLite and Postgres. A dataset's name is technical, shown at every detail as on the Data page, and never leaves the node. `GET /api/ask/catalog` lists the names in force as `datasets`, the value sampler answers each with its count, and a name the registry does not hold is refused at validate (`unknown_value`) with the names it does. The engine reads the places again when one is added, moved or retired, which moves no epoch, so a retired dataset is no dataset at the next request; an answer cached before keeps the names it was run with until it is run again with `fresh`.
- The Data page whole (2026-10-09): what a dataset holds and where it is, `GET /api/datasets/{name}/summary` under `data:see` at plain: its subjects, visits (out of the session cache, null where nobody has built it) and scans, how sure the sort is, its scans by kind (the base axis by its label, a FLAIR as one) and by body region, its files found, read, refused (with the newest batch that refused one) and held, and its steps (found, pseudonymised where it has originals, read, sorted, body part, post-contrast, main scans, pictures and 3D views), each with its state (`done`, `running`, `queued`, `waiting` or `off`), its counts and the job that did it last with its times, or its progress while it runs; a sort's pictures are said to be made while its run makes them after its row says done. Body part and post-contrast are operations of their own (record 56): each step says whether a model is served for it, the run over the dataset's scans that runs or ran last, how many scans its model answered and how many wait on a person, and how sure the sort is counts only the questions the sort asks; one cohort's document carries the same steps over its members' scans (`steps`). A dataset's jobs alone, `dataset=` on `GET /api/jobs`: the ones that name it or a path in its folder or carry its name and a day, its digests' and its sorts', the runs of the body-part and post-contrast models over its stacks, and every job queued after any of those. The main scans of a cohort's members, `cohort=` on `GET /api/picks/summary`, and each role's `subjects`. The cohort documents' `parts` (what brought the members: a dataset's read, a query, a hand, an import) and `datasets` (the datasets holding scans of them, with the ones that feed it); one cohort's document also names the dataset of each digest's join, when each release was made (`finished_at`), and its members' clinical coverage (`clinical`, never a kind marked sensitive and never a value). Counts only; a stack of a sample sealed now is counted by no kind and no region (record 48). Contract v7 amended in place, additively.
- A rule change shows its effect on the sorting before anyone decides (record 56, Nima's ruling of 2026-10-09: a rule change shows how it affects the sorting, how many stacks and to what). Rule edits are typed operations, written as a pack patch (`contracts/patch/v1`, a new contract): `add_words` and `remove_words` (the words a value's rule reads: its axis file's list, every longhand keyword clause that sets it, or the bucket either reads, narrowed to a `field` or a `rule`), `move_rule` (inside its set), `move_set` (in the pack's order), `set_priority` (in an exclusion group), `add_value` (to an axis's vocabulary, with its label, family, group, words, place in the order and BIDS name), `add_rule` (from templates: words in a field, a tag equal to a value, a physics range, a flag, an axis value, or their combination), `silence` (an axis says nothing and asks nothing where a condition holds: decided as nothing by a set that runs after what it reads, with each pass that writes the axis leaving those stacks out of its target; without an axis, the stack is nobody's question), `by_model` and `map_name` (a value's BIDS name, or its label). Each carries its scope, the whole pack (a pack edit, shipped as a rules release) or `site:NAME` (the datasets under one source root), `dataset:NAME` or `scanner:KEY=VALUE` (an overlay, which never moves a stack outside its scope), its reason and its evidence. The engine applies them in order to the pack's own documents and builds the result with the pack's own loader; an operation that cannot apply is refused with a plain why, and the pack's corpus and the patch's own cases judge the result. An overlay document reads as the word edits it is. `nils pack apply DIR --ops FILE [--out DIR]` checks a patch, and writes a patch of pack edits as the pack's next version, each changed file rewritten where it changed so its comments stay.
- `nils pack rehearse --ops FILE [--scope ...]` and `POST /api/packs/{name}/rehearse` (pipelines:work or review:work; OpenAPI 7, added in place) answer what a patch does: the registry in scope is sorted twice from what it holds, by the pack and by the patched pack, without a row written (the rules, the decisions in force, the session passes, the physics vote, the questions a sort asks, the disposition and the main-scan picks), and the answer is the transition table per axis (from, to, how many stacks, their datasets, sites, makes and scanners, example stacks, and the stacks a decision holds), the descriptive and BIDS names that change, before and after, the picks that change by role, the Review questions that appear and go by kind, right to wrong and wrong to right against a person's decisions in force on the rules' own answer, the scope and the version it ships as, and the seconds each step took. The stacks are read once and sorted in parallel chunks, as the archive replay sorts its packets. A stack of a sample sealed now is never read. On a planted registry the moves and the questions equal a full re-sort under the patched pack, stack for stack; on a real study corpus of 1,003 scans a patch of four operations rehearses in under a second and matches the re-sort and the pick run exactly.
- Body part and post-contrast run from their steps (2026-10-10, record 56): `POST /api/datasets/{name}/steps/{step}/run` and `POST /api/cohorts/{name}/steps/{step}/run`, under `pipelines:work` at detail quasi, freeze the scans the step counts into a handle and queue the step's pipeline over them as a `pipeline` job that names its `step` and what it is `for`, which the pipeline lane runs. Body part runs the certified model of record 50: the newest active `bodypart-infer-fusion`, given the encoder its served head names, that head, and the served coarse mode of the same encoder, one whose card names the head first. The step then goes `queued`, `running`, and `done` or `failed`, and the dataset's log lists the job. A refusal answers its `reason` beside its words: `running`, `no_pipeline`, `no_model` (post-contrast always: no post-contrast model is installed), `no_runtime`, `no_place` or `no_scans`; a dataset's summary and a cohort's document say the same of each of the two steps before anyone presses Run (`refusal`). The HTTP API contract stays version 7, amended in place, additively.
- The dataset view's names (2026-10-09): each scan of `GET /api/datasets/{name}/scans` carries `name`, its descriptive name in v0's grammar; `bids`, its BIDS name in the full style without the `sub-` and `ses-` entities the tree says (a derivative with its `desc-`, null where the standard has none, for a scout or a working scan, or with no pack served); `datatype` (`anat`, `dwi`, `func`, `perf`, `fmap` or `other`); `folder`, the descriptive layout's folder (`anat/SyMRI` and the rest); `axes`, every decided axis as stored; `series_number`; and `questions`, the kinds of its open review questions, now also without `pictures=1`, so the desk can show a dataset as a tree of subject, session, datatype and scan. Each name is built from the scan's own facts, as `nils_release::run::scan_names` builds it; a release also separates two scans of one session that build one name.
- The Data card can say how sure a sort is of a dataset, "120 scans, 112 sure, 8 need a look": a source's `totals` on `GET /api/sources` gain `sure` (sorted stacks no open or staged review item asks about), `unsorted` (stacks no sort has judged) and `need_a_look`, the stacks that need a look by the kind of the question (`{"body_part:low_confidence": 3, ...}`); `to_sort` now also counts a stack that a stack-scoped question (System 1's) asks about, so `stacks` is `sure + to_sort + unsorted`. `GET /api/review` and `GET /api/review/summary` take `dataset=` (a name or an id), so the card's Review button opens the items about that dataset.
- Picking main scans is a pipeline step after the sort: a `classify` job that ends done at the worker is followed by `pick run --after-sort JOB` for the subjects whose stacks it judged, with the same pack and its `picks/main.yml`, as a job of its own. A dataset can turn it off with `picks: off` (`--picks off` on `nils place add` and `nils place set`, or the field on `POST /api/places` and `PUT /api/places/{id}`); `after_sort` is the default. A chain that names its own pick run, as the bring-in of a dataset that feeds a cohort does, keeps it, and that one is left out too where the dataset's picks are off. A pick run's job keeps its report as its result, which now also counts ties and gives every count per role. `GET /api/picks/summary?dataset=NAME` answers, per role, how many occasions are picked, how many are clear, how many tied, the open border items and their borders by reason, with the last run that decided them.
- A stack's preview, made when it is sorted, so the Data page's pictures never wait for a job queue or a 3D volume: the classify run makes the previews of the stacks it judged (`--no-previews` leaves them), one file a stack under the working place beside the pyramids, keyed by the stack's content digest and made again only when its files change. A preview holds the three middle planes (the stack's own plane and the two across it, about 256 pixels on the long side in true proportions) and every plane at display size (512 at most), each an 8-bit grey JPEG windowed as the pyramid opens, with a held copy for a stack that carries burned-in annotation. `nils preview build --stack ID | --dataset NAME | --all [--force]` makes them for stacks sorted before, resumably, as one job of kind `preview` for many, and `--all` removes the previews of stacks the registry no longer holds. `GET /api/instances/{stack}/preview` answers the first picture in one request (the header and the three planes as data URLs, or one plane as an image with `plane`), `GET /api/instances/{stack}/preview/planes?from=&to=` a range of planes in one container read in one go; both carry an ETag on the digest, are immutable when `v` names it, answer 304 to If-None-Match, are opened and audited as every picture is, and come from open files the engine keeps (512 at most). A stack with no preview is made in the request. `GET /api/datasets/{name}/scans?pictures=1` gives each scan its picture inline and the kinds of its open review questions, so a page of fifty is one request.
- A scan with no preview yet shows its first picture at once, as v0 drew one: the preview door decodes the middle plane's one file in the request (a JPEG 2000 plane at a lower resolution, since it is drawn at 256 pixels) and answers it as a partial preview (`partial: true`, never cached), and the whole preview is made after on a thread of the engine, the stack a reader opened before any other. On real JPEG 2000 stacks of up to 448 planes of 640 by 640 the first picture takes 12 to 60 ms where it took 1.8 to 6 s. A short range of planes (32 at most) is decoded from its own files in the request; a longer one waits for the preview, up to eight seconds, else 503 with `retry_after_ms`. A page of scans with pictures gives such a scan the same picture within a tenth of a second's budget and decodes the rest for the page asked next.
- A preview's frames keep to a budget (preview format 2): 384 pixels on the long side for a stack of more than 200 planes, and the highest quality whose frames come to about 6 MB a stack, so a 448-plane stack is 5 to 6 MB where it was 16 to 21 MB. Previews of format 1 are made again. `frames.quality` says the quality taken.
- The manifest door holds a request up to three seconds while the stack's pyramid is built and answers as the build ends; the instance doors' 202 carries `retry_after_ms` (250 at first, then 500 and 1000) beside `Retry-After`, now one second.
- The pyramids are built after a sort in the background: a classify that ends done queues `pyramid build --classified JOB` on a lane of its own, the pictures lane, which `nils serve --worker` runs beside the main and pipeline lanes at a lower priority and with half the workers, so a pyramid never holds up a digest, a classify or a pseudonymise, and a reader's single-stack build (the on-demand fallback) runs first on the lane and between two stacks of a background build. `nils jobs work --lane pictures` runs that lane alone. A worker beside the doors starts a job as soon as a door queues it, rather than at its next look at the queue, which stays as the fallback for jobs another process queues.
- `GET /api/datasets/{name}/scans?limit=&after=` lists a dataset's scans a page at a time, for the list under its card on the Data page: the stacks its digests created first (the ones `totals.stacks` counts), read from the registry and never through a cohort, so a dataset that feeds none lists its scans too. Each is `{stack, subject {id, code}, session {id, label}, series_description, orientation, images, day}`, sorted by subject code, day and stack id, 50 a page by default and 200 at most, `next` the stack id to page on from. Below detail quasi the subject's code, the day and a session label made from a date are answered as their shapes. A stack of a sample sealed now is not listed, and a dataset nothing has read answers an empty list. Under `data:see`.
- What PatientID holds is the dataset's to declare: `patient_id: subject-code` (an identified dataset's default) or `patient_id: id-type:<name>`, as `--patient-id` on `nils place add` and `nils place set` and a field of `POST /api/places` and `PUT /api/places/{id}`. The pseudonymiser writes the subject's code, or the subject's value of that id type from the linkage store, found through the subject the subject code generator's code names; every value read is audited. A file whose subject has no value of the type is held with its subject and the type, and the next run writes it once a map gives the subject one (a map of `canonical:personnummer` and `identifier:<type>`, or of `code` and `identifier:<type>` with `--make-types` for a new type). The copy's folder stays the subject's code. The digest reads the pseudonymised tree back by the same declaration: the code verbatim, or the value as that id type through the linkage store, and so does a dataset read in place that declares it and stores no identity rule. A personnummer is never what PatientID holds. What PatientID holds is changed only before anything is pseudonymised. Registry schema 81: a held file keeps the subject and the id type it waits for, and `GET /api/linkage/held` names that type as `waits_for` (null for a file whose subject is unknown). The sources door and the places capabilities carry `patient_id`.

- `nils place explore [PLACE] [--json]` looks at the source places again: every dataset's state is read again from its structure, and nothing is added; `GET /api/places?explore=1` does the same before it answers. `nils place layout PLACE|DIR [--json]` shows what a folder holds, a root's folders each with its state, and writes nothing. A dataset's layout (`state`, `reads`, `pseudonymises`, `loose`, `loose_entries`, `loose_dicom`, `question`, `move_into`, `settings` with what `patient_id`, `subjects` and `copy_folder` take, `moved`, `renamed`) answers every declaration, `POST /api/ingest/look` and each dataset of `GET /api/places`, where a root carries `datasets`, its datasets' names, and every source place `not_read`, why it is not read or null.
- A dataset may name each pseudonymised copy's folder by the id type's value PatientID holds instead of the subject's code: `copy_folder: id-type` (`--copy-folder`), with `patient_id: id-type:<name>`; `subject-code` is the default. A value that is no safe folder name is made one. Like what PatientID holds, it changes only before anything is pseudonymised.
- `nils setup --scheme blake2b-8|blake2b-32` chooses the scheme of the registry it makes (`--scheme subject-code-generator` names `blake2b-8` too, here and in `nils init`, and a registry records it as `blake2b-8`), and `--reg-key-file FILE` takes the registry's key from a site's key file instead of a passphrase: one line, `REG_KEY=<value>` or the value alone, used byte for byte, readable by its owner only. A file others may read, an empty value, a value with a leading or trailing space and one longer than 64 bytes are refused before anything is written, and the key is never shown, only its fingerprint.
- Every file the pseudonymiser writes into `dcm-anon`, and every DICOM file a release writes, says it was de-identified and how: `PatientIdentityRemoved` (0012,0062) `YES`; `DeidentificationMethod` (0012,0063) naming NILS, its version and the writer (`pseudonymise` or `release`); `DeidentificationMethodCodeSequence` (0012,0064) with one item per option of DICOM PS3.16 CID 7050 the writer's own plan applies, derived from that plan and never written by hand: 113100 the basic profile always; 113106 full dates kept, since every date is kept; 113108 patient characteristics, while the age, sex, size or weight is kept; 113109 device identity, while the device serial number or the station name is kept; 113110 UIDs, while they are kept (the pseudonymiser always, a release only when it does not remap them); 113111 safe private elements, where the pack's allowlist keeps them; 113112 institution identity, while the institution's name or address is kept; and `LongitudinalTemporalInformationModified` (0028,0303) `UNMODIFIED`. The marks are written after every removal, so no category and no dataset's `remove` list takes them out, and a release of a pseudonymised file replaces the pseudonymiser's marks with its own.
- Every file the pseudonymiser or a release writes leaves without its accession number (0008,0050) and study id (0020,0010) (Nima's ruling of 2026-10-09: both leave a file when it is pseudonymised), whatever the categories a run applies and whatever a dataset names to keep. Until now `dcm-anon` kept both, since no category the pseudonymiser removes holds them, and so its files named the basic profile (113100) untruly; the basic profile is now claimed only by a plan that removes both, which is every plan. Both are removed rather than written empty, as every other identifier the categories hold is. `GET /api/pseudonymize/tags` serves them as `examination`, with the reason `examination`, and the run's report counts them.
- `GET /api/pseudonymize/tags` (contract 7, additive) mends the five findings of using it: every row carries a `reason` (`category`, `computed`, `covariate`, `code`, `mandatory`), so a covariate is read from its row; the mandatory rows say a release remaps the instance UID and never removes it; `version` is the engine's, for a reader's cache; `fate_meanings` says what each of `fates` means; and the contract says `covariates.opt_out` is a field's name, not for display. A `marks` block states the four marks and, per writer, every option it can state, with when it applies and whether its default plan does.
- The release gate runs the official BIDS validator (3.0.2, pinned in `tools/release-check/bids-validator.sh`) over the fixture release in both naming modes over a second fixture of stacks that share names, and over a third with a SyMRI exam, and holds it to 0 errors; warnings are listed in the job's summary. `reference --names` and `reference --symri` write those fixtures.

### Fixed

- A BIDS release passes the official validator on real data (Wave 7a section 8.2, T12). Run on a release of a development install's test data, the validator found three faults the synthetic gate cannot show. A conversion `dcm2niix` failed partway left the images it had written, under letter-suffixed names, in the tree: they are removed with the failure. A gradient table `dcm2niix` writes beside a scanner's trace stayed when the suffix's group was unknown: only a `dwi` image keeps one. And an image whose kind BIDS requires metadata the headers never carry (an MP2RAGE's preparation timings, an ASL image's labelling, a field map's units, a pepolar map's readout time, a `dwi` without its table, an echo without its echo time, a T1w of four dimensions) is now written as DICOM under `sourcedata/` with what it lacks, as a stack that does not convert is, instead of as an invalid image.
- A document read from a handle made since the ask catalog was built is validated against that handle, at every ask door and not only at the start door: each handle a body names (`from.handle`, or a set's `handle:N`) is read and told to the catalog first, so whether a handle just made is known no longer hangs on when the catalog was built (found as a flaky `every_door_holds_a_list_of_fewer_than_five_that_an_age_chose`, which then read "no handle" instead of "holds no rows").
- A request that reaches the engine after its last handler ended (the end of `nils serve --requests`, or a stop) is answered 503 with why and a Retry-After, never dropped (2026-10-12). Dropped, its caller read an empty 500.
- A pick run says what it wrote (2026-10-12, found trying the desk on a laptop install: a dataset's run of its main scans ended at once, its log said it picked for 10 subjects, and it wrote nothing, since none of the dataset's T1w scans held a main role). The run's report adds `visits`, `visits_picked` and `subjects_picked`, and where it wrote nothing `nothing`, why: none of its subjects has a scan of a main role, no visits found for its subjects (their studies carry no date), or the rules allow no scan on its visits. `said` is the run in one line ("Main scans picked for 3 visits of 2 subjects", "No main scans picked: its one subject has no scan of a main role"), which `nils pick run` and `nils jobs show` print. The Main scans map, subjects and strips doors and a person's pick named by its subject and day bring the session cache up to date for the scope's subjects before they read visits (`nils_session::ensure_for`), as the pick run, the release and a run's main input do, so a dataset read and sorted with no pick run since shows its visits. A dataset's summary counts no visits (`sessions` null) while the cache lacks some of its dated studies, rather than saying 0.

- A date written as text is read as a date wherever a date meets it, on both backends (2026-10-12). Postgres compares no date with text, so `first >= "2020-01-01"`, the kind of filter the chat writes, answered 500 there, and so did an instant such as a cohort's `created_at`, a list of dates, and a date function given a day as text (`days_between`, `age_at`, `shift`, `bucket`, `part`). The compiler binds such a text, a literal or a parameter's value, as a date: a day, `YYYY-MM-DD`, or an instant in UTC, `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ`, where it meets an instant. A day and an instant are compared by their days; a set's or a group's earliest and latest dates stay dates; `coalesce`, `case`, `least` and `greatest` read their texts alike; and `~=` counts the days between two dates on both backends, where SQLite compared their digits. Any other text, or a number, that a date meets is refused at its clause as `unknown_value` (400) with the two forms to write, and so is a date parameter's value that is no date; where only the compiler can tell the date it meets, a binding's, it is refused as `not_compilable`. The quasi-identifier rules hold as they were: a filter reads a date at every detail, and a column is answered as the detail allows. Two gate fixtures hold it on both backends. The OpenAPI 7 contract is amended in place, additively.
- A stored document whose session scheme was removed since it was stored is refused with what to do, where it answered 500 (2026-10-12). Every door that reads a stored document (run, jobs before it queues, validate, explain, preview, diagnose, describe, options, profile, documents, and start from a document) answers 409, `scheme_mismatch` at `scheme`, naming the document and the scheme that is gone, and saying to store the document again with a scheme the registry keeps or to keep the scheme again with `nils session scheme add`. A document the request carries that names a scheme the registry does not keep is refused with 400 at every door, as validate refuses it, in the same words. `nils ask run --document` and the other verbs of `nils ask` refuse both with exit 2, so a queued ask whose scheme is gone fails saying so. The OpenAPI 7 contract is amended in place, additively.
- What only the compiler refuses of a document the validator passed, a parameter declared without a value for one, is refused as a document at every door that runs a question, where it answered 500 (2026-10-12). The run, preview and explain doors, and the MCP tools through them, answer 400, `the document is refused`, with the compiler's words at the path it names as `not_compilable`, the way they answer a validation refusal: a compile error is a run's error of its own, never a message. The jobs door refuses it before anything is queued, where it queued the job and the worker failed on it. At the command line `nils ask run` and `nils ask explain` name the issue and exit 2, as `nils ask validate` does and as a door's 400 reads through `--server`, and so do a refusal by the validator and a measure's refusal, which exited 1; `nils ask validate` compiles what run would, as the validate door does, where it passed such a document. The OpenAPI 7 contract is amended in place, additively.
- A `picked` whose role the served pack does not pick is refused, where it read no rows (2026-10-12). Validation refuses it at the clause's path as `unknown_value`, naming the roles the pack's picks declare (`picked reads no role named t2; the pack's roles are t1w, flair, t2w`, with the clause to write in `next`), so a question about the main scans of a role written another way (`t2`, `T2w`, the pick's own name `main`) is told what to write at every door instead of answering nothing. A role given as a parameter is read as before. The OpenAPI 7 contract is amended in place, additively.
- The FreeSurfer recon-all starter declares 32 GB of memory, where it declared 8. FreeSurfer 8's release notes give about 24 GB for recon-all, and its install notes about 80 GB in use at one point, which a machine of 32 GB carries only with nothing else running. The pinned 8.2.0 image already carries the fix of 8.2.0's expert-options bug (its `recon-all` and `rca-surfreg` have the checksums of those in `fs820_updates.sh`), which only touched a run given an expert-options file. The engine adds the starter's new version when it starts.
- The MRIQC starter passes `--notrack`: MRIQC 24.0.2 sends usage telemetry to its developers unless told not to, as it uploads the IQMs unless told `--no-sub`, which the starter already passed. The engine runs it without a network either way. The engine adds the starter's new version when it starts.
- The Main scans map shows a scan copied into two datasets under each of them (2026-10-12, Nima's model: a dataset is a folder that holds stacks). By dataset, `POST /api/picks/map` put each stack under the first dataset that read it; a stack with a path in two datasets (the duplicate policy of 2026-10-10) is now under every dataset whose folder holds a file of it, read through the one place that says which folders hold a stack (`dataset_subjects::holders`). Each of those columns counts its visit, so the columns' visits, and a kind's cells across them, add up to more than the visits: `shared_visits` on the map and its strips says how many visits are shown under more than one column, and each column's `shared` how many of its own are. Other datasets counts each visit once, however many of the datasets past the eight named hold its scan. A visit at `/map/subjects` lists every column it is under in `columns`, `column` staying the first, and a cell's subjects are found under any of them. The OpenAPI 7 contract is amended in place, additively.
- The draft door refuses a clause the compiler cannot read, with the form to write, where it answered a bare 500 (2026-10-12, found by the chat sandbox bench). A `bucket` bound without its unit passed the validator and failed the compiler while `POST /api/ask/draft` counted its diagnosis. Validation now refuses, at the clause's path as `not_compilable` and with the form to write in `next`, each clause the compiler cannot read: an op given fewer arguments than it reads; a `bucket` or `part` without its unit, or with one the compiler does not read (`part` takes year, month, day or dow: the `hour` the validator offered was never read); a `shift` unit other than day, month or year; a list anywhere but as the list of `in` or `not_in`, an `=` with a list being told to write `in`; an `in` list of anything but texts and numbers; a `contains` or `starts_with` without its literal text; `round`'s places as anything but a whole number; and a `picked` without its role, or with a number for one. And the diagnosis, which compiles what it counts, refuses what the compiler alone refuses (a parameter without a value among it) before it counts anything, so the draft and diagnose doors answer it as `not_compilable` in the diagnosis, as `POST /api/ask/validate` and the documents door already refused it.
- `bodypart-infer-fusion` declares 12 GB of memory, where it declared 8: the cascade over 19,275 stacks at 32 workers peaked at 10.4 GiB for all its processes, the main process 2.2 GiB with stage 1 streamed and a worker at most 3.3 GiB.
- nils-bodypart's cascade with its student on the card decodes on the card with one worker by default, not two, so the card holds two processes, as the card's lease allows; `--gpu-workers` still sets it, and without the cascade the default stays 2.
- nils-bodypart's CPU build on a machine with a card: `infer-fusion --device cuda` (or `auto`) ran the cascade's student, and `--encoder-device cuda` the encoder, on a card this torch cannot use, and the run crashed. Each now runs on the card only where torch can use one, and on the CPU otherwise with a warning; the student is loaded before any slice is read.
- nils-bodypart refuses a cascade whose card names no threshold, or not its head's: the engine stages each `body_part` decision at the threshold of the card that names it, the cascade's, so another would stage what the run said abstains, or the reverse.
- nils-bodypart's reduced reader (0.4.0's default `reduced:touched`, and the cascade's middle stages) no longer plans a stack again for ever when a file's SliceThickness reads as not a number: it takes the first kept file's thickness once, NaN included, as the full reader does.
- A check's item that its close could not write stays visible (found by type 3's first end-to-end test, 2026-10-12). The close named it in its answer and wrote nothing for it, but kept nothing on the item and closed its question as superseded, so after a reload the campaign showed it agreed and accepted. An item any close refuses is now kept in the state `refused` with when and why (`refused` on `GET /api/campaigns/{id}` and on a check's item door; registry schema 92), and a check's refused item keeps its question open, marked refused. A person may close such a check again (`close_again` on the campaign): the later close writes each refused item whose outcome now reads, as any close does, and clears its refusal. It never writes an older judgement over a newer one: an item whose result another open check holds stays refused, and one whose result was checked since leaves that check standing, stays refused saying so, and its question is closed.
- `GET /api/capabilities` answers at once while the host's container runtime is slow to say what it is. The door asked `podman info` itself whenever its finding was half a minute old, which took 5 s on a busy laptop and up to the probe's 30 s cap on a busy host, and held every other capabilities read behind it; a desk, which waits 5 s for the engine, said every half minute that the engine did not answer. `nils serve` now looks for a runtime on a thread of its own, at start and again behind the doors when its finding is two minutes old or unknown, and the doors answer the last finding (pipelines off for a moment, with that reason, until the first look comes back).
- A sort's job ends after the previews it makes, and a preview never fails it (review of 2026-10-10). The job was marked done before its first preview, so a queue waited behind a job shown done, sometimes for hours, that no cancel reached, and an error of the preview step turned it failed and ended its chain. The job stays running through the previews now, beating and hearing a cancel, and ends done after them; a preview that cannot be made is counted in its result.
- One handle cut short no longer fails the whole list of saved selections (review of 2026-10-10). The list sized each selection from its newest frozen handle and asked a truncated handle for its keys, whose refusal made the list answer 409; such a selection is listed unsized now.
- A visit's scans are its subject's alone (review of 2026-10-10). The scans doors narrowed a visit by its studies only, so where two patients' files name one study, which the registry files under the first, a visit of the first listed the second's series too.
- `nils uninstall --dir` refuses a directory other than the setup on record, and removes nothing (review of 2026-10-10). Where a record existed the `--dir` was ignored, so `--purge --yes --dir OLD` purged the recorded install and its key.
- `nils setup` reports a source's layout and never renames or makes a folder in it (review of 2026-10-10). Setup settled a source that is a dataset folder as a person's add does, so a v0 folder lost the `dcm-raw` v0 still reads, renamed `dcm-anon`, and an identified one got an empty `dcm-anon`. Such a folder is now set down unknown and read by nothing, with what adding it would do said; a person adds it from Data, which settles it as before.
- A release counts each shared name's stacks once, over what the version holds (review of 2026-10-10). A stack an earlier round of marks set apart, and a later round found to be one acquisition made again, was counted as a repeat and as told apart both, so a tree's README could say 2 were made again and 5 were not, of 5; the record of decided names listed raw-route names only while `not_repeats` counted derivatives and folders too; and the counts were of the whole registry, which places names every stack of. A stack is a repeat or told apart now, never both, the record lists every stack `not_repeats` counts, and the four counts are of the stacks the version places.
- A release never names two scans apart by how the rules decided their base (review of 2026-10-10). Where the name decided one scan's base and the physics the other's, `base_basis` separated them and `+Name` or `+Physics` entered `acq-`, a decision trail driven by protocol text; they take the number now, as the contract says.
- A sort supersedes only the questions it asks itself (review of 2026-10-10). A re-sort, a rules release's among them, marked superseded every open question its stacks belonged to, so it closed the body-part and post-contrast steps' `<axis>:model` groups and any question about an axis the pack leaves to an operation of its own (`review.by_model`), and the steps' counts of what needs a look went to nothing.
- A person's decision that an axis holds nothing answers it (review of 2026-10-10). The row such a decision writes carries no value and was read as no answer, so every sort asked `<axis>:missing` about the stack again, and the effect report counted it as asked on both sides.
- A column of the ask wrapped in many clauses is shaped below detail quasi, and the ask refuses clauses nested more than 64 deep (`not_compilable`, record 55 K7, review of 2026-10-10). The shapes check traced what a column reads only twelve levels down, so a quasi identifying field inside thirteen `coalesce`s was answered unshaped; it traces every level now, and refuses what it cannot.
- A main-scan pick counts an enhanced multi-frame file by its frames (the 2026-10-10 study of the pick borders, R5). The pick's slice count read a stack's instances, so a volume stored as one file of 27 to 192 frames scored as a volume of one slice, below every percentile of its population, and raised a slice-count outlier or a near tie. It now reads the images a stack holds, each frame of a file one image, and where one file's frames were split over several stacks, the frames that stack holds; the population a slice count is ranked in counts the same way. Only a stack whose SOP class can hold several frames is read for them. On a real study corpus the open pick borders go from 59 to 57 (three settle with their picks kept, one near tie the old count hid is raised), and the effect report reads the slice count as a pick run does.
- The physics vote reads the stacks of its pack's own modality only. A classify of the whole registry read every stack into the vote's corpus, so the MRI pack's vote filled a base and a technique on CT stacks from the MR pool (5 of a real study corpus's 12 CT stacks), where a pack that does not judge a modality says nothing of its stacks.

- A series whose EchoNumbers (0018,0086) counts something other than echoes is one stack again, not one stack per echo number. The stack key is a file's own, and FONAR writes a phase-contrast cine's frame there (32 frames at one position, the echo time stated on two of them and zero on the rest), which became 32 stacks of one image each beside the series' scout, while GE's 3D SWI EPI writes 1 and 2 on alternate slices under one echo time, which became two half volumes at twice the slice spacing. Once a digest has written its files, the stacks of every series it read are read back together, and those that agree on everything but the echo number and the echo time, with two or more echo numbers and never two echo times, are one stack: a zero or missing echo time states none. A real multi-echo series states an echo time for each echo and keeps a stack per echo. The run reports the stacks it folded (`echo_stacks_folded`) and the groups it left as they were because a person or a release named one of their stacks (`echo_groups_kept`), and the dry run counts the same. What the machine made of the stacks goes with them: questions no person answered, answers no person put in force, and a model's tables and scores, so the stack that stays is sorted anew and the body-part and post-contrast steps read as not run for it; a group a person or a release holds (a person's decision or answer, a pick, a seal, a campaign, a release, a pipeline's own derivative or measure) stays as it is. A registry digested before keeps such series until a run reads one of their files again (`nils digest <root> --reread-every --reread-one`, then a fingerprint and a sort). The viewer puts planes at one place in order of their trigger time, temporal position, echo number and instance number, so a cine's frames run in their order.
- A dataset added while the engine runs is read by its name at once: `@name` in a queued job, and a probe's location, are the locations the engine was started with and every active source place by its own name, read from the registry on each request, so a dataset added from the desk no longer answered "is not a registered ingest location" until a restart. A digest of a dataset whose pseudonymised tree is a link elsewhere is refused, as for any other dataset, while the dataset does not say what PatientID holds and how its subjects are found.
- The look at a folder before it is added (`holds_dicom`) looks into the dataset's trees (`derivatives/dcm-anon`, `dcm-raw`, `dcm-original`) first and follows symbolic links, stopping at the first DICOM file; a dataset whose tree is a link to its files said "no" DICOM. A filled tree in which the bounded look found no DICOM is `unknown`, never `no`.
- A dataset whose `derivatives/dcm-anon` (or `dcm-original`) is a symbolic link to a tree elsewhere listed no scans and counted no stacks on the Data page: a place is now matched to a digest's root through its folder and through each of its trees, both sides resolved the same way.
- `nils uninstall --keep-data` keeps everything a later setup needs. It deleted the setup record, which names the schemas this install made in a Postgres the site runs and the sign-in, place and model server choices, and the whole of Kvasir's directory, with its models, its keys and the sealed credentials. The next setup read only the record, so every choice had to be given again and a later purge left the schemas behind. Now the record's facts are kept in the base directory as `setup.kept.toml`, which the next `nils setup` in that directory takes its defaults from and carries the schemas and lingering this install made into the record it writes, so a later purge still drops them; with no record left, `nils uninstall` reads the copy (in `~/nils`, or the directory given with the new `--dir`). Kvasir's models, store, seal key, pepper and the assistant's key stay, and only what building Kvasir made goes, as for the assistant. The plan names, under `Keeping`, the kept record with the facts it holds, Kvasir's state and the models it holds.
- The series description and the protocol name are technical, as every sequence name is: the catalog classed them quasi-identifying, so the value sampler showed only their shapes, even at detail quasi. They are now answered as values at every detail.
- The quasi-identifier rule holds at every door that runs a question. Below detail quasi, a column that reads a quasi-identifying field (a station name, a date, a subject's code, or a binding, a group's key or a value worked out from one) is answered as its shape, as the value sampler shows it, by a run, a preview and a queued job, and so by MCP and by a handle's pages. The field still filters, orders and counts. A post-pass measure over such a column is refused below detail quasi. A handle records which way it answered (`quasi`: raw or shape), so an answer cached before this is never served again below detail quasi. The command line still reads every class, and each `nils ask run` at the keyboard writes an audit row.
- A BIDS release no longer writes a `.bval` and a `.bvec` beside a diffusion-derived image (a trace or an ADC map): the standard admits them beside `dwi` only, and the official BIDS validator reported each as an error. Any file the converter writes that the suffix's group does not admit is left out.
- The HTTP API contract is YAML a strict parser reads again, as a client or a page made from it needs: version 7 held a description with `: ` in an unquoted value (`GET /api/packs/{name}`) and the 404 of `POST /api/campaigns/{id}/assignments/{assignment}/answer` twice, now once with both its causes, and version 2 a summary with `: ` (`POST /api/ask/run`). Nothing the documents say changed, and a test parses every version strictly.
