- Version 7 of the HTTP API contract reads as it is written: in 82 values inside braces, mostly the descriptions of responses and parameters, a comma ended the text for a YAML reader and the rest of the sentence became a key with no value ("No such campaign, or not one of the caller's" read "No such campaign"); those values are quoted. `GET /api/ask/selections/{name}` and `POST /api/ask/handles/{id}/promote` declare the path parameter they name, so the document validates as OpenAPI 3.1. A test holds the current version to both.
- A picture asked for while its pyramid is being built no longer answers 500. The builder wrote the pyramid's manifest in place, and the manifest door, which holds a request while the build runs, could read it empty or half written ("EOF while parsing a value"). The manifest is now written whole or not at all, as a level's packed file is: a part file of its own, flushed to the storage and renamed over `manifest.json`.
- An axis value in a question is found whichever of its names the question uses and whichever a row holds. The MRI pack stores a value's label rather than its identity on several axes (post-contrast `given` is stored `1`, base `T2starw` is stored `T2*w`, technique `3D-TSE` is stored `SPACE`), so a question that named a value by its identity, as the catalog lists it and the assistant writes it, missed every scan the rules had classified, and one that named the label was refused. A value may now be named by its identity, its label or a former identity, and `=`, `<>`, `has`, and now `in` and `not_in`, on an axis match every row that holds it under any of them. A group keyed by an axis counts each value once, in the form the pack stores, and a `where` on its key reads either name. The catalog says for each axis which form its rows store (`stores`) and lists a value's former identities (`aliases`), and the value sampler answers a stack's axis (`GET /api/ask/catalog/stack/{axis}/values`). No answer cached before is served again.

## [1.0.0-alpha.79] - 2026-10-06

The MRI pack is 1.0.1: certificate 2's rule gaps and three conventions settled by deep research, on top of the certified pack 1.0.0. 1.0.1 is not certified; it is shown by an archive replay (28 of 546,440 stacks move) and by every earlier reference read (10 settled cells fixed, none broken), and it needs a fresh read before it may claim the certificate. After updating from alpha.77 or alpha.78, a reclassify is all that is needed: no re-read and no fingerprint. Registry schema 79 (fingerprint revision 7), HTTP API contract 7, pack contract 8, review-item contract 4, suite contract 3 and job contract 1, as before. No desk release: installs keep desk 1.0.0-alpha.62.

### Added

- A pack may read two numbers the engine works out from the fingerprint rather than stores: `images_per_position`, a stack's images over its slice positions, and `dwi_b_value_count`, the distinct b values its images write. A pack compares them with each other (`{field: images_per_position, gt: {field: dwi_b_value_count}}`), which tells a diffusion set that holds directional images beside its isotropic one where the file counts no directions. A rule, a pass and a pick read them alike; a replayed packet, a corpus case and a session sibling work them out from the fields beside them. No column is added and the fingerprint's revision does not move, so nothing needs to be fingerprinted again for them.

### Changed

- The MRI pack is 1.0.1: certificate 2's five rule gaps and three questions of convention, which deep research and a production archive's counts settled. It is not certified: 1.0.0 is, and 1.0.1 claims that certificate only after a small fresh read. Replayed over the 546,440 unsealed MR stacks of the archive, 28 stacks move against 1.0.0; over every held development reference, 10 settled cells move, all to the reference's value, and none is broken.
  - A spin-echo inversion recovery above 2,600 ms whose inversion time nulls CSF at its long repetition time is a FLAIR (a `t2_tirm` at TR 10,140 ms read STIR by its `tirm`).
  - GE's private pulse sequence name states an inversion (`T1FLAIR/Prop`), so a PROPELLER written ScanningSequence SE at a STIR's inversion time is a STIR.
  - A Philips diffusion stack with direction `I` and no direction count that holds more images a slice than b values is a diffusion set, no Trace, as one with a direction count already was.
  - A Siemens susceptibility-map-weighted image (SMWI) is the SWI output of its QSM protocol, not an acquired echo.
  - GE's `Reg - ` copy of a perfusion run written DERIVED is motion corrected, as Philips' is.
  - A real (phase-sensitive) inversion recovery in the STIR window whose name states no weighting is T2-weighted at an echo time of 40 ms or more (PD-weighted where named PD), beside its PSIR and Real; at a shorter echo time its base is left for a person.
  - A series named for a pituitary or sella dynamic of fewer than 10 time points, or frames more than 10 s apart, is a T1-weighted run, never DCE or PWI, whatever its name says; and DCE now also needs frames no more than 10 s apart where the fingerprint knows the interval.
  - A declared local convention: GE's motion-corrected diffusion outputs name each output in ReceiveCoilName (`isoDWI/b1000/R3`, `T2w(b=0)/R3`, `isoADC/...`, `FA/...`, `ExpAtt/...`), and within that closed set the label outranks the series name and the b value: `isoDWI` is Trace, any other label is no Trace, `isoADC`, `FA` and `ExpAtt` are the ADC, FA and exponential ADC. It rests on what the labels mean, not on vendor documentation, and is to be revisited if the tool that writes them is documented.

  After updating, a reclassify applies the pack. No re-read and no fingerprint is needed: the fields it reads are the fingerprint's of revision 7.

## [1.0.0-alpha.78] - 2026-10-05

The MRI pack is 1.0.0, the certified pack: MRI pack 0.25.0's rules, unchanged, which passed the final header certificate of 2026-10-04 (7 of 5,000 clean stacks wrong, Korn-Graubard upper bound 0.26 % against 1 %, every make under 3 %). Released from a branch at the pack's merge, so it carries no later change.

### Changed

- The MRI pack is 1.0.0, the certified pack. Its rules are MRI pack 0.25.0's, byte for byte: only the version moves, so every verdict is the same and a classified stack names `mri@1.0.0` where it named `mri@0.25.0`. Pack 0.25.0 passed a final certificate on 2026-10-04: a fresh, sealed read of 5,000 stacks drawn from a production archive, graded once on the answers the pack had stored, with 7 of the 5,000 wrong (the one stack the read left out counted wrong) and a Korn-Graubard upper bound of 0.26 % against a bar of 1 %. Replayed over the 546,440 unsealed MR stacks of that archive, 1.0.0 gives the same answer as 0.25.0 on every axis of every stack. Nothing needs to run again after updating, though a reclassify writes the new version onto each stack's answer.

## [1.0.0-alpha.77] - 2026-10-04

A seal takes a saved selection of any size. `nils labels seal --select` froze its selection under an answer's cap and refused one of more than 5,000 stacks, so a certification sample larger than that could not be sealed in one piece; this release seals it whole. Nothing else changes: MRI pack 0.25.0, registry schema 79 (fingerprint revision 7), HTTP API contract 7, pack contract 8, review-item contract 4, suite contract 3 and job contract 1, as before. No reclassify and no re-read is needed after updating from alpha.76. No desk release: installs keep desk 1.0.0-alpha.62.

### Fixed

- `nils labels seal --select` seals a saved selection however many stacks it holds. It froze the selection under an answer's cap and refused one of more than 5,000 stacks, so a certification sample larger than that could not be sealed in one piece.

## [1.0.0-alpha.76] - 2026-10-04

A reclassify of a large archive should take about a third of the time it did (the step this release removes was about 70 % of it), and no longer grows from one run to the next. Classify read the open review questions of each chunk of stacks from Postgres, which walked every review item ever written; it now reads them once a run. Nothing else changes: after updating from alpha.75 nothing needs to run again, and the next reclassify is simply faster. Registry schema 79 (fingerprint revision 7), HTTP API contract 7, pack contract 8, review-item contract 4, suite contract 3 and job contract 1, as before. MRI pack 0.25.0. No desk release: installs keep desk 1.0.0-alpha.62.

### Fixed

- A reclassify no longer slows down as review items pile up. Before writing a window, classify supersedes the open questions of the stacks it judges again, and it used to ask Postgres for them per chunk of 256 stacks: the grouped question walked every review item ever written each time, and the per-stack one compared each chunk's refs with every open question, the run's own new ones included. On an archive of 558,874 stacks that was about 70 % of a 772 s run, and it grew with every reclassify. The open questions are now read once at the start of a run, by the stack they stand on, and each window supersedes its own by id. What is superseded is unchanged.

## [1.0.0-alpha.75] - 2026-10-03

A pass's target reads a field by pattern as a rule does. With MRI pack 0.25.0, whose physics vote reads a sequence variant and a series name by pattern, alpha.74's classify ended in a panic at the vote; this release fixes that, and nothing else changes. After updating from alpha.74, a reclassify is all that is needed: the re-read and the fingerprint alpha.74 asked for stand. Registry schema 79 (fingerprint revision 7), HTTP API contract 7, pack contract 8, review-item contract 4, suite contract 3 and job contract 1, as before. No desk release: installs keep desk 1.0.0-alpha.62.

### Fixed

- A pass's target may read a field by pattern (`matches`), as a rule may: the physics vote's reference keeps the pack's patterns, where a row of it panicked on the first pattern it met. MRI pack 0.25.0's vote reads a sequence variant and a series name so, and a classify with it ended in that panic.

## [1.0.0-alpha.74] - 2026-10-03

The MRI pack is 0.25.0, the fingerprint reads seven more fields, and classify is faster. MRI pack 0.25.0 carries conventions round 5 and round 6 and the rule gaps an archive-wide protocol read found; it reads the Philips TFE prepulse, which tells an MPRAGE from a turbo field echo where neither the sequence variant nor InversionRecovery does, and leaves the technique undecided where the file cannot tell. The fingerprint adds AngioFlag, Acquisition Contrast, the temporal resolution, the diffusion directionality and how the pixels are stored, and the MRI pack ingests Philips' Prepulse Type and Prepulse Delay. Classifying a stack takes about 30 % less time, and a process reuses a pack it loaded while its files are unchanged. Registry schema 79 and fingerprint revision 7: after updating, `nils digest <root> --reread-every --reread-one --reread-missing` for each root fills the new columns from one file per MR series, then `nils fingerprint` and a reclassify. No desk release: installs keep desk 1.0.0-alpha.62. HTTP API contract 7 (amended in place), pack contract 8, review-item contract 4, suite contract 3 and job contract 1. Kvasir 1.0.0-alpha.9 and the assistant 1.0.0-alpha.27, as before.

### Added

- A pack may read seven more fields of a stack (the 2026-10-03 fingerprint fields): `angio_flag` (AngioFlag), `acquisition_contrast` (AcquisitionContrast, from the MR Image Frame Type group where an enhanced object leaves the top level empty), `temporal_resolution` in milliseconds with `temporal_resolution_source` (TemporalResolution where the series writes it above nought, `header`, else the median interval between acquisitions of one slice position, `acquisition_times`), `diffusion_directionality` (every DiffusionDirectionality the stack's images write, `ISOTROPIC` on an enhanced trace image), and `photometric_interpretation` and `samples_per_pixel`, which tell a colour display composite from a map. The MRI pack ingests and shows Philips' TFE prepulse, `philips_prepulse_type` (2001,xx1C) and `philips_prepulse_delay` (2001,xx1B), which tell an MPRAGE from a TurboFLASH on a classic Philips 3D T1 TFE. A private element whose VR is CS is read in upper case, so `Inv` is `INV`. The reader shows the new fields. The fingerprint's revision moves to 7, so the next `nils fingerprint` derives every stack again and fills the angiography flag, the temporal resolution and the directionality from what the registry holds. Registry schema 79; the pack contract stays version 8, with fields added; the HTTP API contract stays version 7, amended in place.
- `nils digest <root> --reread-every --reread-one --reread-missing` fills the new series columns and the prepulse on a registry digested before them: every MR series whatever its manufacturer, one file of each series rather than every file (found a chunk of 500 series a query), and only the series whose row has no SamplesPerPixel yet, so a run stopped or repeated reads only what is still missing. `--reread-one` suits a value a series holds once; it does not see two files of a series disagree.
- `nils pack replay` names the pack's constraints each answer breaks (`broken`, each with its kind and id), as a classification reports them, so a pack's constraint breaks can be counted over an archive before a reclassify.

### Changed

- The MRI pack is 0.25.0: conventions round 5, six questions a final grade left, read the way published sources read them and checked against a production archive's counts. A quantitative T1 or R1 map is a quantity and has no base (an MP2RAGE T1 map no longer takes T1w; its UNI image and a synthetic T1-weighted image stay T1w), and old Philips acquisitions whose ImageType says `T1 MAP` are read as the weighted images they are. A GE SWAN output written one image per slice combines its echoes, comb-ME-GRE, and the `IP` of GE's `MIN IP` is no longer read as an in-phase image. A Siemens TWIST-VIBE dynamic run that names no angiography is DCE. A subtraction made from a stitched composite is SubtractionDerived with construct Composed, from its own words or, by a new session pass, from the composite of its geometry beside it. `MTC` in a name keeps giving MT where the header says nothing and the words do not switch it off. A quantitative T2 or R2 map has no base either. MRI pack 0.25.0 also carries the rule gaps a protocol sweep found: a reformat of a 3D exported by a workstation (DERIVED\SECONDARY, no series number) is a reformat; a GE FIESTA reformat is bSSFP by its flip; an MP2RAGE reformat takes MP2RAGE from its source, and Siemens FastView and GE three-plane series are localizers; two-echo Siemens FLASH components are ME-GRE and outputs named Combined comb-ME-GRE; a fast Siemens FLASH given contrast and named for a bolus is CE-MRA; velocity-encoded FLASH is a phase contrast; gradient-echo real and imaginary components have no base; Dixon part prefixes (DIXW, DIXF, GE IDEAL) give Water and Fat; GE's Average DC is MD, `ExpAtt:` eADC and `cFA:` FA; SyMRI's synthetic contrasts and split maps are read as what they are; a GE reversed b=0 reference is FieldmapRef; a technique word in a name never outranks the sequence (a FLAIR word on an MPRAGE, Cube on a GE 2D FSE); MTw from the token only on a gradient echo; a real inversion recovery (Siemens, or Philips `R_IR`) is PSIR; and several smaller readings. It builds on 0.24.0, below. A GE multi-echo QSM's maps no longer break the implication that an echo combination is T2*-weighted (63 stacks on a production reclassify of 0.24.0), and a position display named for an SWI series takes no SWI base. And it carries the answers to the questions the study left (research round 6): a Philips 3D TFE is no longer read as a turbo field echo from InversionRecovery NO, since its private prepulse is an inversion on every such stack read; one that states neither an inversion time nor `MP` is left undecided and asked, as is a GE reformat whose header is a BRAVO and whose name a Cube FLAIR; a Siemens name with `fs` as a word gives FatSat; a Philips registered diffusion copy stays a diffusion set with no Trace, as its source, since every image of it holds the directional images beside the isotropic one; the SWI image and minIP of a two-echo Siemens FLASH combine its echoes; an R2* map has no base; GE's multi-echo QSM outputs are read by the name GE stamps on them where the private pulse sequence is missing; a GE reversed reference is FieldmapRef up to b 50 and asked between 50 and 100; and the physics vote no longer fills a base the rules left empty on a quantitative map or a stack left undecided. With the fingerprint's new fields the pack reads the Philips TFE prepulse (an inversion is an MPRAGE, none a turbo field echo), AngioFlag (a TWIST-VIBE with an angio signal is a CE-MRA, an unnamed 3D FLASH angiogram a TOF), Acquisition Contrast where no word states a weighting, an isotropic DiffusionDirectionality as Trace, and a SyMRI stack saved in colour as a display composite, read as its screenshot is; each falls back to the reading before it where its field is empty. Stacks need a reclassify, and the new fields need the headers read again.
- The body-part descriptors pin the `nils-bodypart` 0.3.0 image that 1.0.0-alpha.73 published, a new build of the same sources. Its baked encoder weights verify with the same two digests as before, so those pins stay.
- Classifying a stack takes about 30 % less time, which more than takes back what MRI pack 0.24.0 added. A keyword clause's words are lowercased once, when the pack loads, and found in a stack's text by one automaton per text rather than searched for one by one, each lowercased again for every stack; and a stack's lowercased texts are kept while it is classified. The verdicts are the same, evidence, votes and diagnostics included, on the pack's 806 cases and on about 9,700 variants of them.
- A process that loads a pack it has loaded before, with every file it read unchanged, takes the pack it built then instead of building it and running its corpus again. The loader notes each file it read and the corpus folder's listing and reads them again to compare; any difference, and the pack is built afresh. The engine's doors that name a pack, `GET /api/capabilities` among them, ran the whole corpus on every request, about 0.2 s each.

## [1.0.0-alpha.73] - 2026-10-03

The engine keeps its port, and the MRI pack is 0.24.0. Out of open files, an engine failed an accept, its HTTP library ended the accept loop and dropped the listening socket, and the engine lived on answering nobody and logging nothing. An accept error is now never the end of the server, the engine bounds the files it opens, keeps a share of its handlers for every reader and for the doors that are not pictures, says what goes wrong on stderr, answers a health door, and under systemd's watchdog is restarted when it stops taking requests. `nils setup` and `nils update` write the engine's unit with `LimitNOFILE=65536`, `WatchdogSec=120` and `NotifyAccess=main`. MRI pack 0.24.0 fixes the T2* exclusion, the rule gaps a final grade of 0.23.0 found and reads a weighting the name states against the physics; stacks need a reclassify, and classify takes about 28 % longer with its larger flag set and two new session passes. No migration and no desk release: installs keep desk 1.0.0-alpha.62. Registry schema 78 (fingerprint revision 6), HTTP API contract 7, pack contract 8, review-item contract 4, suite contract 3 and job contract 1, as before. Kvasir 1.0.0-alpha.9 and the assistant 1.0.0-alpha.27, as before.

### Added

- `GET /api/health`, with no token and numbers only: `live` (requests are being taken), `ready` (live, and nine tenths of the open files are not in use), `why`, and the counts of the handlers, open files, tile reads, the slab and render caches, accept errors, panics and failed health probes. 200 when ready, 503 when not. It is answered by the thread that takes requests, so it answers while every handler is busy. The v7 contract lists it, in place.
- Under systemd's watchdog the engine says `READY=1` once it listens and feeds the watchdog only while its own health door, asked over its own socket, says it is live.

### Changed

- The MRI pack is 0.24.0. The exclusion that T2*-weighting is never a spin echo held on every stack of a production archive but 7, and on each the header's own readout contradicted one side. A gradient-echo scout is read by its readout and never by the words of the series it plans: an image typed LOCALIZER or SCOUT on a plain gradient echo, and now a Siemens position display (`POSDISP`) whose readout is a gradient echo, takes neither a diffusion nor a spin-echo technique from its name (a position display derived from a spin-echo image keeps its spin echo). A GE fast gradient echo by its pulse sequence (`fgre`, `fspgr`, `efgre3d`) takes no spin-echo technique from its name. A GE research-mode series named for a gradient echo is no longer read as a spin echo by its flip angle. The base's gradient-echo windows are not read on a stack the pack reads as a spin echo. Stacks need a reclassify.
- MRI pack 0.24.0 also carries the rule gaps a final grade of 0.23.0 on a fresh, archive-wide read found, each widened from the stack to its rule, and four disputes settled. A Siemens reformat of an inversion recovery reads its source beside it by a session pass: a real source makes it PSIR with Real, and a DANTE source black blood. GE: an SPGR name rules out TOF on `VASCTOF_GEMS`; an acquired MAGiC image of private type 2 is Real; `swi_ks` outputs are SWIRecon; one echo of a multi-echo QSM is ME-GRE and its combined outputs comb-ME-GRE; GE's own map names (the spelt-out exponential ADC, MCFA) are read as D3 reads them; a B0 map has no base; an EPI SWI is EPI. Philips and keywords: the vendor's sequence identity outranks a technique word in the name; `MToff`, a phase-encoding "phase" and "trace" on a b=0 image are no longer read as TOF, Phase and Trace; a SWIp projection is ProjectionDerived; `subtraktion` is a subtraction; `MAXIMUM` is a projection only by its geometry; a SmartExam patient-aligned reformat is a Localizer; `Du-IR` is DIR; a synthetic T1 FLAIR is T1w. Siemens: a named contrast-enhanced VIBE angiography is CE-MRA (a TWIST-VIBE dynamic run that names no angiography is left as it was); MOLLI's inline T1 map is a T1 map with no base; a MEAN magnitude over echoes keeps Magnitude; a `tirB` BLADE is Radial; an MPRAGE is read by its shot interval when it has no sequence name. Disputes: a GE `ksepi` 3D T1 is GRE-EPI; a Philips diffusion set with its directional images takes no Trace; a Siemens `Image Filter` copy written DERIVED is Filtered; a Siemens QSM-named acquired echo has no Magnitude. Stacks need a reclassify.
- MRI pack 0.24.0 reads a weighting the name states against the physics by a new convention (round 4): the name stays the default, and the physics overrides it only where it contradicts it outright. A STIR-window inversion named T1 is T2w; a FLAIR-window inversion named T1 at a long echo time, or on a 3D TSE, is T2w with FLAIR; a long-TR, long-TE spin echo without inversion named T1, a short-TR, short-TE spin echo named T2, and a short spoiled gradient echo named T2 are read by their physics. A T1-IR window keeps a T1 name (a 3D SPACE inversion recovery, a T1 FLAIR BLADE or PROPELLER, GE's T1 FLAIR option), a STIR named T2 keeps its name, DIR is left out of the inversion-time rules, and a 3D TSE's written echo time is never read as its contrast. Two new axes say how a base was read: `contrast_mix` (none, intermediate_PD_T2, T2like_T1, T1like_IR_longTE, PD_T2star) and `base_basis` (name, physics, name+physics, acq_contrast). They are described, not graded. The roles take no stack with a `contrast_mix` value. `nils pack derive` derives both beside the other axes; a question that lists the axes to derive must list `contrast_mix` with `role`.
- The engine's unit, as `nils setup` and `nils update` write it, sets `LimitNOFILE=65536`, `WatchdogSec=120` and `NotifyAccess=main`. The engine also raises its soft limit on open files to the hard one at start, and says on stderr when the limit stays below what its handlers may want.
- One thread takes every request from the socket and queues it. Pictures (the tiles, slab, render and thumb doors) wait per client and are taken client by client in turn, each at most an even share of the picture slots while others wait; a quarter of the handlers is kept for the doors that are not pictures. A client with 512 pictures waiting is answered 503 with `Retry-After`.
- Tile files are read in turns across the whole engine, a sixteenth of the open-file limit at once, from 16 to 256, each opened, read and closed within its turn. A tile read that fails for a reason other than a missing file is answered 503 with `Retry-After`, where it was 404.
- Accept errors, a handler's panic, pictures turned away, open files running short and a failed health probe are said on stderr, the journal, at most once per 10 s per kind.

### Fixed

- An accept error (out of open files, among others) no longer ends the accept loop and drops the listening socket: the loop pauses, from 10 ms doubling to 1 s, and accepts again. A connection whose socket cannot be duplicated, or whose thread will not start, is closed and the loop goes on, where the accept thread panicked. tiny_http 0.12.0 is kept with these changes under `engine/vendor/tiny_http`.
- A request handler that panics answers 500 and takes the next request, where it took its thread with it.
- The slab and render caches stay in use after a panic elsewhere left their lock poisoned.

## [1.0.0-alpha.72] - 2026-10-02

The reader's pictures come at once. The slab door read a slab's tiles one file after another, which on a network share took seconds a slab; it now reads them at once and keeps recent slabs in memory. A claim names the items that come next with the stacks each shows, so the desk warms their pictures while the item claimed is read (desk 1.0.0-alpha.62). No migration.

### Added

- A claim names the items that come next: `ahead`, up to five items the same claim would offer after this one as it stands now, each `{item, position, stacks}` with the stacks a reader shows for it (the item's own, an anchored item's panels in their order, a pair's left then right) and nothing else of them. `next` stays and is the first of them. A held lease names them too. The v7 contract's claim says so, in place.

### Changed

- The slab door reads a slab's tile files up to 16 at a time, and plane by plane the tiles door does the same; the bytes are as before.
- The slab door keeps recent slabs in memory, 256 MB, the oldest let go first, keyed by the pyramid's build like the render door's planes; the access checks and the audit row come first as before.
- The body-part descriptors pin the `nils-bodypart` 0.3.0 image that 1.0.0-alpha.71 published, a new build of the same sources. Its baked encoder weights verify with the same two digests as before, so those pins stay.

## [1.0.0-alpha.71] - 2026-10-02

The MRI pack is 0.23.0: the rule bugs a development grade of 0.22.0 found, and its dispute settled. The ADC or eADC map of a Siemens or Philips tensor acquisition is DTIRecon where only the source series beside it names the tensor, read by a second session pass, and a plain DWI beside it keeps the map RawRecon; the physics vote no longer fills a base the rules decide as nothing; a Siemens 3D-card range (DERIVED with PARALLEL or RADIAL) is a reformat whatever it is named; and a Siemens BLADE written `tseBNR` carries the restore, as `tseBR` does. Stacks need a reclassify; no re-read. No engine code change. The body-part descriptors pin the image 1.0.0-alpha.70 published; a change after this release pins them to the build it publishes. No desk release: installs keep desk 1.0.0-alpha.61. Registry schema 78 (fingerprint revision 6), HTTP API contract 7, pack contract 8, review-item contract 4, suite contract 3 and job contract 1, as before. Kvasir 1.0.0-alpha.9 and the assistant 1.0.0-alpha.27, as before.

### Changed

- The MRI pack is 0.23.0. It fixes the rule bugs a development grade of 0.22.0 found and settles its dispute. The ADC or eADC map of a Siemens or Philips tensor acquisition is DTIRecon where only the session names the tensor: a second session pass reads the source series beside the map (named for a tensor, mddw or six or more directions, or an FA made from it), of the same geometry and frame of reference, and leaves the map RawRecon where a plain DWI is beside it as well. The physics vote no longer fills a base the rules decide as nothing (a calibration, a phase contrast, a real or imaginary spin-echo component without an inversion, a spectroscopy, an excluded object and the other base-none rules). A Siemens 3D-card range (DERIVED with PARALLEL or RADIAL in its image type) is a reformat whatever it is named. A Siemens BLADE written `tseBNR` carries the restore as `tseBR` does. Stacks need a reclassify.
- The body-part descriptors pin the `nils-bodypart` 0.3.0 image that 1.0.0-alpha.70 published, a new build of the same sources. Its baked encoder weights verify with the same two digests as before, so those pins stay.

## [1.0.0-alpha.70] - 2026-10-01

The MRI pack is 0.22.0: the rule bugs a development grade of 0.21.0 found, and its two disputes settled. A Philips turbo field echo is also read from the MR module's PulseSequenceName that a re-exported object keeps, so a 3D T1 TFE whose module says no inversion is FSP-GRE there too; a raw Philips tensor acquisition takes no Trace from its private direction; an image typed LOCALIZER on a gradient echo takes no diffusion technique from its name; SmartExam's planning reformat is a planning scout; a STAGE echo takes the weighting its name states; and every Siemens MEAN output of a multi-echo SWI is a combined multi-echo gradient echo. No desk release: installs keep desk 1.0.0-alpha.61. Registry schema 78 (fingerprint revision 6), HTTP API contract 7, pack contract 8, review-item contract 4, suite contract 3 and job contract 1, as before. Kvasir 1.0.0-alpha.9 and the assistant 1.0.0-alpha.27, as before.

### Changed

- The MRI pack is 0.22.0. It fixes the rule bugs a development grade of 0.21.0 found and settles its two disputes. A Philips turbo field echo is also read from the PulseSequenceName of the MR module a re-exported classic object keeps, so a 3D T1 TFE whose module says no inversion is FSP-GRE there too. A Philips stack of six or more diffusion directions takes no Trace from its private direction, the isotropic image being a series of its own. An image the scanner typed LOCALIZER or SCOUT on a plain gradient echo takes no diffusion technique from its name or private technique. Philips SmartExam's planning reformat counts with SmartBrain as an automatic planning scout. An acquired STAGE echo named for PD or T1 takes that base. Every Siemens MEAN output of a multi-echo SWI, the SWI image and its projections included, is a combined multi-echo gradient echo. Stacks need a reclassify.
- The body-part descriptors pin the `nils-bodypart` 0.3.0 image that 1.0.0-alpha.69 published, a new build of the same sources. Its baked encoder weights verify with the same two digests as before, so those pins stay.

## [1.0.0-alpha.69] - 2026-10-01

The MRI pack is 0.21.0: the third round of published vendor conventions as ruled, checked against the archive's counts, three new values (provenance Calibration, constructs Speed and StdDevTime) and the rule bugs of a development grade. A vendor's localizer flag outranks the slice count, stitched composites are projections, GE's gradient-echo pulse sequences and fast recovery decide their technique, phase-contrast outputs and calibration scans have no base, and Philips mDixon TSE parts are TSEs. The engine reads the ImageType Philips writes per frame of an enhanced MR object, splits such an object's frames by the Dixon part they name, and ingests GE's series plane. Registry schema 78 adds the column to the stack and its fingerprint; the fingerprint's revision is 6. Stacks need a reclassify, and GE series a re-read for their series plane (`nils digest <root> --reread-exact <manufacturer>`). `nils pack replay` names the axes a rule decided as nothing. The body-part descriptors pin the `nils-bodypart` image 1.0.0-alpha.67 published; a change after this release pins them to the build it publishes.

### Added

- A pack may read the ImageType Philips writes per frame of an enhanced MR object in its (2005,140F) private sequence as `private_frame_image_type`, whose third and fourth values name a Dixon part (W, F, IP or OP), and the frames of one such object that name different parts are now a stack per part; frames that differ there otherwise, as a magnitude and a phase image do, stay one stack, and every other file keeps its stack key, a classic image and a Siemens enhanced object read the field empty, and a split by it is `image_type_variation`. Registry schema 78 adds the column to the stack and the fingerprint, empty, and the fingerprint's revision moves to 6.

### Changed

- The MRI pack is 0.21.0. It follows the third round of published vendor conventions as ruled, checked against the archive's counts, and fixes the rule bugs of a development grade. A vendor's localizer flag outranks the slice count: GE's series plane 256 (now read from the file), ImageType LOCALIZER, SCOUT or POSDISP, the vendors' automatic planning scouts and a series split into planes are localizers whatever their slices, and otherwise a planning word makes a localizer of at most 5 slices a plane. A stitched composite (MobiView, Siemens Inline Composing) is ProjectionDerived with Composed, a composite of survey stations a localizer. Philips' MAXIMUM is a MIP only where the geometry shows a projection, and a Siemens CSA MPR or CSA MIP image is read by its own image type whatever its series is named. GE's VASCPC_GEMS and phase-contrast pulse sequences are phase contrast, a GE collapse of a 2D phase contrast is the acquisition, Philips' M_PCA is Magnitude and its PCA angiogram Speed, and no phase-contrast output has a base. A calibration scan has provenance Calibration and no base. GE's fgre without SP is SS-GRE, an efgre3d without SP GRE and fspgr FSP-GRE; GE's FR_GEMS decides the flip-back TSE, an inversion first, and an FRFSE without it is a TSE; T1FLAIR_GEMS gives FLAIR over the CSF null. A Philips mDixon TSE is a TSE whatever ScanningSequence says, and its per-frame Dixon part is read. A Philips 3D T1 TFE whose module says no inversion is FSP-GRE. Siemens' Dixon parts are SP-GRE, a Philips Reg - diffusion copy is MoCo, PROMO is no MoCo, GE MUSE and Philips IRIS are multi-shot EPI, the EPIMix T1 FLAIR is an EPI, a T2-named gradient echo is T2*w at any echo time, and a real or imaginary spin-echo component without PSIR evidence has no base. Stacks need a reclassify, and GE series a re-read for their series plane.
- The MRI pack's vocabulary gains provenance `Calibration` (a coil-sensitivity, receive-field or prescan reference, not for reading) and constructs `Speed` (a phase-contrast angiogram, neither a signed velocity nor a magnitude) and `StdDevTime` (the standard deviation over a series' measurements), each with BIDS tokens and the pack's base, disposition and projection rules.
- `nils pack replay` names the axes a rule decided as nothing (`none`), which an empty value alone cannot tell from an axis no rule reached.

## [1.0.0-alpha.68] - 2026-10-01

Sequence names are shown everywhere, at every detail. The quasi rule of 1.0.0-alpha.66 is withdrawn: the standard SequenceName and PulseSequenceName, GE's two pulse sequence names and Philips' scanning technique are shown at plain as at quasi and sensitive, in the reader's documents, the header door, a campaign batch's signature and the ask doors. The MRI pack is 0.20.2, which drops the `quasi` marks; no stack's values change, and no reclassify is needed. Identifiers, identifier-shaped values and dates stay withheld as before. The body-part descriptors pin the `nils-bodypart` image 1.0.0-alpha.67 published; if this release publishes a new build, a change after it pins them to that one.

### Changed

- Sequence names are shown everywhere, at every detail. The standard SequenceName and PulseSequenceName, GE's pulse sequence name and internal pulse sequence name, and Philips' scanning technique are no longer held back below detail quasi: not in the reader's documents (`header`, a clause's header, the line, `texts` and `private`, blind or not), the header door, which no longer counts them under `left_out.below_detail`, a campaign batch's `signature`, nor the ask doors, where `series.sequence_name` is now a technical field (federated, as `stack.text_sequence_name` already was). A packet built from these carries them too. The `quasi` mark that 1.0.0-alpha.66 let a pack put on a `private.shown` entry is withdrawn: the engine ignores it, and `nils pack show --json` no longer names it. The MRI pack is 0.20.2, without the marks. Identification blocks and values shaped like an identifier are still withheld, dates are withheld as before, and the other quasi-identifying texts stay at detail quasi and above. The HTTP API contract stays version 7 and pack contract 8, both amended in place.
- The body-part descriptors pin the `nils-bodypart` 0.3.0 image that 1.0.0-alpha.67 published, a new build of the same sources. Its baked encoder weights verify with the same two digests as before, so those pins stay.

## [1.0.0-alpha.67] - 2026-10-01

The MRI pack is 0.20.1, a fix to 0.20.0 found by its first reclassify of an archive: a Philips SWIp image of combined echoes, and its MIP and minIP, no longer break the pack's own implication that a combined multi-echo gradient echo is T2*-weighted, so they no longer raise a `classify.implied` item or have their base and technique written below every threshold. No stack's values change; after the update, a reclassify with the new pack clears the items 0.20.0 raised on them. The body-part descriptors pin the `nils-bodypart` 0.3.0 image that 1.0.0-alpha.66 published; if this release publishes a new build of the image, a change after it pins them to that one. No desk release comes with it: installs keep desk 1.0.0-alpha.61. The HTTP API contract and the pack contract now say, amended in place, what 1.0.0-alpha.66 already does: a vendor's sequence name is shown at detail quasi and above only. The registry stays at schema 77; the HTTP API contract stays version 7, the review-item contract version 4, the pack contract version 8, the suite contract version 3 and the job contract version 1. An install fetches Kvasir 1.0.0-alpha.9 and the assistant 1.0.0-alpha.27, as with 1.0.0-alpha.66. It carries kineuro/nils#330, #331 and #332.

### Fixed

- The MRI pack is 0.20.1. A Philips SWIp image of combined echoes, and its MIP and minIP, no longer break the pack's own implication that a combined multi-echo gradient echo (comb-ME-GRE) is T2*-weighted. Pack 0.20.0 made these outputs comb-ME-GRE, as ruled, while the SWI route keeps their base SWI, so the first reclassify of an archive with 0.20.0 raised a `classify.implied` item and wrote the base and technique below every threshold on each of them (299 stacks of one archive). The base rule for comb-ME-GRE now leaves out an image whose construct is SWI, MIP or MinIP; no other comb-ME-GRE stack of that archive carries one, and no stack's values change.

### Changed

- The body-part descriptors pin the `nils-bodypart` 0.3.0 image that 1.0.0-alpha.66 published, a new build of the same sources. Its baked encoder weights verify with the same two digests as before, so those pins stay.

## [1.0.0-alpha.66] - 2026-10-01

The engine reads what the header could not say before: an enhanced MR object's MR Pulse Sequence module and the SOP class decide its technique, a spectroscopy object is MRS by its SOP class, a session pass decides a stack its own header leaves open from the other stacks of its session, and a person reading a stack is shown the private elements the served pack lists, a vendor's sequence name among them at detail quasi and above only. `nils pack replay` says what a pack decides of each stack from its header packet, offline. The MRI pack is 0.20.0 on pack contract 8, carrying 0.18.1 and 0.19.0: it settles nine questions of a development grade and fixes that grade's rule bugs. After updating, run `nils fingerprint` (the fingerprint's revision moves to 5, so every stack is derived again and the SOP class and phase contrast fill at once), read the enhanced objects' files again with `nils digest <root> --reread <manufacturer>` where a manufacturer has them, fingerprint again, and classify with the new pack. The release publishes a new build of the body-part image; the body-part descriptors in this tag pin the image 1.0.0-alpha.65 published, and a change after it pins them to the new one. No desk release comes with it: installs keep desk 1.0.0-alpha.61, which shows the new header texts and does not yet show `private`. The registry is at schema 77; the HTTP API contract stays version 7 and the review-item contract version 4, each amended in place; the pack contract is version 8, and an engine at 8 loads a contract-7 pack unchanged; the suite contract stays version 3 and the job contract version 1. An install fetches Kvasir 1.0.0-alpha.9 and the assistant 1.0.0-alpha.27, as with 1.0.0-alpha.65. It carries kineuro/nils#322, #323, #324, #325, #326, #327 and #328.

### Added

- A person reading a stack is shown the private elements the served pack lists (record 53): the reader's document and the header door carry `private`, by the pack's name for each element, and `private_withheld`, how many were held back because the value was shaped like an identifier (a personnummer or a date, a UID, an address, more than 64 characters). The list is an allowlist in the pack's private file (`shown`), and an element it does not name is never read; a campaign of the pictures alone shows none. A vendor's sequence name is shown at detail quasi and above only, as the sequence name is: an element the pack's `shown` marks `quasi` (the MRI pack marks GE's two pulse sequence names and Philips' scanning technique), and the standard PulseSequenceName, where Siemens XA writes its sequence name, are left out below it in the reader's documents and the header door, and `nils pack show --json` says which are. The MRI pack shows GE's pulse sequence names, ASL contrast technique and private image type and Philips' scanning technique and diffusion direction. The HTTP API contract stays version 7, amended in place.
- `nils pack replay <pack> [--input FILE|DIR]` says what a pack decides of each stack from its header packet, offline: the reader's packet (`texts`, `sequence`, `physics`, `geometry` and `private`) or a flat `fields` object, one a line or one a file, with the session list, so the rules that read a private element and the session pass act as in production; the physics vote reads the whole registry and is not replayed. `nils pack show --json` says what a packet must carry for it: the elements shown, with the address a packet builder reads each at, and what the session pass reads of each other stack.
- The pack contract is version 8 (record 53). No manifest key changes. A passes file may declare a pass of kind `session_context`, which decides a stack whose own header does not say which output of an acquisition it is from the other stacks of its session: only its rules read the session, each sibling seen by its header through the fields the pass names (`session.sibling_fields`), by a related series number, the same geometry and the same frame of reference, never by what was decided of it. It never replaces a value a header flag or number decided, a person's answer or a decision; a value read from the name, inferred or defaulted only at or below `replaces_at_most`. Every value it writes has tier and basis `session`, its evidence names the pass, the rule and the sibling stacks, and each answer is also a review item of kind `<axis>:session`, whatever the pass's `emit` says. A pick's `fallback` border may name several values. A private file may list the ingested elements a reader is shown (`shown`); the loader refuses one that is not ingested, not of a technical kind or at an identification block. An engine at 8 loads a contract-7 pack unchanged and refuses these keys in a pack that declares less. The HTTP API contract stays version 7, amended in place with the basis `session`.
- The MRI pack is 0.18.1, and 0.19.0 below carries it. A Siemens position display (`POSDISP`) is no longer read as T2*-weighted by the name it carries, which is the name of the series it plans. Pack 0.18.0 read a gradient echo named T2 as T2* from an echo time of 8 ms, and position displays at 12 to 15 ms began to break the exclusion `t2star-not-spin-echo` beside the TSE their words name. Every such stack is a localizer; nothing else changes.
- The MRI pack is 0.19.0, on pack contract 8. An enhanced object's MR Pulse Sequence module decides its technique by the standard's mapping table (a spin or gradient echo, EPI, a train, single shot, the steady state, spoiling, time of flight, phase contrast, arterial spin labelling, radial or spiral k-space and inversion recovery); an MR Spectroscopy Storage object is MRS by its SOP class; a session pass takes an unprefixed GE SWAN image the SWI route could only call the processed image as the acquired magnitude where GE wrote a computed output of the same acquisition beside it, at 0.65, so each is asked about; the `epimix_fallback` border names NeuroMix beside EPIMix; and GE's pulse sequence names, ASL technique and private image type and Philips' scanning technique and diffusion direction are the private elements a reader is shown. A classic image reads as before. Stacks need a reclassify.
- The MRI pack is 0.20.0, still on pack contract 8. It settles nine questions a development grade raised, by a deep research and the archive's counts, and fixes that grade's rule bugs. A Philips FFE is read by its spoiling, its private scanning technique and then SequenceVariant, never by "T1W" in its name (`T1FFE` is SP-GRE, `FFE` SS-GRE). One echo of a Philips multi-echo FFE written on its own is ME-GRE, and comb-ME-GRE is only the combined image, as a Philips SWIp output of a multi-echo acquisition is. A Siemens inversion recovery reconstructed as the real part is PSIR. The ADC or eADC of a Siemens or Philips multi-direction DTI is DTIRecon, and GE's AvDC is DTIRecon with MD while its ADC stays RawRecon. A MEAN phase or magnitude of a multi-echo SWI is the acquisition's echoes combined, RawRecon, and a Siemens MEAN phase or magnitude over echoes is comb-ME-GRE. A series of at most five slices a plane named for planning is a localizer. A b=0 run named by its phase-encoding direction is a field-map reference, unless its own sign says otherwise. GE's `FL:` image is Filtered where written DERIVED only. A derived motion-corrected DSC run is RawRecon with MoCo; only the maps are PerfusionRecon. Also: a single-voxel result named MRS has no base; a Siemens Dixon FLASH without a Dixon token is told in-phase or opposed-phase by its echo time; a FLAIR name at a short repetition time with no inversion is no FLAIR; GE research-mode spin and gradient echoes are read by their train and names; GE's DTI outputs on a rewritten header are diffusion images; a turbo spin echo's letters inside another word no longer outrank a Siemens gradient-echo sequence name; Siemens' `mIP_Images` is a minimum projection; a survey named `ÖSIKT` is a localizer; and GE's Inhance 3D Velocity is a phase contrast. Stacks need a reclassify.
- A pack may read what an enhanced MR object and an MR spectroscopy object say of how they were acquired, and which kind of object a file is (record 53). The MR Pulse Sequence module's mechanism attributes, EchoPulseSequence (0018,9008), MultipleSpinEcho (0018,9011), EchoPlanarPulseSequence (0018,9018), SteadyStatePulseSequence (0018,9017), TimeOfFlightContrast (0018,9015), ArterialSpinLabelingContrast (0018,9250), GeometryOfKSpaceTraversal (0018,9032) and SegmentedKSpaceTraversal (0018,9033), are read from the top level of the file or the private per-frame sequences, and Spoiling (0018,9016) and InversionRecovery (0018,9009) from the MR Modifier functional group as well. The digest keeps them on the MR series; the fingerprint carries them, the phase contrast the series already held, and the series' SOP class, and a pack reads them under their own names (`echo_pulse_sequence`, `sop_class_uid` and so on). A classic image writes none of them, and they read empty there. The reader's `texts` shows each where the file has it. The fingerprint's revision moves to 5, so the next `nils fingerprint` derives every stack again and the SOP class and the phase contrast fill at once; the new series columns fill when the files are read again (`nils digest <root> --reread <manufacturer>`). Registry schema 77; the pack contract stays version 7, with fields added; the HTTP API contract stays version 7, amended in place.

### Changed

- The body-part descriptors pin the `nils-bodypart` 0.3.0 image that 1.0.0-alpha.65 published, so `bodypart-infer-fusion` 0.3.0 runs the code its descriptor describes. Its baked encoder weights verify with the same two digests as before, so those pins stay (kineuro/nils#323).

## [1.0.0-alpha.65] - 2026-09-30

Pipeline runs whose stacks do not meet go on side by side within the lane's one budget, with the steps that write what every run shares taken one run at a time under a registry lock, and a job whose own process still runs on this machine is never taken over as stale. The body-part image is `nils-bodypart` 0.3.0: `bodypart-infer-fusion` is about ten times faster on a network file system, with the same answers, and its descriptor asks for 16 cores and 8 GB. This release publishes that image; the body-part descriptors in this tag still pin the image 1.0.0-alpha.63 published, and a change after it pins them to the new one. No desk release comes with it; installs keep desk 1.0.0-alpha.61. The registry stays at schema 76; the HTTP API contract stays version 7, the suite contract version 3, the pack contract version 7 and the job contract version 1. An install fetches Kvasir 1.0.0-alpha.9 and the assistant 1.0.0-alpha.27, as with 1.0.0-alpha.64. It carries kineuro/nils#319 and #320.

### Changed

- Pipeline runs whose stacks do not meet go on side by side. A run that would take a stack a running run holds is refused, as every second run was before, and the refusal names the run that holds it, its job and how many of the stacks it holds. The lane's cores and memory and the card's lease are held across every run on the machine, read from the running units' rows, and the units of a run whose engine went away hold nothing. The steps that write what every run shares are taken one run at a time under a registry lock (an advisory lock on Postgres, a file lock beside the database on SQLite): the intake of a run's proposals into review groups and the supersede of what an earlier run of the model left untaken, the review items, measures and audit epoch that close a run, a model a run registers, an encoder an embedding names, the release of a bids input and the build of an apptainer image. A run beats its job's heart from a thread of its own while it goes on, and a claim of any kind no longer fails as stale a job whose own process still runs on this machine (a process that started after the job's last beat is not its own), so a long intake is never taken over. `nils run --resume`, `nils jobs cancel` and the pipeline lane's worker, which takes up an interrupted run three times at most, work as before; `--chunk` still runs its parts one after another. The registry stays at schema 76 (kineuro/nils#319).
- `bodypart-infer-fusion` is about ten times faster on a network file system, with the same answers. The `nils-bodypart` image is 0.3.0: `infer-fusion` scores stacks in worker processes instead of threads that shared one core, holds numpy's BLAS to one thread in each (a run of 0.2.0 spent about three quarters of its CPU in BLAS threads with nothing to do), and reads the next stack's files ahead. The descriptor asks for 16 cores and 8 GB and runs 16 workers by default. On a network file system a run is then held by how fast its files can be read: about ten stacks a second, where a run of 0.2.0 scored one. The tables and proposals are byte for byte those of 0.2.0 with one BLAS thread. With 0.2.0's default of one BLAS thread per core, the last bits of a matrix product depended on the machine's core count, which moved a few stacks' probabilities in the fourth decimal; 0.3.0 gives the same numbers on any number of cores. The image can also decode lossless JPEG 2000 on a CUDA card (`--device cuda`) with the same samples, and run the image encoder there in batches (`--encoder-device cuda`), both off unless asked; the descriptor still asks for no card. Over 4,900 stacks compared with 0.2.0 at 64 BLAS threads, 8 stacks' probabilities moved by at most 0.001 and no value or answer changed. The descriptor pins the image of 1.0.0-alpha.63 until the release that publishes 0.3.0 repins it (kineuro/nils#320).

## [1.0.0-alpha.64] - 2026-09-30

The MRI pack is 0.18.0, still on pack contract 7: it fixes the rule bugs a sealed checkpoint showed, follows the published vendor conventions of a new deep research where the archive's counts and the vocabulary agree, and adds the eight values the research found missing (technique `CE-MRA`, `MRS` and `ZTE`, provenance `NeuroMix`, construct `PBP`, `K2`, `Velocity` and `StdDevProj`). Stacks keep their values until they are classified again with the new pack; answers, decisions and campaigns that name an old value are unchanged. The body-part descriptors pin the image 1.0.0-alpha.63 published. No desk release comes with it: the desk takes its value lists from the pack, and installs keep desk 1.0.0-alpha.61. The registry stays at schema 76; the HTTP API contract stays version 7, the suite contract version 3, the pack contract version 7 and the job contract version 1. An install fetches Kvasir 1.0.0-alpha.9 and the assistant 1.0.0-alpha.27, as with 1.0.0-alpha.63. It carries kineuro/nils#315, #316 and #317.

### Changed

- The MRI pack is 0.18.0. It fixes the rule bugs a sealed checkpoint showed and follows the published vendor conventions of a new deep research, checked against the archive's counts. Siemens `epse` and `epir` are spin-echo EPIs whatever ScanningSequence says. GE's `VASCTOF_GEMS` is TOF only without contrast, multiphase or an SPGR name; FIESTA-C is CISS; GE's named SPGR and TRICKS with `FAST_GEMS` are FSP-GRE, its unspoiled `SS\SK` echo SS-GRE, and `MRF_GEMS` alone is no MRF. Philips' `PCA` is phase contrast, `s3DI` an inflow TOF and its QMap phase images MDME. DSC maps and a derived MoCo DSC are PerfusionRecon, a DSC named for susceptibility is never SWI, and a single DSC volume is T2*w. The MAVRIC composite and Elscint's `ENHANCED` take no construct, Siemens' `EXP` is eADC, Philips' isotropic image is Trace and RawRecon, and TTEST on an ASL output is no t-test map. An inversion recovery between 300 and 1,400 ms is FLAIR only near the TI that nulls CSF at its TR. Enhanced objects without ScanningSequence, GE headers rewritten with numbers, MobiView, SCOUT, standard deviation projections, subtractions named SUB and several names read as what they do not mean are handled. Stacks need a reclassify.
- The MRI pack's vocabulary gains the values its conventions had no word for: technique `CE-MRA` (contrast-enhanced angiography, single phase or time resolved), `MRS` (spectroscopy) and `ZTE` (a zero echo time radial readout, GE's Silent MRA among them); provenance `NeuroMix`, apart from EPIMix; and construct `PBP` (percentage of baseline at peak) and `K2` (leakage) for DSC maps, `Velocity` for a phase contrast's velocity image and `StdDevProj` for a standard deviation projection. Series that were filed under the nearest value before (TRICKS and TWIST as spoiled gradient echoes, NeuroMix as EPIMix) move to their own. Answers and campaigns that name the old values are unchanged; stacks need a reclassify.
- The body-part descriptors pin the `nils-bodypart` image that 1.0.0-alpha.63 published, a new build of the same 0.2.0 sources. Its baked encoder weights verify with the same two digests as before, so those pins stay (kineuro/nils#315).

## [1.0.0-alpha.63] - 2026-09-30

The MRI pack is 0.17.0, on pack contract 7: a pick raises v0's nine reasons for a look where it raised three, the main pick carries v0's MP2RAGE preference, and a new role `t2w` names an anatomical T2w that is not a FLAIR. The pack's 0.16.0 fixes of a fourth development reference come with it. A model that disagrees with a person's decision is shown for review and never applied, keeping the run's pick on a pick border is a person's decision that stands through later pick runs, and no door decides an item an open campaign holds. segcsvd is a starter, and a bids run reads what an earlier one made. Reclassify with the new pack to apply it. After updating, the next `nils pick run` raises more `pick.border` review items than before: the six new border reasons and the new role raise their own. That is expected, and a person's pick stands through them. Released with desk 1.0.0-alpha.61, which follows these doors. The registry stays at schema 76; the HTTP API contract stays version 7, amended in place with the additive disagreement items and the pack door's `picks`; the suite contract stays version 3; the pack contract is version 7, so an engine at 6 refuses the new MRI pack; the job contract stays version 1. An install fetches Kvasir 1.0.0-alpha.9 and the assistant 1.0.0-alpha.27, as with 1.0.0-alpha.62. It carries kineuro/nils#308, #309, #310, #311, #312 and #313.

### Added

- A pick raises v0's nine reasons for a look, where it raised three (record 51 R6). Beside `too_close`, `rare` and `nothing_eligible`, the MRI pack's main pick now declares v0's other six, each defined as v0 computed it and with v0's number: `retake` (the winner holds more than one stack of one acquisition, counted after v0's partial-volume demotion, so a stack under half the largest slice count of 60 or more is set aside; a Dixon keeping more than one stack of its canonical output, an MP2RAGE more than two), `unknown_dim` (no acquisition type), `slice_count_outlier` (a largest slice count strictly outside the 5th to 95th percentile of its dimension's population), `pre_post_twin` (another candidate at least 0.85 of the winner's score whose contrast shares none with the winner's; a border only, where v0 also made the twin a main), `epimix_fallback` (a stack of the winner is an EPIMix) and `dixon_vs_plain` (a Dixon won and a candidate that is neither a Dixon nor water-excited is within a tenth). A pack that does not declare a reason never raises it. The `pick.border` item's evidence and the pick's `parts` carry `notes`, what each border found (the retake's variant, the twin's or the plain candidate's stacks, the slice count and its bounds), and `nils pick explain` prints them (kineuro/nils#311).
- A new role, `t2w`: an anatomical T2w acquisition that is not a FLAIR (record 51 R8). The main pick scores it on technique and modifier tables of its own, which are ours and not v0's: a 3D turbo spin echo first, then the 2D one, a balanced steady state near the floor. A release takes a `t2w` pick as the session's `_T2w`, and a pipeline may name it under `x-nils.input.roles` (kineuro/nils#311).
- The pack contract is version 7 (record 51). No manifest key changes. A pick file gains the six border keys and a list of families, each with a `name`, what a family holding none of its canonical outputs becomes (`without_canonical: drop` or `apart`) and how many kept stacks make a retake (`retake_above`). A role that a component with per-role tables scores must have a table of its own there, and is refused at load otherwise, and a border key the engine does not know is refused rather than skipped. An engine at 7 loads a contract-6 pack unchanged; the new keys in a pack that declares less than 7 are refused, so an engine at 6 refuses a pack that uses them ("the pack wants contract 7") instead of picking without them (kineuro/nils#311).
- segcsvd is a starter (record 49 R1's fourth analysis): white-matter hyperintensities on each session's FLAIR and enlarged perivascular spaces on its T1w, a mask and a probability map for each and their volumes in mm3 as a table, from the session's SynthSeg label map. The image is segcsvd rc03 as its authors publish it on Hugging Face (GPL-3.0), pinned by the digest of a public registry copy whose layers are that archive's (kineuro/nils#312).
- A model that disagrees with a person's decision is shown, never applied (record 51 R5). Where a pipeline run proposes, at or above the model card's threshold, another value than a person's decision in force on a stack, it raises an `<axis>:decision` review item on that stack, with the model, its value and its confidence as the evidence (`source: model`). Nothing is staged and the person's decision stays in force. There is one open such item per stack, axis and model: a newer run of the model refreshes it, or closes it as superseded once it agrees, and one a campaign asks is left to it, with none raised beside it. Below the threshold, or against an agent's decision, the proposal is only counted, as before. The run's `proposals.ingested` says `disagreements` and `agreed_again`; the HTTP API contract stays version 7, amended in place (kineuro/nils#310).
- `GET /api/packs/{name}` and `nils pack show --json` list the pack's `picks`, each with its name and the roles it picks for, so the desk offers the served pack's roles (record 51). The HTTP API contract stays version 7, amended in place (kineuro/nils#310).

### Changed

- The MRI pack is 0.16.0. It fixes the rule errors of a fourth development reference. A Siemens BLADE named only by its sequence stem (`*tseB`) and GE's `PROPks` are Radial. A derived image of a 3D acquisition named for another plane than its protocol is a reformat (ProjectionDerived, MPR). A dual-echo spin echo that only its name calls FLAIR, with no inversion in its header, is a TSE whose echo decides its base. A Siemens acquired diffusion image takes no ADC from a protocol named for its ADC map. GE IDEAL's `OutPhase:` output is OutPhase. Stacks need a reclassify (kineuro/nils#309).
- The MRI pack is 0.17.0 and declares pack contract 7. Its main pick carries v0's MP2RAGE preference, which the pack did not carry before: an MP2RAGE's images are one candidate, its UniformDenoised image, else its Uniform image, and where neither is labelled its acquisitions stand apart as before. Expect more `pick.border` items at the next `nils pick run`: the six new reasons and the new role raise their own. A person's pick stands through them as before (kineuro/nils#311).
- Keeping the run's pick is a decision. Accepting a `pick.border` item (`nils review accept`, `POST /api/review/{id}/accept`, the desk's Keep) writes a person's pick of the stacks the run picked on the item's occasion, with the person and a why (theirs, or "kept the run's pick" naming the borders), and closes the item as accepted with that pick. Like any person's pick it stands through later `nils pick run`s, which raise no border on the occasion, until a person withdraws it; the audit holds `pick.set` beside `review.accept`. Where the run picked nothing (`nothing_eligible`), the kept pick names no stack: the ask and a release of the picks read it as no pick, `nils pick list` and `nils pick explain` say that no stack stands there, and the pre-flight names the person's pick as why a unit misses the role. `--pick` (`pick_id` at the door) names the run's pick the person read, and a keep is refused when a run has picked again since. An agent or a model does not keep a pick (403 at the door). A `pick.border` item's evidence names the session scheme and the pack the run looked under (kineuro/nils#313).
- No door decides or accepts an item a campaign holds. `nils review apply` and `nils review decide` (a value, several values, `--nothing`, with or without `--stage`) and `nils review accept` refuse a review item an open campaign asks, in the door's words, before anything is written, and `POST /api/review/{id}/accept` refuses it with 409 as the apply doors do. A staged decision on an axis an open campaign asks of a stack it reaches stays staged until the campaign closes: `nils review commit` by filter or `--all` leaves it and counts it (`left_held`), and a commit that names it is refused (kineuro/nils#313).
- A pipeline's `derivative:<kind>` input also takes the derivatives of the selection's sessions and subjects, which a run of the bids layout registers, beside those of its stacks. One bids run now reads what an earlier one made, as segcsvd reads SynthSeg's label maps (kineuro/nils#312).
- The SynthSeg starter's memory need is 24 GB, up from 12: robust SynthSeg on a 1 mm T1w was killed under a 12 GB container cap, and the unit was still counted as succeeded without its label map. A SynthSeg unit whose `mri_synthseg` fails, or that writes no label map, now fails (kineuro/nils#312).
- The body-part descriptors pin the `nils-bodypart` image that 1.0.0-alpha.62 published, a new build of the same 0.2.0 sources. Its baked encoder weights verify with the same two digests as before, so those pins stay (kineuro/nils#308).

## [1.0.0-alpha.62] - 2026-09-30

The MRI pack is 0.15.0: it follows the rulings on 0.14.0's disputes and fixes the rule errors of a third development reference, with a new technique `DWI-TSE` for a diffusion read out by a turbo spin echo. Reclassify with the new pack to apply it. The engine's code is unchanged since 1.0.0-alpha.61, and no desk release comes with it: desk 1.0.0-alpha.59 stays the newest. The registry stays at schema 76; the HTTP API contract stays version 7, the suite contract version 3, the pack contract version 6 and the job contract version 1. The body-part descriptors pin the `nils-bodypart` image that 1.0.0-alpha.61 published, a new build of the same 0.2.0 sources with the same encoder weights. An install fetches Kvasir 1.0.0-alpha.9 and the assistant 1.0.0-alpha.27, as with 1.0.0-alpha.61.

### Changed

- The MRI pack is 0.15.0. It follows the rulings on 0.14.0's disputes and fixes the rule errors of a third development reference. A new technique, `DWI-TSE`, names a diffusion read out by a turbo spin echo (Philips DWI-TSE, a PROPELLER, BLADE or MultiVane diffusion, a HASTE diffusion), with base DWI, family SE and the BIDS token `DWITSE`; a Siemens BLADE diffusion is no longer read as an EPI. A GE spin echo not split by echo with a stated train of 2 is a TSE. A Philips diffusion image with a positive b value written `PROJECTION IMAGE` is the trace. A balanced steady-state scan (TrueFISP, FIESTA, balanced FFE, CISS) has base T2w by convention, standing for its T2/T1 contrast, except a 2D one at a flip of 10 degrees or less (PDw) and a magnetisation-prepared one. A GE SWI in research mode with a train of 32 or more is an EPI. GE's GRASS and MPGR are SS-GRE, a gradient echo at a long repetition time, a low flip and an echo time of 12 ms or more is T2*w, GE's MAGiC acquisition named `Qmap` is an MDME in the SyMRI route, and an old Siemens inflow angiography named `tun_s` is a TOF. Stacks need a reclassify.

## [1.0.0-alpha.61] - 2026-09-30

The MRI pack is 0.14.0: it fixes the rule errors of a fresh development reference on the five header axes of the certificate and undoes two regressions of 0.13.0. A pipeline run takes a selection of any size, and `nils run --chunk N` runs a large one in parts. A campaign may show its raters the pictures alone (`--hide-header`), for single reads where the header could give the answer away, and anchored reading reads a candidate stack beside a pre and a post anchor of the same subject for the post-contrast gold (`nils campaign anchored`). Reclassify with the new pack to apply it. Released with desk 1.0.0-alpha.59, which reads a campaign's `hide_header` and the anchored doors. The registry is at schema 76 (migrations 75 and 76). The HTTP API contract stays version 7, amended in place with the additive `hide_header` and anchored doors; the suite contract stays version 3; the pack contract stays version 6; the job contract stays version 1. The body-part descriptors pin the `nils-bodypart` image 0.2.0 that 1.0.0-alpha.60 published, with the same encoder weights. An install fetches Kvasir 1.0.0-alpha.9 and the assistant 1.0.0-alpha.27, as with 1.0.0-alpha.60.

### Added

- `nils run --chunk N` runs a pipeline over a selection in parts of at most N stacks, in stack order, each part frozen into a handle of its own and run as a run of its own, one after another. Where a part fails, the error names the handles of the parts not run.
- A campaign may show its raters the pictures alone: `nils campaign create --hide-header`, or `hide_header: true` on `POST /api/campaigns`, for an axis or an axes question that suggests none. Its evidence door serves no text of the file's header (no series description, sequence name or physics) and its whole-header door is refused with 409, as in pair mode. Every campaign says its `hide_header`. Schema 75 adds the campaign's column; a campaign from before shows the header as it did. The HTTP API contract stays version 7, with `hide_header` added in place.
- Anchored reading for the post-contrast gold. `nils campaign anchored <name> --items FILE --seed TEXT` makes a campaign whose items are a candidate stack beside a pre anchor and a post anchor of the same subject: one item a line, three stack ids, or a header that names `candidate`, `pre_anchor` and `post_anchor` in any order (other columns are skipped). An anchor of another session than its candidate's is allowed and flagged; stacks of two people, one stack twice in an item, a candidate named twice or a stack not in the registry are refused, and `--dry-run` counts the items, the anchors of another session and the candidates that are also an anchor elsewhere. The seed draws the order of each item's three panels and the order the items are read in; the campaign keeps its sha256, suggests nothing and closes into nothing.
- The answer is `like_pre`, `like_post` or `cant_tell` and resolves into the candidate's `post_contrast` alone (not given, given, can't tell); the anchors are never labelled by it. `GET /api/campaigns/{id}/items/{item}/anchored` serves the three panels, labelled candidate, reference pre and reference post, with a flag on an anchor of another session and nothing else of any stack; `GET /api/campaigns/{id}/anchored` and `/anchored/values` and `nils campaign anchored-export` give the counts and each answer's candidate value. Every door that would show a time, a name, a header or the rules' values refuses such a campaign, as it does a pair campaign. Schema 76 adds `campaign_anchor_panel`; the HTTP API contract stays version 7, the three doors added in place.

### Changed

- The MRI pack is 0.14.0. It fixes the rule errors of a fresh development reference on 0.13.0, on the five header axes of the certificate (provenance, technique, modifier, construct and base), and undoes 0.13.0's two regressions: an inversion time on a synthetic image or map no longer makes a FLAIR or a STIR, and a synthetic weighted image that names a tissue is no tissue map. A FLAIR on a spin-echo readout is an IR-TSE where the file states no train (GE IR alone, research mode or `mrcflair`, Philips `M_IR`). GE's PROPELLER spellings (`ksprop`, `T2prop`, `PropFSE`, `PROPPELLER`) are Radial and a TSE, and radial MIP views are not Radial. A GE series in research mode with a spin echo's flip and echo time is a spin echo, SE or TSE by its train. GE's DTI outputs named `FA-`, `Evec` and `ExpAtt-` are FA and DTIRecon, DTIRecon, and eADC, and a diffusion map whose readout the file does not state is DWI-EPI. A GE SWI named without GE's output prefix is its magnitude, and an SWI projection named neither MIP nor minIP is the MinIP. A Siemens `fl2d8` or a series named for its echoes is ME-GRE, a Philips FFE of several echoes in one image is the combined multi-echo GRE, GE's `FAST_GEMS` on a spoiled gradient echo is FSP-GRE, a 3D gradient echo named for angiography at a TOF's repetition time is TOF, and Siemens `tseBR` is VFA-TSE. Philips `MobiView` is Composed and `SmartBrain` a localizer; `utan MTC` and `MT=OFF` switch MT off. GE's collapse is a MIP, and a Philips `b0` written as a projection is no reformat. A BOLD series with no readout stated at a short echo time is T2*w, a STIR named so is T2w, a spectroscopy acquisition has no base, and a spoiled gradient echo flipped 10 degrees or less at TR 20 ms or more is PDw. Stacks need a reclassify.

### Fixed

- A pipeline run over a selection that reaches more than 5,000 stacks failed with "reaches more than one answer holds; narrow it". A run, its pre-flight and a batch of pyramids now freeze the selection whole, however many stacks it holds; an answer to a question, a campaign, a label set and an A/B campaign keep the ask's caps.
- A selection whose document lists more ids than a statement may bind (65,535 on Postgres, 32,766 on SQLite) failed with "error parsing response from server" on Postgres and "variable number must be between" on SQLite. A list of more than 1,000 values is now bound as one JSON array and read back as rows.
- Taking in the results of a run whose units run together looked each unit up in the whole results list, so the time grew with the square of the units; each unit's entry is now found once.

## [1.0.0-alpha.60] - 2026-09-29

The MRI pack is 0.13.0: it gains the axis `body_region`, the coarse mode of body part, and fixes the rule errors of a second round on the development reference on the five header axes. The certified body-part model runs as the pipeline `bodypart-infer-fusion` of the `nils-bodypart` image 0.2.0, in both modes, with `nils model keep` to keep a registered model's files where a run finds them. Two new kinds of campaign: the A/B campaign settles what independent voters split on, blind, and pair mode reads two stacks of one subject side by side for the post-contrast gold. Pyramids read a sample's stored bits, planes at one place and eight-bit colour images, and a server thread reopens a registry connection the database closed. Reclassify with the new pack to apply it, and build again the pyramids of stacks whose pictures read black. Released with desk 1.0.0-alpha.58, which reads the A/B and pair doors. The registry is at schema 74 (migrations 73 and 74). The HTTP API contract stays version 7, amended in place with the additive A/B and pair doors; the suite contract stays version 3; the pack contract stays version 6; the job contract stays version 1, with the additive `x-nils.input.header`. An install fetches Kvasir 1.0.0-alpha.9 and the assistant 1.0.0-alpha.27, as with 1.0.0-alpha.59.

### Changed

- The MRI pack is 0.13.0. It gains the axis `body_region`, the coarse mode of body part (record 50: one image model, reported in two modes): `head` (a brain, or a brain and neck), `spine` (a spine, or a neck), `chest` and `other`. The rules never read the file for it: they fold the body part the rules decided, so a stack with no body part has no region, and a model's coarse answer reaches a stack only as a proposal a person commits. The region is written at the body part's confidence, 0.65, and asks no question of its own. Because it reads the body part alone, it is derived beside it: a campaign that asks Phase 0's seven axes now derives six (the body region beside role, directory type, disposition, convertible and quality), the reader's derived line shows it, and `nils pack derive` without `--derive` writes it. A campaign made before keeps the derived axes it was made with; `nils campaign requestion` without `--derive` derives the region as well. No other verdict changes. Stacks gain the region on a reclassify.
- The MRI pack's 0.13.0 also fixes the rule errors of a second round on the development reference, on the five header axes left in the certificate (provenance, technique, modifier, construct and base). An inversion-recovery spin echo inverted 1,400 to 2,600 ms before excitation is a FLAIR and under 300 ms a STIR, where its name says nothing (never a DIR, an MDME or a gradient echo). A spin echo whose header states an echo train of three or more is a TSE, and a GE PROPELLER written in research mode is a TSE with blades. Techniques by name: `IRFSE`, GE `3dfse`, `MP-RAGE` and a Siemens `t1_mpr` gradient echo. Modifiers by name: Philips `MV` (Radial), Philips `WATS` (water excitation), DANTE and Philips `BB` (black blood), GE PROMO (MoCo). A Siemens position display is a localizer. A PACS workstation's thick-slab average (`DERIVED\SECONDARY\...\AVERAGE`) is a reformat, and a workstation MIP of an SWI is ProjectionDerived. Diffusion: Philips `iso...` names and GE's `Isotropic Image` are the trace image, a Philips `sb0` is diffusion, a Philips `PROJECTION IMAGE` with a b value is no reformat, and GE's `AvDC`, `FA` and `Exp attenuation` are MD, FA (both from a tensor) and eADC. Elscint's enhanced images and GE's `FL:` copies are Filtered. Nima's rulings on three of the reference's disputes: a single-echo SWI is SP-GRE where SequenceVariant says SP and GRE otherwise; a GE calibration scan is no localizer but an acquired fast spoiled GRE, whose disposition is `scout` as a localizer's is, so no role takes it; the Philips isotropic image is Trace alone. The SWI route reads a multi-echo readout from the echo train as well as the split. Siemens `pat2` is no T2 or T2*, and `multiphase` or `AP phase` no phase image. A phase image has no base, a gradient-echo BOLD series is T2*w, a SyMRI tissue map is a quantitative map, and an isoDWI is scanner-derived as a Trace is. Stacks need a reclassify.

### Added

- The A/B campaign (record 48, the reference read by judges). `nils campaign ab <name> --select selection:NAME@V --voter NAME=DIR ... [--rules] --seed TEXT` reads independent voters' readings of every stack of a selection: a rater's JSON file per stack (the header judge's record or the answer alone, per axis a value and a reason, any name the pack gives a value), and with `--rules` the values in force, with the header facts the deciding clause read as the reason, which on a stack of a sample sealed now needs `--unsealed-access REASON`. A stack is an item where the voters give two or more values on an asked axis (a voter's can't tell is no vote), or where they agree on every asked axis and the pre-registered seed draws it for the audit: `--audit-share` of the agreeing stacks first, then, in the seed's order, each one that adds a cell to an axis with fewer than `--audit-cells` audited. Split and audit items are interleaved in the seed's order. A stack every voter calls a localizer asks only its provenance and body part (`--localizer-asks`), and `--localizers with|without|only` reads them with the rest or apart. `--dry-run` counts the items and the audit; the campaign keeps the seed's sha256, never the seed.
- Each item's candidates are served as letters drawn at random for the item and axis, each with the reason of one voter that gave it, drawn at random too, and never which voter gave which, nor whether the item is an audit (`GET /api/campaigns/{id}/items/{item}/ab`, to the caller holding the item).
