An A/B campaign is made to suggest `none`, so its evidence line is blind, it has no batches or gallery and takes no suggestion from outside, and no engine suggestion is kept beside its answers. An answer keeps what it chose on each axis (`choices`: a letter, `confirm`, `neither`, `cant_tell` or `not_asked`), read by the engine against the candidates. The person who answered may give each axis a cause afterwards (`POST /api/campaigns/{id}/answers/{answer}/cause`: rule bug, convention gap, header ambiguity, rater error, reader slip). `GET /api/campaigns/{id}/ab` says how far it is settled, the seconds per decision, the share of each choice on the split axes and the causes; `GET /api/campaigns/{id}/ab/decisions` and `nils campaign ab-export` give every decision with its candidates, choice and cause. The voters, the items' kinds and the audit's joint error are told once the campaign is closed, and on a stack of a sample sealed now only to a holder of `sealed:see` or with `--unsealed-access`.
- `not_asked` (reading guide ruling 1): on an A/B item whose answer's provenance is the localizer's, each axis a localizer is not asked is answered `not_asked`, neither none nor can't tell. The constraints read it as unnamed, a close writes no decision on it and names it in the item's outcome, and it is refused anywhere else.
- Schema 73: the A/B campaign's items, candidates and causes, and an answer's `choices`. The HTTP API contract stays version 7, with the A/B doors added in place.
- A stacks input may ask for each stack's header facts (record 50): `x-nils.input.header: true` gives every entry of `stacks.json` a `header` object with the stack's fingerprint columns (manufacturer, modality, orientation, the field of view, spacings, size, slices and span, thickness, field strength, the receive coil and the five folded texts), every classification row by axis with its confidence and tier, the name of its first ingest batch, and the cohorts its subject is an open member of. It is off unless asked for, so `stacks.json` is as it was, and it is refused with the bids layout. Units that run apart each get their own stack's. The job contract stays version 1, with the key and the object added.
- `nils model keep <model> --artifact FILE` keeps a registered model's artifact where a run finds it (record 50). A model registered by its card was checked against its file and nothing was kept, so no pipeline could mount it. The file is copied into the lane's output place under `derivatives/models/<name>-<version>/` and registered as a derivative of kind `model` and the new scope `model`, which names the model and neither a subject nor a run. A file whose sha256 is not the model's digest is refused, and so is a retired model; the same bytes kept again write nothing. A run given the model with `--model` mounts the kept file's folder read-only at `/inputs/<input>/` and names the file in its manifest.
- The certified body-part model runs as a pipeline (record 50): `bodypart-infer-fusion`, a new entry point `infer-fusion` of the `nils-bodypart` image, now 0.2.0. It takes the model's registered parts as models, the image encoder, the head and optionally the coarse mode file (`nils run bodypart-infer-fusion --model bodypart-tiny@r7 --model bodypart-fusion@r7 --model bodypart-fusion-coarse@r7`), and refuses the run when an artifact is not the one its digest names or the parts do not name each other. Per stack it reads the files its training listed (96 positions evenly and v0's slices beside them) into an 8 mm volume in patient axes, scores it with the encoder in numpy, and gives that answer and 44 features of the stack's header (its fingerprint and the rules' answer, which the descriptor asks for with `x-nils.input.header`) to the LightGBM head. Fine mode calibrates the answer to the six body_part values at the temperature of the stack's cohort (the first the calibration names of the stack's first ingest batch, the parts of its name after a `-`, and the subject's cohorts), or the global one; coarse mode sums it into the four body_region values and calibrates those. Each scored stack gets a `body_part` proposal by the head's digest and a `body_region` proposal by the mode file's, also below the threshold, so the engine stages what is at or above the card's threshold and makes the rest review items (an abstaining mode's probability is never rounded up to its threshold); and a table of its scores (`bodypart-fusion/<stack>.json`), which the ask reads as `measure.bodypart-infer-fusion.<column>`. A stack with no fingerprint row or no geometry gets no answer and is skipped. The image adds LightGBM and safetensors and runs offline. The descriptor pins the previous image until the release that publishes this one repins all five.
- Pair mode for the post-contrast picture gold. `nils campaign pair <name> --pairs FILE [--pair A,B ...] --seed TEXT [--rater P]` makes a campaign whose items are pairs of stacks of one subject, a pre/post candidate pair or a same-header rerun: one pair a line in the file, two stack ids. The seed draws which stack of each pair is shown on the left and the order the pairs are read in; the campaign keeps the seed's sha256, never the seed, suggests nothing and closes into nothing. `--dry-run` checks and counts the pairs; a pair of two people's stacks, one stack twice or a pair named twice is refused.
- The question of kind `pair` takes one of five answers: `left_post`, `right_post`, `both_pre`, `both_post` or `cant_tell`. Each resolves into the value of the axis (`--axis`, by default post_contrast) per stack: the stack said to be post takes `--post` (given), one said to be pre takes `--pre` (not given), and can't tell leaves both at can't tell. The close keeps the two values on each item's outcome as `stacks`; `GET /api/campaigns/{id}/pair/values` and `nils campaign pair-export` give one row per answer and stack, and `GET /api/campaigns/{id}/pair` says how the campaign goes.
- A pair is read from its two pictures alone. `GET /api/campaigns/{id}/items/{item}/pair` serves the stack shown on each side and the answers, to the rater holding the item; an item names neither stack, and its key is its position. Every door that would show a stack's evidence line, its header, what the pack derives, candidates, a batch, the gallery, suggestions or the combinations refuses a pair campaign with 409, and a rater of the campaign reads the two stacks' pictures through it.
- Schema 74: which stack each pair shows on which side. The HTTP API contract stays version 7, with the pair doors added in place.

### Fixed

- A pyramid reads a 16-bit sample's stored bits alone (Bits Stored, ending at High Bit), as DICOM has it. A scout that carries an overlay's lines in the unused high bits read them as values of 4096 and more, the window taken from the stack's percentiles opened on them, and the picture read black. Pyramids built before keep the old window until they are built again (`nils pyramid build --stack N` builds one anew).
- Planes that all sit at one place (a scout taken again in one session, which one stack holds) are a thickness apart in the pyramid's manifest, the files' Spacing Between Slices or Slice Thickness, never zero, and name no step; a spacing of zero made the viewer's volume flat and its three planes black. The frame still says they are not evenly spaced.
- A colour image of eight bits (three samples, RGB or YBR, native or compressed, as a synthetic MR map is archived) is read as its grey. Its stack's pyramid failed as unsupported pixels, and its campaign item showed no picture.
- The window a pyramid opens at leaves out a plateau at the top, where between a two-hundredth and a tenth of the sampled pixels sit at the very largest value (a reformat's fill, a clipped field): it set the ninety-ninth percentile, and the rest of the picture read black.
- A handler thread of `nils serve` whose registry connection the database closed (a restart of Postgres under a running engine) opens a new one. It kept the closed one, and every request it took that touched the registry answered 500 until the engine was restarted: a gallery's first look at a stack writes the open to the audit, so its pictures came blank on such threads and appeared on a reload.
- A gallery's picture of a stack is drawn when its pyramid is built, so a gallery opened on a campaign just built reads files rather than drawing hundreds at once; two draws of one picture at once no longer write one part file together.

## [1.0.0-alpha.59] - 2026-09-29

The MRI pack is 0.12.0: a workstation reformat is classified on every axis and marked with the disposition `reformat`, which pipelines, picks and a release read to leave it out, and the rule errors of a development reference are fixed. A campaign says what its raters are shown beside each item (`--suggest none|rules|imported`, default none), so a read can be made blind. `nils pack derive` carries answers kept anywhere through a pack's own rules, offline. Reclassify with the new pack to apply it. Released with desk 1.0.0-alpha.57, which reads a campaign's suggest setting. The registry is at schema 72. The HTTP API contract stays version 7, amended in place with the additive `suggest`; the suite contract stays version 3; the pack contract stays version 6; the job contract stays version 1. An install fetches Kvasir 1.0.0-alpha.9 and the assistant 1.0.0-alpha.27, as with 1.0.0-alpha.58.

### Changed

- The MRI pack is 0.12.0. A workstation reformat (ImageType SECONDARY and never PRIMARY) is no longer ruled out with every axis empty: it is classified on every axis as any stack is (provenance ProjectionDerived, construct MPR, MIP or MinIP as its header says, and its source's technique, base, modifier and post-contrast), and its disposition is `reformat`, which is what a pipeline or a pick reads to leave it out. It is convertible, and a release writes it under `derivatives/`, never in the raw tree; no role takes it. A Siemens `SCREEN SAVE` is still ruled out, as a screenshot. The physics vote's pool leaves out projections and reformats, which repeat their source's physics, and no longer fills the base of an MDME or QALAS stack. Other rule fixes from the development reference: an MDME acquisition takes no IR or STIR modifier and no Magnitude construct; a Philips projection image with no MIP token or word is an MPR; an MPR is not also a MIP or MinIP, a MinIP not also a MIP, and a denoised MP2RAGE uniform image not also Uniform; the word `phase` in `in phase`, `arterial phase` or `phase contrast` makes no Phase construct; a Siemens `*tseR` or GE FRFSE is the driven equilibrium TSE (RESTORE); the SWI route keeps ND, calls a reformat ProjectionDerived, a real part RawRecon and T2*w, a split multi-echo readout ME-GRE, and a magnitude named for QSM no QSM map; an MT-weighted multi-echo gradient echo is MTw. Stacks need a reclassify.
- A reading campaign says when it is made what its raters are shown beside each item as the answer suggested: `nils campaign create --suggest none|rules|imported`, or `suggest` on `POST /api/campaigns`. The default is `none`: a campaign made without saying shows nothing, so a read registered as made without suggestions is one. With `none` every door of the campaign reads its items blind, as a sealed stack is read: the evidence door serves the file and nothing a system said of the stack, no batch is formed, the gallery suggests nothing and keeps the items' order, a claim by value takes an order drawn from the campaign's seed, and every answer keeps nothing suggested beside it. `rules` is what every campaign did before: the rules' answer and System 1's, with batches of like stacks. `imported` shows only what `nils campaign suggest` brings in, never the rules, and forms no batch; suggestions are refused by a campaign made any other way. A campaign made before is written as what it showed: `imported` where suggestions were brought into it, `rules` otherwise. Registry schema 72; the HTTP API contract stays version 7, amended in place additively.

### Added

- `nils pack derive <dir> --asked <axes>` carries answers kept anywhere through a pack's own rules, offline and with no registry: JSON lines in, one per stack (`{stack, answer, fields}`, the answer as the reader takes it and the fields the stack as the pack sees it), and the derived axes out (`{stack, values}`), a value, a list, null for none, or `cant_tell` where an asked axis it reads was answered so. Without `--derive` it derives every axis the pack may derive beside the asked ones; for the MRI pack's seven asked axes those are role, directory type, disposition, convertible and quality, the last from ImageType alone. It goes through the same code as the reader's live derived line, so a settled reference, or a system's stored answers, can be scored on the derived axes as a rater's answer is.

## [1.0.0-alpha.58] - 2026-09-28

`nils digest --reread` reads a series at a time through an index instead of joining its target series into every page, so a re-read runs at the rate the parsers and the storage allow; a stopped, failed or killed re-read is continued by the next one (`--restart` starts over); and `--reread-exact <manufacturer>` compares the manufacturer with its case, so Siemens XA can be re-read without the older Siemens fleet. Released alone, with no desk release of the same number. The registry stays at schema 71. The HTTP API contract stays version 7; the suite contract stays version 3; the pack contract stays version 6; the job contract stays version 1. The MRI pack stays 0.11.0. An install fetches Kvasir 1.0.0-alpha.9 and the assistant 1.0.0-alpha.27, as with 1.0.0-alpha.57.

### Added

- `nils digest <root> --reread-exact <manufacturer>` re-reads the MR series of a manufacturer compared with its case, beside `--reread`, which compares without it. Siemens spells its XA11 and XA20 scanners `Siemens` and its older ones `SIEMENS`, so GE, Siemens Healthineers and Siemens XA are `--reread "GE MEDICAL SYSTEMS" --reread "Siemens Healthineers" --reread-exact Siemens`, without reading the older Siemens fleet.
- A re-read that was stopped, failed or killed is continued by the next one: the files it already wrote are counted as unchanged and not read again. `--restart` beside `--reread` starts over.

### Fixed

- `nils digest --reread` no longer joins its target series into every page's query. It resolves them once and reads them a series at a time, each page an index lookup that costs what its series holds, so a re-read runs at the rate the parsers and the storage allow instead of waiting about a minute on each page of 10,000 files of a large archive.

## [1.0.0-alpha.57] - 2026-09-28

The MRI pack is 0.11.0: four new constructs (`DeltaM` and `M0` for ASL, `SBRef` for a multiband run's single-band reference, `FieldmapRef` for a reversed phase-encoding EPI), the rule fixes two header re-measures found, and a BIDS `aslcontext.tsv` beside an ASL image. Each axis now says whether the header or the name decided it (`basis`), in `nils classify`, `nils explain` and the two explain doors. The digest collects PulseSequenceName and the GE and Philips sequence elements, and `nils digest <root> --reread <manufacturer>` reads one manufacturer's MR files again without a walk. Reclassify with the new pack to apply it. Released alone, with no desk release of the same number. The registry is at schema 71. The HTTP API contract stays version 7, amended in place with the additive `basis`; the suite contract stays version 3; the pack contract stays version 6, with additions; the job contract stays version 1. An install fetches Kvasir 1.0.0-alpha.9 and the assistant 1.0.0-alpha.27, as with 1.0.0-alpha.56.

### Changed

- The MRI pack is 0.11.0. Four constructs ruled on 2026-09-28: `DeltaM`, the ASL label-control difference (Siemens `ASL` with `SUB` or `TTEST`, GE `PERFUSION_ASL` or its perfusion-weighted pass), base PWI, BIDS `perf/asl` with `aslcontext` `deltam`; `M0`, the ASL's M0 scan (GE's proton-density pass, or a series named M0 beside an ASL readout), base PDw, BIDS `perf/m0scan`; `SBRef`, a multiband run's single-band reference (named for it, or on Siemens numbered 1000 above its run), BIDS `func/sbref`; and `FieldmapRef`, an EPI of a few volumes with the phase encoding reversed (GE `epi_pepolar`, or named for it), BIDS `fmap/epi`. The pack reads GE's pulse sequence names and ASL elements and Philips' dynamics and ASL label type, collected from the next digest on. The rule bugs two header re-measures found: an ADC, eADC or trace made from a plain DWI is RawRecon, not DTIRecon, and a DTI acquisition itself is RawRecon; an ADC is not also Trace or eADC; `SUR**`, `3-pl`, `Ö-SIKT` and a tri-plane scout of at most 5 slices a plane are localizers; a CUBE FLAIR reformat is 3D-TSE; `DIS3D` is no 3D acquisition; Siemens `tir` is a turbo inversion recovery; a stray GE b value on a stated non-EPI readout is no diffusion; a classic Siemens dynamic VIBE phase with a time to contrast is DCE; GE `MT_GEMS` is MT and its vascular projection a MIP; SyMRI acquisition images keep ND; a Philips projection named MPR is an MPR and `T1W_FFE` is SP-GRE; a gradient echo named T2 is T2*-weighted; post-contrast is no longer read off GE's exam-level ProtocolName; the physics vote no longer writes a base on a phase image or an MP2RAGE inversion; and a gradient-echo EPI SWI is GRE-EPI. The corpus holds a case for each. Reclassify with the new pack to apply it; a reading campaign frozen on an earlier pack keeps its constraints until it is repacked.

### Added

- A pack may read PulseSequenceName (0018,9005) as `pulse_sequence_name`, from the top level of the file or the private per-frame sequences: Siemens XA writes it where it leaves SequenceName empty, on about a quarter of its stacks. The digest keeps it on the MR series and the fingerprint carries it; the fingerprint's revision moves, so the next `nils fingerprint` derives every stack again. Registry schema 71; the pack contract stays version 6, with one field added.
- `nils digest <root> --reread <manufacturer>` reads again, without a walk, only the files the registry holds for that manufacturer's MR series, so a registry digested before the new field, or before a pack declared new private elements, gains them without a full re-digest. Every file of such a series is read, nothing is marked gone, and the fingerprints of those series are derived again. For GE and Siemens XA on an archive of 43.7 M MR instances that is 16.8 M files, about 7 hours at 670 files a second.
- A test shows the GE and Philips elements of the sequence research (GE's pulse sequence names, ASL technique, label text and duration, private image type and multiband text; Philips' label type and number of dynamic scans) read in implicit and explicit VR files, binary SS and multi-valued LO and IS included, and handed to a pack under the names its ingest list gives them.
- Each axis a stack was judged on says whether the header or the name decided it (`basis`), derived from the tier the verdict records: `header` for a flag, a combination, an alternative or a physics window, `name` for a keyword, `inferred` for a longhand rule, and `default`, `answer`, `neighbours` (a pass's vote), `decision` or `other`. It is in `nils classify`'s verdicts, `nils explain` (a column beside the tier) and `GET /api/explain/{stack}`, on each evidence row as well, and in the reader's evidence line (`GET /api/stacks/{stack}/why`). It is computed when a stack is read, so stacks classified before carry it too. The HTTP API contract stays version 7, amended in place.
- A pack's BIDS suffix `asl` may say what every volume of the image is (`aslcontext`, one of `control`, `label`, `m0scan`, `deltam`, `cbf`, `noRF`; refused on any other suffix or word), and a BIDS release writes the `aslcontext.tsv` BIDS requires beside the converted image, one row per volume read from its NIfTI header. The release check reads the table's name as it reads an image's. The pack contract stays version 6: the key is an addition.

## [1.0.0-alpha.56] - 2026-09-28

The MRI pack is 0.10.0: fMRI, perfusion and the gradient-echo family are read from the header. The EPI time series are tried in the order diffusion, ASL, DSC, BOLD; Siemens sequence stems and Philips' scanning technique decide the gradient-echo family, VIBE is recognised by name only, and FSP-GRE is tried before SP-GRE; the short technique words count only as whole words, so VenBOLD is no BOLD; GE's exam ProtocolName no longer decides a technique; DCE is a 3D spoiled gradient echo of 10 or more time points; and a motion-corrected copy follows its image type. A pack may read temporal positions and the series number, and a normalized text may leave one vendor's field out. Reclassify with the new pack to apply it. Released alone, with no desk release of the same number. The registry stays at schema 70. The HTTP API contract stays version 7; the suite contract stays version 3; the pack contract stays version 6, with two additions; the job contract stays version 1. An install fetches Kvasir 1.0.0-alpha.9 and the assistant 1.0.0-alpha.27, as with 1.0.0-alpha.55.

### Changed

- The MRI pack is 0.10.0: fMRI, perfusion and the gradient-echo family, from two research reports reconciled and checked against an archive, with no new vocabulary. The EPI time series are tried in the order diffusion, ASL, DSC, BOLD, then the spin-echo and gradient-echo EPI references. BOLD is read from the header as well as the words: an EPI time series with no diffusion, perfusion or ASL token and no contrast, and a single-band reference as evidence. DSC is read from a perfusion image type, a map only DSC makes, or an EPI time series given contrast. DCE is read from a 3D spoiled gradient echo of 10 or more time points that is no angiography run. The short words (`bold`, `asl`, `dsc`, `pwi`, `dce`, `task`) count only as whole words, so a misspelt `basline` is no ASL and Philips' VenBOLD is an SWI and not BOLD; GE's ProtocolName, which names the exam rather than the series, no longer decides a technique; and a word for an EPI technique is not believed on a series whose header states another readout. A Siemens sequence name decides against ScanningSequence (a CISS or TrueFISP written SE is no spin echo, `epfid` is a gradient-echo EPI and `epse` a spin-echo one), the Siemens stems are anchored (`fl3d` inside `tfl3d` is TurboFLASH), and FISP, DESS and PSIF are read from their names or from SequenceVariant TRSS. Philips' scanning technique (2001,1020) is read at last: T1-FFE, TFE, balanced FFE, T2-FFE and the EPI readouts. VIBE, LAVA and THRIVE are recognised by name only, since no header field marks interpolation, so an unnamed 3D spoiled gradient echo is SP-GRE. GE's `MP_GEMS` is no magnetisation preparation. A motion-corrected copy written `ORIGINAL\PRIMARY` is not derived: it keeps modifier MoCo, is RawRecon and is no longer scanner derived, and only one written DERIVED takes BOLDRecon or PerfusionRecon; a computed map is still decided by its own token. The corpus holds a case for each. Estimated by replaying the rules over 558,405 fingerprints, 9,675 stacks change on some axis. Reclassify with the new pack to apply it; a reading campaign frozen on an earlier pack keeps its constraints until it is repacked.

### Added

- A pack may read a series' temporal positions (`temporal_positions`) and its series number (`series_number`), which the fingerprint held and no rule could reach, and a normalized text may leave one vendor's value of a field out (`{field: ..., unless_manufacturer: [...]}` in `from`). The pack contract stays version 6: both are additions.

## [1.0.0-alpha.55] - 2026-09-28

The MRI pack is 0.9.0: an MP2RAGE's two inversions take base none, a dual-echo PD/T2 FSE or TSE is technique TSE, an acquisition's own SWI, BOLD, ASL and MDME images are RawRecon, an SWI's magnitude is T2*w and its phase has no base, and GE's readout-direction tokens are never FatSat. A rule set may decide an axis again (`redecides`), and a BIDS suffix's `when_technique` is checked when the pack loads. The MRI corpus holds an MP2RAGE whose name also says T1. Reclassify with the new pack to apply it. Released alone, with no desk release of the same number. The registry stays at schema 70. The HTTP API contract stays version 7; the suite contract stays version 3; the pack contract stays version 6, with the `redecides` key added; the job contract stays version 1. An install fetches Kvasir 1.0.0-alpha.9 and the assistant 1.0.0-alpha.27, as with 1.0.0-alpha.54.

### Changed

- The MRI pack is 0.9.0, with the reading conventions of 2026-09-27 and 28. An MP2RAGE's two inversions, INV1 and INV2, take base none and the construct says what they are, as BIDS files them `inv-1` and `inv-2` of the suffix `MP2RAGE`; the uniform image stays T1w, and a base beside an inversion breaks an exclusion. This replaces 0.6.0's INV2 as PDw. A dual-echo PD/T2 FSE or TSE, one stack per echo, is technique TSE and not ME-SE, with base PDw on an echo under 40 ms and T2w from 40 ms; ME-SE keeps a conventional multi-echo spin echo, and GE's `memp` with one echo is a plain spin echo. An acquisition's own images are RawRecon and what the scanner computed from them takes the product: an SWI's magnitude and phase, a BOLD or ASL series as acquired and MAGiC's MDME images are RawRecon, the SWI image, an ASL's CBF map (no longer PerfusionRecon), an fMRI map and the synthetic contrasts keep SWIRecon, ASLRecon, BOLDRecon and SyMRI, and an SWI's minIP is ProjectionDerived. An SWI's magnitude is T2*w and its phase has no base. GE's `FSA_GEMS`, `FSP_GEMS`, `FSS_GEMS`, `FSI_GEMS`, `FSR_GEMS` and `FSL_GEMS` are the readout direction and never FatSat, which the pack now says where FatSat's evidence is defined. The corpus holds a case for each. Reclassify with the new pack to apply it; a reading campaign frozen on an earlier pack keeps its constraints until it is repacked.

### Added

- A rule set may decide again an axis an earlier set decided (`redecides`), where one of its rules fires and has a value to put there; a person's answer is never moved. The MRI pack's SWI and SyMRI routes use it to give an acquisition's own images the provenance RawRecon.
- The MRI pack's corpus holds an MP2RAGE whose name also says T1, with its inversion in the image comments. A reader greyed INV2 out on such a stack; the cause was the desk (kineuro/nils-desk#164), not the pack.

### Fixed

- A BIDS suffix that waits for a technique (`when_technique`) names it by its identity, checked when the pack loads. A label or a misspelling loaded before and never matched, so the stack fell through to its base contrast without a word. A test builds the MRI pack's own MP2RAGE names: `inv-1_MP2RAGE`, `inv-2_MP2RAGE`, `UNIT1` and `rec-Denoised_UNIT1`.

## [1.0.0-alpha.54] - 2026-09-27

An engine update brings the rule packs of the engine's release to the directory the engine reads, keeps the packs it replaces in `<dir>.previous`, and never touches a site's own packs or a first-party pack changed on the machine; `nils update --check` and the install door's `release.packs` show each pack's version beside the release's. A classification over stacks of a sealed sample raises no review item, and `nils repair sealed-review` closes the ones an older engine raised. Released alone, with no desk release of the same number. The registry stays at schema 70. The HTTP API contract stays version 7, amended in place; the suite contract stays version 3; the pack contract stays version 6; the job contract stays version 1. An install fetches Kvasir 1.0.0-alpha.9 and the assistant 1.0.0-alpha.27, as with 1.0.0-alpha.53.

### Fixed

- An engine update brings the rule packs of the engine's release. Where a site named its pack directory, `nils update` put the new packs where the account running it would look (beside the binary) instead of where the engine reads them, so the engine went on classifying with the packs of the release it was installed from. `nils update --all` now puts the packs of the installed engine's release in the directory the engine reads, whichever binary replaced the engine, and does so where the engine is already at its newest release, so an install left behind catches up on its next update. Only packs that differ from the release's are replaced, in one swap; the ones they replace are kept in `<dir>.previous`, a site's own packs are never touched, and a first-party pack changed on the machine after an update put it there is kept and said. `nils update --check` lists each pack beside the release's, and `GET /api/supervise/install` adds `release.packs`, with `packs` in `release.behind` where they are behind (HTTP API contract 7, amended in place).
- A classification over stacks of a sample sealed now raises no review item and says it raised none. The items were already left out before they were written, but the run's report and each stack's classification row still counted them, so a run over a sealed sample read as having raised questions it never asked. A pass that votes counts the same way.

### Added

- `nils repair sealed-review [--dry-run] [--json]` closes the open review items about stacks of a sample sealed now that an older engine raised: a stack's own item, or a grouped item whose members are all sealed. The items are marked superseded, never deleted, in one transaction with one `registry.repair` audit row naming them. An item a reading campaign holds is kept, since the campaign is how a sealed sample is read, and is counted. A second run finds nothing.

## [1.0.0-alpha.53] - 2026-09-27

The engine and the desk release independently, under version numbers of their own. `nils setup` and `nils update` take the newest desk release whose `contracts.json` floor the engine meets and pull the desk's image by the desk's own version, so an engine release needs no desk release of the same number; `nils update --check` and `release.parts` say which desk release an install takes and which waits. `NILS_SETUP_DESK_VERSION` pins the desk for a lab. Pictures come without a 40 ms stall per request, and rendered coronal and sagittal planes are kept. The registry stays at schema 70. The HTTP API contract stays version 7, amended in place; the suite contract stays version 3; the pack contract stays version 6; the job contract stays version 1. An install fetches Kvasir 1.0.0-alpha.9 and the assistant 1.0.0-alpha.27, as with 1.0.0-alpha.52.

### Changed

- The engine and the desk release independently, and their version numbers need not match. `nils setup` takes the newest desk release whose `contracts.json` floor the engine meets, and a container install pulls the desk's image by the desk's own version instead of the engine's, so an engine release no longer needs a desk release of the same number. `nils update` moves the desk to the newest release the engine speaks, passing over a newer one that waits for an engine release; `nils update --check` and `release.parts` say which (`takes`, `waits`; HTTP API contract 7, amended in place). `NILS_SETUP_DESK_VERSION` pins the desk at one version for a lab. An install made before keeps the images its record names until its next update.
- Every HTTP response is sent without a 40 ms stall. The engine wrote a response as its head and then its body without TCP_NODELAY, so over the desk's kept-open connection the body waited for the delayed acknowledgement of the head; the listener now sets TCP_NODELAY, and through the desk a manifest takes about 1 ms instead of 42 ms and a cached render 1.5 ms instead of 107 ms.
- The render door keeps the planes it drew, up to 64 MB with the oldest dropped first, keyed by the pyramid, its manifest's time and length, the level, the axis, the plane, the window and whether the band is held, so a coronal or sagittal plane asked again is not decoded again. Access checks and the audit row still come first, and the cache headers are unchanged.

## [1.0.0-alpha.52] - 2026-09-27

Each part is checked and updated against its own newest release. `nils update --check` lists every part, the engine, the desk, the assistant and Kvasir, beside its own newest release; `nils update --part <part>` updates one part alone; the install door adds `release.parts` and `release.behind`, and `POST /api/supervise/update-all` takes `{part}`. A desk release whose `contracts.json` needs an engine contract the engine does not speak waits and is not installed. Released with desk 1.0.0-alpha.52, which offers each part's update on its Parts page and publishes its `contracts.json`. The registry stays at schema 70. The HTTP API contract stays version 7, amended in place; the suite contract stays version 3; the pack contract stays version 6; the job contract stays version 1. An install fetches Kvasir 1.0.0-alpha.9 and the assistant 1.0.0-alpha.27, as with 1.0.0-alpha.49.

### Fixed

- An update is offered when any part is behind its own newest release, not only when the engine is. The engine, the desk, the assistant and Kvasir each release on their own, so a desk released alone was never offered: `nils update --check` compared the engine alone, and the supervisor's install door, which the desk's Parts page reads, offered nothing while the engine was at its newest. `nils update --check` now says each part's installed version beside its own newest release and what `--all` would take; `nils update --part <part>` updates one part alone; `nils update --all` moves a desk image to the desk's own newest tag and the assistant and Kvasir to their own newest tags, never below the tags this engine pins. `GET /api/supervise/install` adds `release.parts` and `release.behind`, and `release.newer` names the first part behind where the engine is not; `POST /api/supervise/update-all` takes `{part}`; the helper takes `update <part>` (HTTP API contract 7, amended in place). A desk release that names, in a `contracts.json`, an engine contract the engine does not speak is held and said to wait, and is not installed.

## [1.0.0-alpha.49] - 2026-09-27

The MRI pack is version 0.8.0. Philips's `VERTEBRALCRANIUM`, written as the body part examined for the spine and the brain alike, names no body part, so the series' own words, the coil or the geometry decide, or the axis stays empty for review. The German `WS` and a range of vertebral levels such as `C2-Th1` or `C3-6` read as a spine. The registry stays at schema 70. The HTTP API contract stays version 7; the suite contract stays version 3; the pack contract stays version 6; the job contract stays version 1. An install fetches Kvasir 1.0.0-alpha.9 and the assistant 1.0.0-alpha.27, as with 1.0.0-alpha.48.

### Changed

- The MRI pack is version 0.8.0. The stated-anatomy normalizer drops `vertebralcranium`, the value Philips writes as Body Part Examined on its neuro exam cards for the spine and the brain alike and no DICOM defined term, so it decides nothing: its `vertebral` no longer makes every stack whose own words say nothing a spine, 3D FLAIR, DIR and head surveys among them. The series' words, the coil or the geometry decide, or the axis stays empty for review. The anatomy normalizer spells the German `WS` (Wirbelsäule) as a word, so the `wirbel` keyword reads it, and a new rule, `spine_levels`, right after `spine`, reads a range of vertebral levels such as `C2-Th1`, `Th4-9`, `C4-L1` or `C3-6` as a spine; `T1` and `T2` are never read as levels. `HWS`, `BWS` and `LWS` cite their sources, and `PROVENANCE.md` lists them. Stacks classified before keep their verdicts until they are classified again under this pack.

## [1.0.0-alpha.48] - 2026-09-27

Sealed means sealed on every door. While a stack is of a sample sealed now, what any system said of it is withheld from every caller, an admin included, but a token holding `sealed:see`: the ask, explain and why, the review, the timeline, label sets, the classifier's votes and signals, selections and releases leave it out or answer blind, while the file, its header and its pictures still open. No decision or pick is written on a sealed stack. At the keyboard `--unsealed-access REASON` lifts this for one command and is audited; a server refuses the flag. The registry stays at schema 70. The HTTP API contract stays version 7, amended in place; the suite contract stays version 3, amended in place with `sealed:see`; the pack contract stays version 6; the job contract stays version 1. An install fetches Kvasir 1.0.0-alpha.9 and the assistant 1.0.0-alpha.27, as with 1.0.0-alpha.47.

### Changed

- Sealed means sealed on every door (record 48, D1 of the move). While a stack is of a sample sealed now, what any system said of it is withheld from every caller, an admin included, but one holding `sealed:see`, a grant of suite contract version 3 (added in place) that no ladder set holds and `--auth off` does not give. The ask and every door that runs a question leave the stack out; `GET /api/explain/{stack}` and `GET /api/stacks/{stack}/why` answer blind; its review items are not listed, not shown and not answered, and a classification or System 1 raises none; its timeline keeps its arrival alone; a label set leaves its lines out; the classifier's signals and batch diagnostics leave it out; no selection or release reaches it. A handle answered with sealed stacks in it opens only to `sealed:see`. The file itself, its header and its pictures, still opens, and the rater's doors are as blind as before. A selection frozen into a campaign, a seal or a label set still names its sealed stacks, so a sealed sample becomes a reading campaign.
- No decision or pick is written on a stack of a sample sealed now, for any caller: a commit by filter leaves such stacks out and counts them in `left_out_sealed`, a filter, decision, answer or pick that names one is refused (409), a campaign closing into decisions or picks leaves its items unresolved, and `nils labels import-v0` skips them. At the keyboard `--unsealed-access REASON` lifts this for one command, audited; a server refuses the flag.
- The keyboard withholds the same: `nils explain`, `nils review list`, `show`, `decide` and `accept`, `nils ask` and its handle export, `nils labels export`, `nils pick list` and `explain` and `nils classify votes` refuse or leave out a sealed stack unless `--unsealed-access REASON` is given, which is written to the audit as `sealed.read`. A job a door queued reads as the caller that queued it.

## [1.0.0-alpha.47] - 2026-09-26

A rater lists their own answers and corrects one while the campaign is open, the correction written as a new answer that supersedes the earlier, which is kept. `nils campaign raters` and its door add raters to an open campaign or take them off. A claim with `alone` offers only the items held back to be read one by one, and every claim names `next`, the item a reader fetches ahead; the manifest and header doors send cache headers. A sheared stack is placed where its planes are: the pyramid's manifest carries `step`, and the render and thumb doors cut such a stack's planes square in the patient. A pyramid built before gains `step` only when it is rebuilt. The registry is at schema 70. The HTTP API contract stays version 7, amended in place with additive fields and new doors; the pack contract stays version 6; the job contract stays version 1. An install fetches Kvasir 1.0.0-alpha.9 and the assistant 1.0.0-alpha.27, as with 1.0.0-alpha.46.

### Added

- A rater corrects their own answer while the campaign is open (record 48, after the first gold campaign). `GET /api/campaigns/{id}/mine` lists the caller's answers now, the latest first, filtered by value, and on a sealed stack shows only their own answer. `POST /api/campaigns/{id}/answers/{answer}/amend` writes the correction as a new answer, `via` amend, that supersedes the earlier; the earlier is kept, never deleted, and names it in `superseded_by`. Agreement, the item's outcome and the close read the correction, a correction that makes raters disagree offers the item to an adjudicator, and an export of answers holds both (labels.tsv gains `superseded_by`). Refused on a closed campaign, for another's answer, for one already corrected and on an item that went to an adjudicator. Audited as campaign.amend. Schema 70.
- `nils campaign raters <campaign> --add <principal> --remove <principal>` and `POST /api/campaigns/{id}/raters` (the campaign's maker or a holder of review:work) change the raters of an open campaign that names them. A rater taken off keeps their answers and gives back a live claim; one added who answered the same stacks in another campaign is named in a warning. Audited as campaign.raters.
- A claim with `alone` true offers only the items read one by one (held back from a batch, or of a sealed sample), and every rater's claim names `next`, the item the next claim would offer, so a reader fetches it ahead. The gallery says `held_back_open` and `alone`.
- The manifest door and an item's header door send `Cache-Control: private, max-age=300`.

### Fixed

- A sheared stack is placed where its planes are. The pyramid's manifest carries `step`, the mean step from one plane to the next in the patient, from the planes' own positions; it lies along the normal for most stacks and has a part in the plane where the acquisition was sheared (a tilted gantry, a slab whose planes shift as they go). The render door cuts such a stack's `y` and `x` planes square to its rows or columns in the patient, each plane's line shifted by its step, rather than reading the stored rows as if the planes lay one above the other, and the thumb door's two planes across the stack are cut the same way. A manifest without `step` (built before) reads as along the normal, as before; rebuilding a stack's pyramid adds it.

## [1.0.0-alpha.46] - 2026-09-26

A campaign carries answers suggested from outside the engine, v0's labels or a model's proposals, each with its author and its confidence per class, brought in with `nils campaign suggest` or its door. A campaign that asks one axis has a gallery of a hundred items, the least certain first, accepted in one move with each item's own value, the suggestion and its author (`suggested_by`) kept beside each answer; a stack is drawn small at its thumb door. `nils campaign repack` moves an open axes campaign to the served pack's version and keeps every answer as given. The MRI pack is version 0.7.0, where `body_part` gains `other`. The registry is at schema 69. The HTTP API contract stays version 7, amended in place with additive fields and new doors; the pack contract stays version 6; the job contract stays version 1. An install fetches Kvasir 1.0.0-alpha.9 and the assistant 1.0.0-alpha.27, as with 1.0.0-alpha.45.

### Added

- A campaign carries answers suggested from outside the engine (record 50): v0's committed labels or a model's proposals, each with its author (`v0-model`, `v0-person`, a model id) and, where the source gave them, a confidence per class. `POST /api/campaigns/{id}/suggestions` (the campaign's maker, or a holder of review:work) and `nils campaign suggest <campaign> --file <tsv> [--author]` bring them in, by stack, item or SeriesInstanceUID, so v0's export reads as it is; `GET` counts them. A suggestion is never an answer, a stack of a sealed sample takes none, and an author's later import replaces its earlier one. Schema 69.
- A gallery for a campaign that asks one axis: `GET /api/campaigns/{id}/gallery` lists up to 100 of the items open to the caller, each with the value suggested, who suggested it, the confidence of that value and of every class, and a small picture, least certain first (or grouped by value). `POST /api/campaigns/{id}/gallery/accept` accepts the page in one move with each item's own value, the suggestion or the person's correction: each item is its own answer by the person, `via` batch, with the engine's suggestion and its author (`suggested_by`) kept beside it, `changed` where it was corrected, and the page's time shared among the answers. A sealed item and an item the campaign's seed holds back are never shown or accepted in one move; they are read alone.
- `GET /api/instances/{stack}/thumb`: a stack drawn small, its own plane beside the two across it at their true shape, from the coarsest level of the pyramid that fills it, kept beside the pyramid once drawn.
- `nils campaign repack <campaign> [--keep-values] [--dry-run] [--json]` moves an open axes campaign's frozen question to the served pack's version. The constraints (values, groups, implications, exclusions and hints) are frozen again from the served pack; the asked and derived axes stay; the items and every answer are kept as given, never rewritten. A kept answer that names a value by an identity the pack renamed is read through the pack's alias. A kept answer the new constraints would refuse is kept, reported, and marked for a second look in its item's review item (`second_look`), and a close leaves its item open rather than turn it into a decision. What an answer derived stays as recorded, and the report names where the served pack's rules would derive otherwise. Refused while an item is leased (a lease past its end ends first), for an answer whose value the pack dropped with no alias, and under the version the campaign already has; a dry run writes nothing. Audited as campaign.repack.

### Changed

- The MRI pack is version 0.7.0: `body_part` gains `other`, the unknown (record 50), which no rule writes, and `brain` and `brain-neck` gain the descriptions raters read them by.
- Claims in order of value take an item's outside suggestion into account: its confidence stands for the item's, and suggesters that disagree come first.

## [1.0.0-alpha.45] - 2026-09-26

The MRI pack's rules hold between axes: pack contract 6 lets a pack say exclusions and hints, and the MRI pack 0.6.0 carries constraints checked against public sources, technique DCE, ASL under its own name and some 40 vendors' names. A classification is held to the pack's own exclusions and implications: where the rules' answer for a stack breaks one, a `classify.excluded` or `classify.implied` review item names it and the axes it involves drop to confidence 0.3. The reader's search leaves out the words a route reads as its cue. The registry stays at schema 68, as at 1.0.0-alpha.44. The HTTP API contract stays version 7, amended in place with additive fields; the pack contract is version 6; the job contract stays version 1. An install fetches Kvasir 1.0.0-alpha.9 and the assistant 1.0.0-alpha.27, as with 1.0.0-alpha.44.

### Added

- Pack contract 6: a pack may say `excludes` and `hints`, constraints between axes over axis values alone. An exclusion rules values of one axis out where its condition holds, as `{axis, is}`, `{axis, any}` or `{axis, family}`; a hint names the value usually found on an axis, with its reason and the sources that show it is not always so. An axes campaign freezes both into its `constraints` beside the groups and the implications, an answer that holds both sides of an exclusion is refused with its reason, and a hint refuses nothing. `nils pack shape` names them. A campaign made before carries neither and reads as before, and an engine at 6 loads a contract-5 pack unchanged.
- An axis value may say `aliases`, identities it had before a rename, which still name it in a campaign's values, an overlay's `lists` and a commit by filter.
- A classification is held to the pack's own exclusions and implications, as a rater's answer is. Where the rules' answer for a stack breaks one (an exclusion whose condition holds and whose value the stack has, or an implication of another rule set whose value it contradicts), the values stay as decided, a `classify.excluded` or `classify.implied` review item names the constraint, its reason and its sources, and the axes it involves are written at confidence 0.3, below every threshold. The classify report counts them (`broken`, `broken_stacks`). An implication broken by its own rule set's order is not a break.

### Changed

- The MRI pack is version 0.6.0, pack contract 6, after record 48 checked its constraints against public sources. Technique `DCE` is a value of its own and takes the word `dce` from `Perfusion-EPI`, which is now the DSC series; technique `ASL-EPI` is `ASL`, with `ASL-EPI` as its alias and its stored label unchanged. Base is decided after construct: a diffusion map is base DWI, a perfusion map PWI, a SWI construct SWI, an MP2RAGE's second inversion PDw where it was T1w; an MP2RAGE output is technique MP2RAGE where nothing else decided it; a water or fat image holds modifier Dixon; a DSC or DCE series had contrast. A multi-echo GRE is T2*-weighted only where the file says it is no Dixon or in and opposed phase, and is no longer a constraint. SWI, T2*w, QSM and R2* rule out the spin-echo techniques, a TOF rules out base T2w, and an intensity projection rules out a raw reconstruction. Eight hints say what is usual, BOLD being T2*-weighted and ASL being acquired before contrast among them. Terms are corrected and some 40 vendors' names added, and the contrast words gain the round 4 phrases in the spelling the normalizer leaves. `PROVENANCE.md` lists every change and its sources.

### Fixed

- The reader's search leaves out a route's cue words. A keyword a route's rule reads (EPIMix, SWI, SyMRI) is the cue for the whole combination that rule sets, and the served vocabulary no longer offers it as a name of every value the rule sets: typing "sw" on technique no longer offers EPI. The packs door still shows every word, and a word the axis file itself lists for a value stays its name. MyC and qmaps become terms of MyelinMap and MultiQmap.

## [1.0.0-alpha.44] - 2026-09-25

The reader looks an answer up: the pack's values carry the other names a person knows them by, a question serves its vocabulary, and a new door counts how common each whole answer is. The MRI pack is version 0.5.0, where a T1-weighted FLAIR is no candidate for role `t1w`. An expired lease never blocks, a campaign's owner may give back another rater's claim, and the digest leaves no empty stacks behind, with `nils repair empty-stacks` for those an earlier digest left. The registry stays at schema 68, as at 1.0.0-alpha.43. The HTTP API contract stays version 7, amended in place with additive fields and two new doors; the job contract stays version 1. An install fetches Kvasir 1.0.0-alpha.9 and the assistant 1.0.0-alpha.27, as with 1.0.0-alpha.43.

### Added

- The reader finds a value by any name it goes by. An axis or an axes question as served (`GET /api/campaigns/{id}`) says `vocabulary`: for each value a rater may answer, its label where it differs, its description where the pack gives one, its `terms` and its `keywords`. It is the pack's vocabulary, the same for every stack, so a rater who reads blind has it too. It is never stored with the question, and a question that says it is refused.
- The MRI pack gives its values `terms`, the other names a person knows them by: vendors' names for one sequence (BRAVO, IR-FSPGR, 3D TFE and TFL for MPRAGE; SPACE, CUBE and VISTA for 3D TSE; TrueFISP, FIESTA and bFFE for bSSFP), spellings and radiologists' words. They are display only: no rule reads them, and no verdict moves. An axis value may also say `description`. The pack door (`GET /api/packs/{name}`, `nils pack show --json`) says both.
- `GET /api/campaigns/{id}/combinations`: how common each combination of an axes question's answered axes is across the registry's classifications, most common first, for the reader's whole-combination search. No stack of the campaign and no stack of a sample sealed now is counted, so a count says nothing of any stack a rater reads. A combination outside the question's vocabulary or forbidden by its constraints is left out and counted apart.
- `nils repair empty-stacks [--dry-run] [--json]` removes the stacks and series that hold no instance, which an earlier digest left where every file of a series was a duplicate of an instance held under another series. A stack a decision, a pick, a seal, a campaign item, an answered review item, a release, a derivative or a measure names is kept and reported with the reason; what is derived from the stack alone (its fingerprint, its classification, the open review items raised on it) goes with it. Audited as `registry.repair`, and it moves the epoch.

### Changed

- The MRI pack is version 0.5.0: role `t1w` no longer takes a T1-weighted FLAIR. A T1w stack whose modifier holds FLAIR (a T1-FLAIR, however the modifier axis found it) is a candidate for no role, since T1-weighted FLAIR is rare and special and is not used as a session's main T1w for analysis; its base is T1w, so it is no candidate FLAIR either. A session whose only T1 is a T1-FLAIR has no T1w pick, and a pipeline's pre-flight leaves the stack out as picked by nothing. Picks made before stay until the stacks are classified again under this pack and `nils pick run` runs, which drops a T1-FLAIR from `t1w` and may pick another T1w of the session in its place; a person's pick of a T1-FLAIR stands. The `terms` and `description` its values gained above are display only and change no verdict. Every other value and rule is as it was.
- The campaign's owner and a holder of review:work give back another rater's claim, at `POST /api/campaigns/{id}/assignments/{assignment}/release`, and the operator at the keyboard does with `nils campaign release <assignment>`. The audit row names the holder.

### Fixed

- A lease past its end holds nothing. `nils campaign requestion` was refused while a lease that had run out 25 minutes earlier was still written `leased`; a requestion and a close now end such a lease as expired first, the batches door no longer counts it against its item, and a release of it ends it as expired, whoever asks.
- The digest no longer leaves stacks and series with no instance. A file whose instance another file holds under another series is filed as a duplicate after its series and stack were written; at the end of the run the stacks and series it alone made are removed, and the report counts them (`empty_stacks_removed`, `empty_series_removed`) apart from those created. `nils repair empty-stacks` removes the ones an earlier digest left.

## [1.0.0-alpha.43] - 2026-09-25

The reader after its first real read (record 48): blind hides the systems' answers, never the file, and a rater answers only what needs a person. A pipeline run keeps its outputs apart from its scratch, and its pre-flight counts the units the release makes. The registry is at schema 68 after this update (66 at 1.0.0-alpha.42), and it migrates when this version first opens it. The HTTP API contract stays version 7, amended in place with additive fields and two new doors; the job contract stays version 1. An install fetches Kvasir 1.0.0-alpha.9 and the assistant 1.0.0-alpha.27, as with 1.0.0-alpha.42.

### Added

- Blind shows the file. The evidence line of a stack and of a campaign item carries `texts`, the header's text in full (series description, protocol name, sequence name and variant, scanning sequence, scan options, image type, acquisition type, body part examined, the contrast agent and route, the image comments and derivation description, the study description), and `physics` (TR, TE, TI, flip angle, echo train length, b-values, bandwidth, field strength, manufacturer and model, thickness and spacing, matrix, pixel spacing, orientation, slices), on a blind item as on any other; the quasi-identifying text shows at detail quasi and above. Still hidden on a blind item: the suggestion, the rules' values and votes, System 1's scores, the matched words and the candidates.
- `GET /api/campaigns/{id}/items/{item}/header`: the whole stored header of the item's stack (its study, series, the series' detail, the stack and one representative instance), less what names a person: nothing of the subject's row, nothing of the groups the pseudonymiser always removes or of the release's `ids` group, no UID that names a file or study, and no quasi-identifying column below detail quasi. The campaign doors' scope holds, so nothing opens for a stack outside the caller's own campaigns.
- Asked and derived axes. An axes question may name `derive`, the axes the rater is not asked, which the engine computes from each answer through the served pack's own rules with the asked axes held at the answer, leaving out a route that reads the file's words to decide an asked axis (`nils_pack::derive`); without `derive` it derives the rest of the pack's axes where every one of them can be derived, and nothing otherwise. Over Phase 0's seven asked axes (provenance, technique, modifier, construct, base, body part, post contrast) that is directory type, disposition, convertible and role from the answer, and quality from ImageType. An axis whose rules read the file's numbers or words, or an axis nobody answered, is refused as derived, so a derived value is always the rater's answer carried through the pack. Can't tell on an asked axis makes can't tell of every derived axis that reads it. An answer keeps its `derived` values, the answer door says them, and an export marks each label `derived` true or false in a new `derived` column of labels.tsv.
- `POST /api/campaigns/{id}/items/{item}/derive`: the derived axes of a partial or whole answer, for the reader to show as the rater answers; it writes nothing.
- `nils campaign requestion <campaign> --axes A,B [--derive C,D]`: moves an open axes campaign to fewer asked axes and derives the rest, keeping its items and every answer. An answer given under the old form keeps its value as given, is read on the axes asked now, and gains what it derives. Refused while an item is leased, for an axis that was not asked before, and under another pack version than the campaign's.
- `nils pipeline lane --output-place <place> --scratch-place <place>` names the working place a run's outputs go to (the derivatives and tables it registers, under `derivatives/<pipeline>/<run>/`) and the one its scratch goes to (its input, each unit's folder, its log and apptainer's images, under `runs/<run>/` and `images/`). Unset or `default`, each is the first active working place, as before, and a place that is not an active working place is refused. A run records both (schema 67), so a resumed run goes on where it started; derivative and model inputs are read from the output place first, then the scratch place. The capability, `nils pipeline lane` and the pre-flight name both places.

### Changed

- The pre-flight counts the units the release makes. It counted a BIDS input's units from the picks, where the run's units are the folders of the release that builds its input, so it could count a unit more than the run had and call a session ready whose T1w pick the pack's BIDS mapping releases as `FLAIR`. The release and the pre-flight now share one per-stack plan: a session whose picked stacks the release keeps in `sourcedata/`, holds or merges is counted as the run meets it, `left_out` says why, and a role counts only when a stack picked for it is released under the role's own suffix. A unit missing an input is not run: the run marks it `skipped`, counts it in `summary.units.missing_input` and raises no review item.

### Fixed

- The viewing pyramid reads a file whose private `UL` element declares a ragged length (six bytes, from some older scanners). The header reader already repaired such a length in memory; the whole-file read the pyramid uses now makes the same repair, pixel data included, when a parse comes back truncated, so those series' stacks are no longer counted `unreadable`. The file on disk is untouched, and a file that really is cut short is still refused.

## [1.0.0-alpha.42] - 2026-09-25

Every frame of a multi-frame file in the viewing pyramid, enhanced MR and CT included. The registry stays at schema 66, as at 1.0.0-alpha.41. The HTTP API contract stays version 7, amended in place with additive fields only; the job contract stays version 1. An install fetches Kvasir 1.0.0-alpha.9 and the assistant 1.0.0-alpha.27, as with 1.0.0-alpha.41.

### Fixed

- The viewing pyramid reads every frame of a multi-frame file as a plane, where it read the first frame alone, so an enhanced MR or CT (the whole volume in one file) or a classic multi-frame file showed as one slice. Native data and every compressed syntax the pyramid decodes are read frame by frame, a frame spread over several fragments included. An enhanced object's frames are placed by their Plane Position and Plane Orientation, with their Pixel Measures and Pixel Value Transformation, per frame or shared, and ordered along the normal as single-frame files are. A stack may mix multi-frame and single-frame files, and a file whose frames the digest split between stacks gives each stack the frames the digest assigned it. A classic multi-frame file whose frames say no position of their own is stacked in frame order at its Spacing Between Slices or Slice Thickness.

### Added

- The pyramid manifest's `order` (`position`, `instance` or `frames`, the last where a multi-frame file's frames were stacked in file order) and `multiframe_files`.

## [1.0.0-alpha.41] - 2026-09-25

Compressed DICOM in the viewing pyramid, and analyses that run under apptainer. The registry stays at schema 66, as at 1.0.0-alpha.40. The HTTP API contract stays version 7, amended in place with additive fields only; the job contract stays version 1. An install fetches Kvasir 1.0.0-alpha.9 and the assistant 1.0.0-alpha.27, as with 1.0.0-alpha.40.

### Added

- The viewing pyramid decodes compressed pixel data: JPEG baseline and extended at eight bits, JPEG lossless (process 14 and its first-order prediction), JPEG-LS lossless and near-lossless, JPEG 2000 lossless and lossy, RLE, deflated explicit VR little endian and explicit VR big endian, as well as native little endian as before, and a stack may mix them. Signed samples and the rescale are read as for native data. The manifest names the stack's `source_syntaxes` and says `lossy` when a plane came from a lossy source (a syntax that may be lossy, or a file that says Lossy Image Compression 01). JPEG at twelve bits (extended, process 4) is not decoded yet.
- `pyramid build` over a selection or a handle counts every failure by its reason in `failures_by_reason`, beside the first twenty it lists; a plane whose codec refused it is counted as `undecodable`, and `compressed` now means a transfer syntax the pyramid has no decoder for.

### Fixed

- A unit under apptainer gets its command as written: `apptainer run` is given `--no-eval`, so the image's runscript no longer evaluates the words after the image through a shell and a starter's `bash -c` script keeps its command substitutions, variables and inner quotes, and `--pwd /`, so a contained image starts in its own root. Podman and docker pass the words as before.
- The SynthStrip starter declares 8 GB of memory, seeded as the name's next version: on the CPU it peaks near 6 GB, and a unit held to the 4 GB it declared before was killed.

## [1.0.0-alpha.40] - 2026-09-25

Record 48's blind reading. Raters read their own campaign's pictures without `query:see` and are blind to each other, a rater may answer can't tell on an axis and mark an answer unsure, and one bare DICOM file no longer fails a pyramid. The registry is at schema 66 after this update (65 at 1.0.0-alpha.39), and it migrates when this version first opens it. The HTTP API contract stays version 7, amended in place; the job contract stays version 1. An install fetches Kvasir 1.0.0-alpha.9 and the assistant 1.0.0-alpha.27, as with 1.0.0-alpha.39.

### Added

- Pictures for a campaign's raters (record 48). The picture doors (`GET /api/instances/{stack}/manifest`, `tiles`, `slab` and `render`) open to a caller without `query:see` who holds `campaigns:work`, for exactly the stacks of an open campaign that names them as a rater or an adjudicator, at detail quasi as before. Any other stack is refused with a 403 before anything of it is looked up, and nothing else of the stack opens: not the ask, the explanation or the evidence line outside the campaign. The `instance.open` audit row names the campaign the pictures were read through.
- Can't tell and unsure (record 48, how the reference is read). In an axes answer any asked axis may be `"cant_tell"`, the rater's answer where the data give no clue, distinct from null (the axis has no value here) and from a pack's own fallback such as `Unknown`; every asked axis is still named, so none is skipped by accident. The pack's constraints read a can't-tell axis as unnamed, so no implication or exclusion group refuses it. Two raters who both say it agree on the axis, and can't tell against a value disagrees. It never becomes a decision: the close writes none on that axis, leaves its adopted question open and lists it in the item's outcome as `cant_tell`. The agreement counts it per axis. Answers, outcomes and label sets carry the word as it is, and the body-part pipeline drops it from the labels it trains on. A suggestion, System 1's candidates and the whole-item answer at Review's apply door never take it, and a pack that names the word cannot back an axes question. An answer may be marked `unsure` (`--unsure` at the keyboard), kept on the answer, counted in the agreement and exported in a new last column of `labels.tsv`; it never bears on whether the answer fits. A campaign's question as served says `unsure` and, for an axes question, `cant_tell`. The registry moves to schema 66, which adds the answer's `unsure` column.

### Changed

- Raters are blind to each other (record 48). A holder of `review:work` reads every campaign as before; anyone else reads the campaigns they made, are named in as a rater or adjudicator, or hold an assignment in, and every door under `/api/campaigns/{id}` answers 404 for another. In an open campaign, a caller who does not read every answer sees an item another person was given without its `outcome`, `agreement` and `metric` (and `blind: true`), only their own assignments and no open agreement, and cannot export its outcomes or answers until it closes. Label sets are read by a holder of `review:see`, or by their writer and the people of their campaign; a set of an open campaign's outcomes holds its files back as its answers do.
- `POST /api/label-sets` reads every stack's decisions, so it also needs `review:see`; a campaign made from `{handle}` needs `query:see` and one made from `{review}` needs `review:see`, so a rater cannot name stacks into a campaign of their own.

### Fixed

- One file without the Part 10 preamble and meta group no longer fails its stack's pyramid: the pyramid reads such a file as the digest does, a bare data set in implicit or explicit VR little endian.

## [1.0.0-alpha.39] - 2026-09-25

Records 45, 48 and 49's engine halves. The registry is at schema 65 after this update (62 at 1.0.0-alpha.38), and it migrates when this version first opens it. The HTTP API contract stays version 7, amended in place with additive doors and fields only; the job contract stays version 1, amended in place with additive keys only.

### Added

- The reader's evidence line (record 48 R1). `GET /api/stacks/{stack}/why` under `review:see`, and `GET /api/campaigns/{id}/items/{item}/why` for a rater under `campaigns:see`, answer one entry per axis: the value in force and who set it, the rule and the clause that decided, the other rules that voted with their values, the header values the deciding clause read (the pack says which, through its flags and parsers: `nils_pack::reads`), System 1's candidates where it asked about the stack, and the entry as one line. The words a rule matched, why a person decided and the sequence name show at detail quasi or above only. A stack of a sample sealed now that an open campaign asks is read blind, until the campaign closes, by the campaign's raters and by anyone who neither adjudicates it nor holds review:work: the evidence door answers only the raw header and where the pictures are, and the explanation, the review item doors and a pick item's candidates say nothing a system said of it, each with `blind` true; so the reference labels are not anchored to the model a certificate grades. Claims by value never rank such a stack by worth. `matched` at `GET /api/explain/{stack}` shows at detail quasi or above only. The item's door adds the suggested answer (the rules' value, or the value the rules and System 1 agree on; none where they disagree) and what the value order reads of it.
- Batches of like stacks (record 48 R1). `GET /api/campaigns/{id}/batches` groups the items open to the caller by the deciding rules, the header values their clauses read with the sequence the stack was made with, and the suggested answer. `POST /api/campaigns/{id}/batches/{batch}/accept` answers each item with the suggestion, one answer per item, each still its own decision at the close, in one transaction, and holds back the campaign's share of the whole batch to be read alone, whatever items the caller names, which no later batch takes. The share is the campaign's `hold_back`, set by its maker (a tenth at least, `--hold-back` at the keyboard), and the seed that chooses the items is the engine's and never returned; a request that says either is refused. A batch's signature hashes the sequence name and never returns it. An item of a sample sealed now is never in a batch and is refused if named.
- Claims in order of value (record 48 R1): `order` value on `POST /api/campaigns/{id}/claim`, and `nils campaign claim --order value`, takes first the items where the two systems or the rules disagree, then the least confident.
- Every answer is timed (record 48 R1): `seconds` from its claim, to the millisecond, the engine's `suggested` answer, whether the answer `changed` it, and `via` claim or batch. `GET /api/campaigns/{id}/stats` and `nils campaign stats` give per rater the answers, those read and those batched, the median and ninetieth percentile seconds to a tenth, and the share of suggestions changed; a rater reads their own row alone, without the totals, until the campaign closes.
- Certificates, and labels back into training (record 48 R2). `POST /api/certificates` records what a certification measured on a sealed sample, as a person's act at the door only (`nils labels certificate` and `nils labels unseal` refuse and point to the doors, and `--auth off` refuses both, since it verifies no identity), with a result that names the sample, its digest (which `nils labels seal` now gives), the risk certified, the stacks read and the errors found; `POST /api/certificates/{id}/unseal` unseals that sample, and only that one, by a person other than the one who recorded the certificate, told apart by the identity the engine verified, keeping the seal's rows as history, so a label set holding it trains the next model and `usable_for_training` takes it. A set is read again only from a labels.tsv whose sha256 is its digest; one whose file changed or is gone is held to the seal it was written under and the stacks of the list it pinned, and one that pinned none is not training data. `nils labels export --for-training` and `for_training` at `POST /api/label-sets` write the development labels: every person's decision in force (or those of the author kinds named), on one axis or all, with nothing of a sample still sealed. Audited as `labels.certificate` and `labels.unseal`; neither moves the epoch.
- A lane of its own for pipeline runs (record 49 A1). `nils serve --worker` runs two workers: one takes every job but a pipeline run, the other pipeline runs only, so a long run never holds up a digest, a classify or a release; `nils jobs work --lane all|main|pipelines` does the same by hand, `all` as before. A descriptor may run its units apart (`x-nils.units: apart`, `contracts/job/v1`): each unit gets a container of its own that sees its own input alone (the dataset's top-level files and its subject's or session's folder in the bids layout, a `stacks.json` of its one stack and the folders of its files in the stacks layout), named its subject in `[ParticipantLabels]` and its unit in `NILS_UNIT`, and writes `derivatives/<pipeline>/<run>/<unit>/` with a `results.json` of its own. The lane runs as many at once as the cores and memory each declares (`x-nils.needs.cores`, `memory-gb`; one core and 2 GB where absent) fit in its budget, 48 cores and 512 GB until `nils pipeline lane --cores <n> --memory-gb <n>` sets it, and never more than this machine or its container offers (the cgroup's memory cap is read); a unit that could never fit is refused before the run starts. Each container is told `NILS_CORES` and `NILS_MEMORY_GB`, and runs in a process group of its own so a stop reaches every process it started. A pipeline with a run-level output runs its units together, as every pipeline did.
- Runs are taken up again (record 49 A1). Each unit is a row of the new `pipeline_unit` table (migration 64): queued, running, registering or over, with its attempts, what it held of the budget, the card it leased, its container and process, its exit code and its outcome. A cancel stops the units in flight and closes the run as cancelled with the units it finished kept; `nils run --resume <run>`, the same at `POST /api/jobs`, and `nils jobs resume` of the run's job go on where it stopped: a unit over stays over with its derivatives, a unit in flight runs again from a clean folder after what a killed engine left of it is stopped, and a unit whose container had ended is taken in from what it left without registering anything twice. A run whose engine went away is found by the pipeline lane's worker, marked `interrupted` and queued to go on under what its job recorded, three times at most. A run records `units`, `resumes` and the caller's `threshold`, which a resume keeps; `GET /api/pipeline-runs/{id}` and `nils pipeline runs <run>` add `unit_states` and `units_run`.
- Apptainer as the unprivileged path (record 49 A2). `nils pipeline runtime --set apptainer-first` looks for apptainer before podman; the order is otherwise podman, apptainer, and docker only by choice. Apptainer 1.1 or later is taken, and runs `run --containall --cleanenv --no-home --net --network none` with the unit's folders bound (`run`, so the image's ENTRYPOINT comes before the command line as under podman, where `exec` would skip it), from an image built once from the pinned digest into `<working>/images/<digest>.sif`, or a sandbox folder with `--apptainer-image sandbox` where there is no `/dev/fuse`, and kept by that digest.
- A GPU lease (record 49 A2). A GPU unit starts only when the free memory of the lane's card, read with `nvidia-smi --query-gpu=memory.free --format=csv,noheader -i <card>`, less what the lane's own running units there declared, covers its `x-nils.needs.gpu-memory-gb` (8 where absent); until then it waits and the run's progress says why. It holds its lease until its container ends and is given that card alone (`nvidia.com/gpu=<card>`, `--gpus device=<card>`, or `--nv` with `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES`). The card is 0 until `nils pipeline lane --gpu-card <n|none>` sets it; with none, a unit that needs a GPU is refused and an optional one runs on the CPU.
- Secret inputs (record 49 R3). A descriptor declares `x-nils.secrets` (an id, where the container sees it, and a variable that names it, such as `FS_LICENSE`); `nils pipeline secret set <id> --file <path>` keeps the file's path, never its bytes, and `list` and `unset` read and forget it. The file is read when a run starts, a run that needs one not set is refused before anything runs, and it is mounted read-only into that pipeline's containers alone. After each container every file it left that holds the secret is removed and refused as a `pipeline:qc` item, its log and `results.json` are written again with the secret replaced by `[secret <id>]`, and the run records the secret's id alone. `capabilities.pipelines` adds `lane`, `apptainer_image` and `secrets` (ids only).
- A run's numbers in the ask (record 49 A3, R4). A descriptor declares a `table` output, a file of numbers with typed columns (`number`, `integer`, `text`, each with its unit), one row per unit, in CSV, TSV or JSON; a run's own table names each row's unit in its `unit-column`. The runner registers the file as a derivative of kind `table` and loads its rows as the units' measures (a new registry table, `measure`), and the ask reads each as a field of the unit's grain, `measure.<pipeline>.<column>` on a stack, a session or a subject, the value of the newest run that measured the unit, with `measure.<pipeline>.run` naming that run. A measure is quasi identifying: below detail quasi the ask refuses it in a column, an order, a group's key and any binding but a total over a group (count, distinct, sum, avg, min, max), and allows it in a predicate. A run that loaded measures moves the epoch.
- The lane holds a container to what its unit declares (record 49, after review). Podman and docker are given `--cpus` and `--memory`, and apptainer too where the host's cgroups delegate the controllers; `x-nils.needs.cores-input` and `memory-input` name the parameters a tool is told its threads and memory by, set to the unit's declared values when left out and refused above them. A GPU unit is given only the card its lease holds: CDI names that card for podman, and apptainer's `--nv` is told `CUDA_DEVICE_ORDER=PCI_BUS_ID` with `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES` and `NVIDIA_VISIBLE_DEVICES` of it.
- Secret inputs are swept harder (record 49 R3, after review). Rewrites are written where no container saw, new and never through a link; a cached apptainer image is used only when it matches the digest recorded at its build; a run stopped by a cancel or an error has its units in flight swept too; a folder a container shut is given back and swept, and what still cannot be read fails its unit; gzip streams (a `.nii.gz`, an `.mgz`, a `.tar.gz`) and tar members are read inside, the secret is looked for as base64 as well, and an archive the sweep cannot open is removed and refused. The job's heart beats while a unit's files are swept and hashed.
- Small groups of a measure are withheld below detail quasi (record 49 R4, ruled after review). A group set that totals a measure, or groups a set filtered on one, shows its totals, `_rows` and `_subjects` only for 5 scans or more (k = 5, D27's federation default, for measures alone; D51 stands for everything else), and a count or existence answered over a set filtered on a measure is shown for none or for 5 and more. A list of the rows of a set filtered on a measure is refused below detail quasi, since the list says each member's measure against the bound. A measure is read from the unit's newest run of the pipeline's current version, and `measure.<pipeline>.run` names that run.
- A run's checks and failures are totals below detail quasi (record 49 R4 and R4b, after the assistant's review). Below detail quasi the run doors (`GET /api/pipeline-runs` and `/{id}`), the jobs doors and their event stream for a pipeline job, the review doors for a `pipeline:qc` item and `GET /api/explain/{stack}` say no unit, no unit id, no scan's value and no error text a tool wrote. A run's summary then carries `breaches_by_check` and `failures_by_reason` in place of its per-unit breaches and refusals, `units_run` is empty, a refused table value is counted by its column, and a count that stands for 1 to 4 scans is null and marked withheld (`withheld: true` in a list, its field named in `summary.withheld`); `summary.detail` says `totals`.
