The review list says a run's `pipeline:qc` items as one entry a run, an item status and a check (a breach) or a reason (a failure), `grouped: true` with its count of `units` held the same way, never one a unit; such an item is not read by its id (404), `GET /api/explain/{stack}` leaves them out, the review summary holds their open count, and a run's `summary.review_items` is a count. At detail quasi and above the doors answer as before.
- The pipeline doors read their path decoded: `POST /api/pipelines/volumes%401/preflight` is the pre-flight of `volumes@1`, as the query is decoded.
- Declared checks (record 49 A3). `x-nils.qc` holds each unit that succeeded to its thresholds (`snr >= 8`, `{metric: euler, op: "<=", value: 200}`), the metric read from the unit's `results.json` metrics or else from its tables; a breach is one `pipeline:qc` review item, status `breach`, whose error names the metric, its value and the check, and a breach does not make a run partial. A metric a check reads from `results.json` is kept as a measure. The run's `summary.numbers` counts the tables, rows, measures, missing columns, refused values and breaches.
- The pre-flight of a run (record 49 A3). `nils run <pipeline> --select ... --preflight` and `POST /api/pipelines/{name}/preflight` say, before anything runs, the units a run would have, the units that lack an input and why (a session without a pick of a role the descriptor names under `x-nils.input.roles`, a stack with no file or no derivative its typed input needs), the stacks no pick takes, the time from the pipeline's own past runs here or else from `x-nils.needs.unit-minutes`, whether it wants a GPU and what it would run on, and a unit's cores and memory against the lane's budget, with `ready` and the blockers. Below detail quasi the door names no unit by its subject or session.
- The starter catalog (record 49 A4). The engine seeds R1's first analyses into its catalog at its start, each marked `origin: starter` and pinned by its registry manifest digest: N4, SynthStrip and SynthSeg, SAMSEG with lesions and FreeSurfer 8.2.0 recon-all (all on the FreeSurfer 8.2.0 image), and MRIQC 24.0.2, each with its tables, checks, roles and a unit's minutes, and each running its sessions apart in the pipeline lane. recon-all declares the lab's FreeSurfer licence as a secret input. A version a person added, or a starter a person retired, is never gone over. `nils pipeline starter` lists them, `--seed` seeds now, and `--off` and `--on` set whether the engine seeds at its start. segcsvd is not a starter: it has no public registry image to pin.
- A selection's pictures are one job (record 45 E1). `nils pyramid build --select selection:<name>@<v>` or `--handle <id>`, and the same command line at `POST /api/jobs` under `pipelines:work`, builds the pyramids of every stack as one job of kind `pyramid`, skips a stack whose pyramid is built, counts one it cannot read with the class of the reason (`no_files`, `compressed`, `unsupported_pixels`, `mixed_matrix`, `unreadable` or `build_failed`, never the reader's words, which can name a file's path, since a job's result is served at detail plain) and goes on, and ends with built, skipped and failed in its result, so a run after a failure builds only what is missing. The door adds the deployment's packs and refuses any other flag. A campaign made (`POST /api/campaigns`, `nils campaign create --json`) answers `pictures {stacks, have, missing}` and the job that builds the rest, `pyramid build --handle <id>` (R3: pictures are built for campaign items, not for the whole archive).
- The pyramid manifest says where the planes are (record 45 E2): `orientation`, the six direction cosines of Image Orientation Patient, `origin`, the first plane's position, `frame {parallel, evenly_spaced}`, `plane` and `oblique`. The planes are ordered along the normal the orientation gives rather than by the third coordinate, and the spacing is measured along it. A manifest written before reads as axial with `orientation_known` false.
- A commit by filter takes `model`, `axis`, `from`, `to` and `stacks` (record 45 E3), at `POST /api/decisions/commit` and as `nils review commit --model --axis --from --to --stacks`: a registered model's answers by id, digest or name@version, one change of a change matrix (what the stack holds now, the decision in force or else the classifier's value, to what the decision sets), or some stacks. A model's group whose members do not all match is split: the matching members are each written as a decision of their stack by the same model and put in force, and the group's decision stays staged for the rest. Values compare as the served pack's identities, so a label reads as its value. The rule of who commits a model's answer is unchanged (record 42 R6).
- The `axes` question (record 45 E4, R2): a campaign asks several axes of a stack at once, `{kind: axes, axes, values?}` or `nils campaign create --axes base,technique,modifier`. The engine freezes the served pack's legal combinations into the question (the vocabulary, the multi-valued axes, the exclusion groups, and the schema implications `nils pack shape` reports), a caller may not send its own, and an answer `{axis: value | [values] | null}` the pack forbids is refused before anything is written, with the rule or the group that forbids it. Over open `<axis>:*` review items it asks each stack once. An axis whose adopted question on a stack someone decided while the campaign was open keeps that decision, and the close skips that axis and says so, as it skips a group's member item. Agreement is measured whole and per axis, and the close writes one decision per axis through apply, one transaction per item, under the same rule of who puts a model's or an agent's answer in force. A label set of it has a row per axis.
- System 1's evidence shape is fixed (record 45 R5): the `classify.asked` review item, one open per stack, whose evidence carries the joint answers the pack allows with their probabilities, the rules' and the model's evidence per axis, the axes they agree on, and the certificate's risk level, group and whether the stack was decided without a person. `contracts/review-item/v4` gains its evidence schema and a whole item as an example. The engine does not raise it yet; a hidden fixture, `nils review asked`, writes one held to the shape and the pack, only where `NILS_FIXTURES=1` marks the registry as a fixture's and never without the pack, its evidence marked `fixture: true`. An item that asks several axes is answered whole with `values` at `POST /api/review/{id}/apply`, one decision per axis in one transaction. An item an open campaign asks is refused at either form of that door (409, naming the campaign): the campaign answers it and its close closes it.
- The rating workspace's lease and blind rating are the engine's (record 45). `POST /api/campaigns/{id}/assignments/{assignment}/renew` lengthens a lease its holder still holds, and nobody else's; the answers door shows a rater only their own answers until the campaign closes, and so does the export: an export of an open campaign's answers is refused to anyone else, and a label set of them is read without its files until the close, and every answer to an adjudicator and to a holder of `review:work`; an `axes` answer reads back as the object it was sent as.
- A pick campaign's session item names its candidates (record 45): `GET /api/campaigns/{id}/items/{item}/candidates` answers the session's stacks with what the classifier says of each and the picks standing for the role, at detail quasi.
- A model's proposals say what they would change (record 45): an `<axis>:model` group's evidence counts what its members hold now under `from`, and each member's evidence names its own, so the desk draws the change matrix.

### Fixed

- Every door reads its query decoded (record 45): `?kind=body_part%3Amodel` matched nothing, since only the derivative doors undid the percent-encoding; it is percent-decoding only, so a `+` keeps its meaning, as in a time's `+02:00` offset or a principal.
- A signed or rescaled stack renders in the modality's values (record 45). The render door and the window now apply the file's Rescale Slope and Intercept after the shift of a signed volume into u16, so a CT window in Hounsfield units is right; the manifest adds `slope`, a stored value being `stored * slope + intercept`. A signed 8-bit stack was not shifted at all.
- An opened stack is audited once per person in ten minutes, exactly (record 45): the check read only the newest 200 rows, so a grid of more stacks wrote each again. The render door also keeps the manifests and the working place it read, so a grid of 250 tiles renders in a tenth of the time.
- `nils setup --model-server` waits up to 60 seconds for Kvasir to list the stations' purposes before it maps them to the server's model. Right after a restart the list could still lack them, and setup said that no station went to the model and carried on as if it had succeeded. Mapping none now stops an install and is reported on an update, and purposes still missing after the wait are named.
- The body-part pipeline's descriptors pin the published body-part image and its two encoder weights by digest, not placeholders.

### Changed

- An install fetches Kvasir at `v1.0.0-alpha.9` and the assistant at `v1.0.0-alpha.27`: a request waiting on Kvasir's card hears its first heartbeat within a second, and the assistant gains the analysis-plan and run-read stations. `nils update --all` moves both.

## [1.0.0-alpha.38] - 2026-09-24

Records 41, 42, 43 and 47. The registry is at schema 62 after this update (51 at 1.0.0-alpha.37), and it migrates when this version first opens it. The linkage store migrates when it is first opened, and stays at its old version until then. The HTTP API contract is version 7, the grant suite version 3, and two contracts are new, `contracts/model/v1` and `contracts/job/v1`.

### Added

- A model server for the stations from the command line (record 47, E1). `nils setup --model-server URL --model-key-file FILE [--model-server-model ID]`, on an install and with `--update`: once Kvasir runs, setup reads the key from the file and seals it in Kvasir (`PUT /v1/credentials/{name}`: staged as `server-staging:<id>` for a server Kvasir does not hold yet, which Kvasir moves onto the backend once it holds it, and taken back where it does not; under the backend's own id, replacing the key it had, for one it holds), reads the models the server offers (`GET /v1/servers/models`), admits the one the stations use (`POST /v1/servers`), which is the model named, by its id or an alias, else the one the wizard asks for, and maps each of the stations' purposes to it (`PUT /v1/purposes/{id}/policy` with `model`). With nobody to ask, as on every `--update`, a model not named stops the run before it changes anything and names the models the server offers. A model that is not offered, does not answer or does not pass admission stops an install and is said on an update. The key is never shown or written: the setup record keeps `[model_server]` with the address and the key file's path, so an update or a repair seals it again from the file, and `--print` shows both without the key.

- Pipelines have a catalog (record 43 S1, D9). `contracts/job/v1` fixes `nils.job.yml`, v0's Boutiques 0.5 subset with an `x-nils` block, with the stack manifest a runner hands a pipeline and the `results.json` it hands back: the image pinned by its registry manifest digest, the parameters with their types, ranges, choices, defaults and units, the command line with its value-keys (the BIDS-Apps shape when there is none), the analysis level (`participant`, `session` or `stack`), the input layout (`bids` or `stacks`), the typed inputs (`model`, `label_set`, `derivative:<kind>`), the outputs by derivative kind with path templates, the needs (`gpu: none, optional or required`) and the axes it may propose on. `nils pipeline add <nils.job.yml>` checks a descriptor and keeps it whole with its digest as its name's next version, or as the version it already is; an image named by a tag, or by an image id as v0's `container-hash` was, is refused (R3). `nils pipeline list | show | runs` read the catalog and the runs, and `GET /api/pipelines`, `GET /api/pipelines/{id}`, `GET /api/pipeline-runs` and `GET /api/pipeline-runs/{id}` answer them under `pipelines:see`. v0's N4 descriptor is ported under `pipelines/n4-bias-correction/`, pinned to the public ANTs image by its manifest digest and needing no wrapper. The schema is version 59, which adds the `pipeline` and `pipeline_run` tables empty.

- Pipelines run (record 43 S2). `nils run <pipeline> --select selection:<name>@<v> [--param id=value] [--model M] [--labels ID]` freezes the selection into a handle the run pins and runs the pipeline over it as a job of kind `pipeline`, one at a time on the existing worker (R1), queued at `POST /api/jobs` under `pipelines:work` at detail quasi, since a pipeline reads pixels. The runtime is rootless podman, then apptainer, and docker only where an operator opted in with `nils pipeline runtime --set docker` (R2, D18); with none, `capabilities.pipelines.enabled` is false with the reason, and nothing is an error (D1). The input is materialised under `<working>/runs/<run>/`: a release in the BIDS layout with the picks applied (R4), so a BIDS App meets the one image a pick chose per role and session, or `stacks.json`, each stack's files under the source places mounted read-only, with the frames of a multi-frame file. The container has no network, its input and typed inputs are read-only, it writes one folder, `<working>/derivatives/<pipeline>/<run>/`, and its process is the user and not root on the host (`--userns keep-id` for podman, `--user` for docker); a GPU is passed by CDI or `--nv` where the descriptor asks and the host has one, and a pipeline whose need is optional runs on the CPU otherwise and records `device cpu`. After it exits the engine reads `results.json`, or finds each unit's files by the descriptor's templates when there is none, hashes every file itself, registers each as a derivative naming the run, passes the proposals to the review spine's hook (which takes none until record 43 S6), raises a `pipeline:qc` review item for every unit that failed or went unreported, and closes the run with every parameter, the runtime and its version, the host, the device, the models, the label set, the summary and a digest of what it said, which a re-run that makes the same files repeats. Audited as `pipeline.add`, `pipeline.run` and `pipeline.runtime`, none of which moves the epoch; custody lists the store.

- A derivative travels by the door or by a shared path (record 43 S3, decision record 07). A place may declare where the clients that share its volume reach it, `nils place add|set --share <path>` or `share` in the guarantees at the places doors, and then `GET /api/derivatives/{id}/content?transport=share` answers with the path the file is read at there instead of its bytes, gated at detail quasi and audited as the bytes are; a place that declares none says so with 409, and the bytes come through the door as before. A derivative says which transports it goes by, and the listing and `nils derivative list --run` take a run's files.

- The embedding cache (record 43 S4). An embedding is a derivative of kind `embedding`, one per stack, encoder and preprocessing version: one file holding the slice indices it was made from and a float32 matrix, one row per slice, in a format `contracts/job/v1/embedding.md` fixes for the pipeline images (the magic `NILSEMB1`, a JSON header naming the stack, the encoder's weight digest, the preprocessing version, the rows, the width and the slices, then the matrix as little-endian float32 from a 64-byte boundary, which numpy maps without a copy; one self-describing file rather than an `.npy`, which holds one array). An encoder is a model of kind and task `encoder`, registered by the digest of its weights, which stay in the image, once however often an image registers it. The key is a unique index over the live rows (migration 60, after the pipeline catalog and its runs in 59), so a new preprocessing version or a new encoder is a new key: everything is embedded again and the old rows stay. A runner asks which stacks have an embedding under the key already and gives the pipeline only the rest; an output for a stack the key holds is not registered, and the row there stays, because two devices do not give the same bytes. A withdrawn embedding frees its key. A derivative registered at the door without a preprocessing version is outside the key.

- A pipeline's proposals become review items and staged decisions (record 43 S6). The `proposals` of a run's `results.json` (`contracts/job/v1/proposals.schema.json`) carry per stack an axis, the value proposed, the probability of every class and the registered model, by id or digest. They become `<axis>:model` review items, one grouped question per axis, model, value and confidence band (below the task's threshold, or at it and at 0.9, 0.95 and 0.99 above it), each stack a member with its probabilities as its evidence. A group at or above the threshold gets a staged decision by the model through the one write path, when the model is admitted or promoted and registered for the axis; a group below it is a review item only, and nothing is in force until a person commits it, by id or with `--min-confidence`, which reads the lowest confidence in the group. A stack whose axis a person or an agent decided is not asked again. A run's proposals go in once, in one transaction, and a file that says what the contract does not is refused before anything is written. The review-item contract's kind pattern admits the `model` reason.

- Campaigns: one mechanism for annotation and curation (record 42). v0 ran five QC products that differed only in their workflow and kept nothing of their own; a campaign asks one question of a frozen list of items and closes through the one write path decisions already have. It is made from a saved selection, frozen into a handle the campaign pins, from a handle, or from open review items of a kind or a kind prefix, adopted as they are, one open campaign at a time asking each; every item is backed by a review item, so it is in the ordinary queue. The question is one of five: `axis` (which value of one pack axis, the pack's vocabulary by default), `pick` (which stacks stand for a role in a session), `form` (a small declared form), `derivative` (a file answer, a mask, named by its derivative id) and `free`. Raters claim the next item under a lease, answer and give it back; a lease past its time returns the item to the pool, no rater is ever given the same item twice, and a principal holding a live lease is handed it again rather than a second. An answer is never a decision, because `apply` withdraws the earlier decision on the same key and two raters written as decisions would cancel each other; every answer is kept with its author and the kind its verified actor was. An item holds one answer per rater and round, which the table holds too: the same answer again on the same assignment is answered again and written once. An answer and a metric read the lease and the state again under the campaign's lock, and a close takes the campaign from open to closing before it writes, so two closes never both write; a person runs again a close that died and left its campaign closing, which writes only the items not yet resolved, and each item's decision is written in one transaction with the item's link to it. Once every rater answered, an item is agreed, waits for an external metric (a Dice over masks, which something with pixels computes and posts; the engine only compares it with the threshold), or is offered to an adjudicator in a second round, which the review item's evidence then says. Closing writes each settled item through `apply` into one decision, in force or staged, authored by the adjudicator where there was one, by the model where every answer behind it was one registered model's, and by the one closing otherwise, each as its verified actor, naming its campaign in `campaign_id` and in its why the answers and the models behind it. A decision is in force as the campaign closes only when the answers behind it and the one closing are all persons: an item a model's or an agent's answer settled, or one an agent or a model closes, is staged whatever the campaign says, and only a person commits it, since a model's answer is evidence until a person does (R6), and so, by the wave's ruling, is an agent's. A close may also write into a person's pick, written by the same writer as `nils pick set` and `POST /api/picks` so a pick run leaves it standing, which names its campaign too and is refused for an item a person did not settle or for stacks on another occasion than the item's; or into nothing, the review item closed with the outcome. The close keeps the agreement over the campaign: the share of items all raters agreed on, Fleiss' kappa, and Cohen's where the same two raters answered every item. `nils campaign create | list | show | claim | answer | release | metric | close | export`, the doors under `/api/campaigns`, audited as `campaign.*`, and in custody. At detail plain the doors read a campaign as the rest of the engine reads what is quasi-identifying: a session's item without its subject, its day or the key that spells them, and an answer without its why, its form, its actor detail or a free text; a label set of forms, free text or derivatives is read without its files, its metadata and counts only; claiming in a campaign of sessions needs detail quasi. No table is named for a QC product, and a test holds every table name to that. The grants are new, `campaigns:see` and `campaigns:work` (suite contract version 3), because a rater is not a reviewer of the whole queue; closing needs `review:work` beside them, because it writes decisions. A model that answers names its registered model in `X-Nils-Actor`, the answer keeps it, and a close carries it onto the decision, staged as a model's answer always is; a pick is written in force, so an item a model answered, or a close by an agent or a model, writes no pick and says why. The schema is version 58: 57 adds the four campaign tables and 58 the label sets and the sealed samples.

- A commit by filter. `nils review commit --min-confidence P` and `--campaign C`, and `POST /api/decisions/commit`, commit the part of the staged decisions a filter names and leave the rest staged, as v0's body-part commit did: the confidence of a decision is the agreement of the campaign item that closed into it, else the confidence its review item names. It reads what it chooses by and writes what it commits in a few statements whatever the number of staged decisions, as a commit by id or of all does now, with the campaign item found by its decision through an index. The drift check applies as before, and so does the rule of the commit by id: when the part holds a model's or an agent's answer, an agent or a model committing it is refused and nothing is committed (record 42 R6, extended to agents), at the door by the verified actor and at the keyboard by `NILS_ACTOR`, which `nils review commit` now reads for a commit by id or `--all` as well.

- Label sets. `nils labels export --axis A` writes the decisions in force on an axis, resolved as the classifier resolves them, as `labels.tsv` (stack, subject, what, value, derivative, author kind, author, decision, campaign, model and answer) with `provenance.json` (the source, the handle and its content hash, the epoch, the pack version, the scheme digest and the digest), kept to a saved selection or a handle, which the set pins, to kinds of author, or to a campaign. `nils campaign export` writes a campaign's outcomes, or with `--answers` every answer, in the same form. The digest is the sha256 of the canonical `labels.tsv`, so the same state gives the same digest and one new decision changes it. A set is named and versioned: a set under a name taken is the name's next version, which a door writes into `labels/<name>/v<version>`, and no set writes over another's files, so a directory that holds one is refused at the keyboard; `labels.tsv` is put in place only once the set is recorded, and the digest a set's row holds is always its file's. A set any of whose items is of a sample an operator sealed for certification carries `sealed`, and the registry refuses it as training data and refuses to register a model trained on it (record 40 R3): `nils labels seal --select selection:NAME@V` (or `--handle ID`) keeps the sample's stacks as sealed, before anyone looks, audited as `labels.seal`, and a set is sealed when it holds any of them, or, for a set of sessions, a session of a sealed stack's subject. Whether a set is sealed is the registry's finding: no caller sets it, and a door body that says `sealed` is refused. A model's card names its label set by a digest this registry wrote, or its registration is refused. The doors are `/api/label-sets` and a campaign's export, and each set is audited as `labels.export`.

- v0's human labels come in as person decisions. `nils labels import-v0 --tsv FILE --axis body_part` reads a TSV of SeriesInstanceUID, value and date, maps each label to the stacks of its series, and writes a person's decision on each, marked `imported:v0` and dated as v0 dated it, through the one write path, in one transaction, which also reads what stands, so an import writes every label or none and sees a decision another writer committed while it waited; a stack where a person's decision already stands keeps it, whether the decision was written on the stack, its group, its series or its subject, an import run twice writes nothing new, the values are held to the pack's, and `--dry-run` counts without writing. It reports counts and never a UID. Importing a label set is not a database migration: v0's databases still migrate nowhere (record 42 R5).

- A person may pick the stack that stands for a session's role (record 42). `nils pick set --role ROLE --stack ID [--stack ID] --why TEXT` and `POST /api/picks {role, stacks, why}` write a pick with `author_kind` person, for the stacks of one acquisition on one occasion of one subject under the scheme; `nils pick withdraw ID` and `POST /api/picks/{id}/withdraw` take it out of force. A pick run leaves a person's pick standing: it writes its own pick beside it as evidence that does not apply, pointing at the person's (`overruled_by`), and withdrawing the person's pick lets the run's apply again without a new run. A run's pick is not withdrawn; a person overrules it with a pick of their own, and a newer person's pick on the occasion withdraws the older one. The ask, a release and `nils pick list` read the person's pick where it stands. Only a person writes or withdraws one: both doors refuse a call whose `X-Nils-Actor` names an agent or a model. Both doors are under `review:work`, and both acts are audited as `pick.set` and `pick.withdraw`, which move the epoch. Migration 55 adds `pick.why`, `pick.withdrawn_by` and `pick.overruled_by`, and a session rebuild moves a run's overruled pick with its occasion.

- A session whose pick is ambiguous is a review item (record 42). `nils pick run` raises one open `pick.border` item per occasion whose pick has a border (two candidates too close, a rare winner, nothing eligible), v0's `needs_check`, with the run's numbers and every candidate's stacks as evidence and never a value of a person; a later run brings it up to date or closes it as superseded when nothing is left to doubt, a person's pick answers it, and no item is raised where a person's pick stands. The run's report counts `standing` and `raised`.

- Derivatives have a home (record 42). A derivative is a file made from the archive that is not the archive, a mask, an embedding, a pyramid or a pipeline's output, kept in a working place under `derivatives/<kind>/<two hex>/<sha256><extension>` and named in the new `derivative` table (migration 56) with what it belongs to (a stack, a series, a subject or one occasion of a subject), its place and path, bytes, sha256, media type, who registered it and who acted for them, the registered model that made it (`model`, by id, digest or `name@version`, refused when no model answers to it), the pipeline run once that exists, and the derivative it supersedes, which is of the same kind and was made from the same subject and, where either names one, the same stack, or the registration is refused. `POST /api/derivatives?kind=&stack=&sha256=` takes the file as the body, streams it into the working place while hashing it, and registers nothing when the body is not the digest named (422); `GET /api/derivatives`, `GET /api/derivatives/{id}` and `GET /api/derivatives/{id}/content` list, describe and serve it, the bytes streamed with `X-Nils-Sha256`. The grants are the Pipelines page's: `pipelines:see` for the rows, `pipelines:see` at detail quasi for the bytes, since a mask or an embedding is drawn from the pixels, and `pipelines:work` to register. Every registration and download is audited (`derivative.register`, `derivative.read`). With no working place bound the capability is off: `capabilities.derivatives.enabled` is false with the reason, and the registering door and `nils derivative add` answer so. `nils derivative add|list|show` do the same at the keyboard, and `nils custody` lists the store. HTTP API contract version 7.

- The model registry (record 42 S2, D15). A model whose answers become registry facts, an encoder, a head, a pass or a segmenter, is registered by the digest of its canonical artifact with its card, and passes through four states: registered; admitted by a check that passed, which stays on it as the check that admitted it, while a check that failed is kept as an event and moves nothing; promoted, which is refused unless the model was admitted and retires the model promoted before it in the same task and slot, so a slot holds at most one; and retired. A head names its encoder by digest, which must be a registered encoder, and a card may name the label set a model was fitted on by digest. The slot is `site`, one site-wide model per task, or `cohort:<name>` for a cohort's own (R4). Every transition is a row of `model_event` and an audit row (`model.register`, `model.admit`, `model.promote`, `model.retire`), none of which moves the epoch. `nils model register --card FILE [--artifact FILE]`, where the artifact's sha256 fills in or checks the card's digest, `nils model admit MODEL --check FILE`, `nils model promote MODEL [--review-item ID] [--why TEXT]`, `nils model retire MODEL`, `nils model list` and `nils model show`, a model being named by its id, its digest or `name@version`; and the doors `GET` and `POST /api/models`, `GET /api/models/{id}` with its events, and `POST /api/models/{id}/admit`, `/promote` and `/retire`, under the grants `models:see` and `models:work` (R7). The schema is version 54, which adds the two tables empty. Custody lists the models.

- A model's answer names its registered model, and a model decides nothing on its own (record 42 S2 and R6). `review apply` with a model as the author names the model, `--model MODEL` at the keyboard and `{"kind": "model", "model": ...}` in `X-Nils-Actor` at the door, and is refused for a model nobody registered (404 at the door), for one that is not admitted or promoted, and for one registered for another task than the axis the item asks about; the decision carries the model's id and the registered model's version, never a version typed beside it. It is staged whatever the call asked, because a model's answer is evidence until a person commits it, an agent's apply is staged in the same way, whatever it asked, at the door and at the keyboard (`nils review apply --as agent`); an agent or a model withdraws only a decision its principal staged as that kind and nobody put in force, and a person's decision in force is refused to it (403 at the door); and a commit that would put a model's or an agent's answer in force is refused for an agent or a model: a staged decision whose author is an agent or a model, that names a model, or whose campaign item an agent or a model answered, is committed by a person only. `nils explain` and `GET /api/explain/{stack}` name the model of a decided value by its name, version and digest, with the person who committed it, and a release names the models whose answers are in force on its tree: in its report, in `nils release`'s output, in the `models` of its row and in the `Models` of the tree's `GeneratedBy`, which is left out when there are none, so a tree without a model's answer is written as before.

- The model contract, version 1 (`contracts/model/v1`): the card, identity by digest, kind, task and slot, the encoder and the label set by digest, metrics, preprocessing, intended use, limits and the runtime a check ran under; and the lifecycle, the four states, the transitions and the check record. The engine's registry and Kvasir's lifecycle keep the same idea, and Kvasir adopts the card when it next touches its lifecycle. The HTTP API contract is version 7 and the suite contract version 3, whose vocabulary holds the two new grants: the reviewer's set holds `models:see`, the operator's and the admin's both.

- A decision, a piece of classification evidence and a pick name the registered model a model's answer came from (`model_id`) and the campaign it was given in (`campaign_id`), and a decision names who put it in force (`committed_by`): its own author when it was written in force, and the person who committed it when it was staged, since a staged answer's author and the person who let it in are two different people. `nils classify` carries a decision's model and campaign onto the evidence it writes for the decided value. The schema is version 53; a registry from before gains the columns empty, and a decision written in force names its actor as its committer, while one staged and committed before says nobody, because nobody was recorded (record 42 S1).

- The classifier's signals carry the text an unresolved axis was matched against. `GET /api/classify/signals` answered the tiers, the spread, the review items, the shadowed and unused words and the fields of overridden stacks, and named only the axes that were left unresolved, so a reviewer, or a station tuning a site's words, had nothing to read a site's own word from until a person had already overridden a decision. `unresolved_texts` now samples the stacks in scope under the served pack, with the overlay the site adopted for it last as the registry was classified under it (`overlay` names it), at most 2,000 unless `sample` says otherwise, and per axis, for the stacks the pack leaves `axis_unresolved`, folds the pack's own normalised `search_text` into distinct texts with the number of stacks each covers, the most common ten first. A text is shown only when it covers at least five stacks of at least three people in the sample, since a description only one or two people's stacks carry is theirs rather than the site's word; the rest are withheld and counted. It is the text a keyword is matched against, it carries counts and texts and never a stack, and the door stays a reviewer's (kineuro/nils#94).

- `nils pack shape <dir>` says what a pack's rules can reach. It walks the rule sets in the order they run and names every value of the vocabulary that no rule can write, with the reason: no rule names it (`no_rule`), the rules that name it can never fire because what they read of the other axes is never reached before them (`no_valid_combination`), or every rule that writes it also writes a member of its exclusion group that beats it (`exclusion_group`). A value it calls unreachable is unreachable on every stack, because every flag and every word is taken as free; a value it calls reached is only not ruled out. It also lists the clauses that restate another axis rather than read the stack, a condition on other axes' values and nothing else, such as `technique = MPRAGE` giving `base = T1w`: those are implications of the schema, not evidence, and a label model that counted them as witnesses would count the other axis twice. `--json` gives every value and every clause; the short form gives the counts and the unreachable values, and `nils pack validate` prints the counts beside what it already said. On `mri@0.2.0` it finds 154 of 156 values reached: `construct.FatFraction`, which the pack's own comment says was never written, and `base.Unknown`, v0's internal marker for no answer, which no rule writes and no row should hold; and 67 clauses in 35 rules that restate another axis.

- `nils classify` records every rule's vote, not only the rule that decided. An ordered rule set stops at the first rule that fires, and a rule stops at the first of its clauses that holds, so the evidence of a stack names one rule per axis and nothing records what the rules behind it would have said: a rule's accuracy cannot be estimated from the stacks where an earlier rule spoke over it, and a label model has nothing to read. A run now also hears, on every stack, each clause that held of each rule whose set was entered and whose own condition held, whether or not the rule decided anything, and the value its rule gives the axis; the verdicts, the evidence and the review items are exactly what they were. The votes are kept compactly: a pack's voters, one per clause of a rule and axis it writes, are numbered once per pack version in `classification_voter`, and each stack holds one short list of `[voter, value]` pairs per phase in `classification_vote`. The schema is version 52. `nils classify votes` writes the matrix as tab-separated lines, `stack_id`, `axis`, `rule_set`, `rule`, `clause`, `value`, `tier` and `restates` (1 where the clause only restates another axis, which `nils pack shape` calls an implication of the schema: a label model reads it as a constraint, not a second witness), identities and the pack's own vocabulary only, to standard output or `--out FILE`, for one `--axis` if asked; `nils classify --no-votes` writes none and removes the ones it would have replaced. Hearing every rule costs a run over the synthetic registry of 6,983 stacks 11 per cent of its time, 0.85 s against 0.94 s, and the evaluator alone 13 per cent over the pack's own corpus, whose stacks hear seven clauses each. The vote matrix is not a door and no door queues it, because it writes where it is told.

- The body-part pipeline image, `nils-bodypart` (record 43 S5), v0's body-part detector ported from its worker into `pipelines/nils-bodypart/` with four entry points, each with its descriptor. `bodypart-embed` prepares a slice as v0 did (slope and intercept, a 1 to 99 percentile clip, a z-score, uint8, a letterbox to 224, the DICOM window ignored) and embeds, once per stack, every slice the later steps read with BiomedCLIP and SigLIP2, writing one file per stack and encoder in the engine's embedding format, keyed by the encoder's weight digest and the preparation's version, and computing only what the embeddings it is given lack. `bodypart-seed` is v0's two-pool seeding per value: the stacks the pack's rules already gave the value are picked for diversity by farthest-point sampling, and the stacks the rules left without a body part are subsampled by technique, scored by the zero-shot text-image margin at temperature 100, gated at 0.55 and 0.05 with v0's fallback, and picked the same way; its seeds are suggestions a curation campaign starts from, not a model's proposals. `bodypart-train` fits v0's head on a label set, a scaler, PCA and the regularisation chosen by cross-validation and a balanced logistic regression (or a forest, or an RBF SVM), and calibrates every head, not only the SVM as v0 did: temperature scaling for logistic regression, whose labels it leaves as they were, and `CalibratedClassifierCV` for the others. It reports the cross-validated accuracy, expected calibration error and Brier score before and after calibration, refuses a sealed label set or one whose `labels.tsv` is not the file its provenance names, and writes the head as JSON coefficients rather than a pickle, with a model card of kind `head` and task `axis:body_part` that names the label set by its digest. `bodypart-infer` reads five slices of an axial stack and the three centre slices of any other, applies v0's rule that brain in the upper slices and spine in the lower is brain-neck, weights the centre slice double on a sagittal or coronal stack, and proposes a value per stack with the probability of every class and the head's digest. The weights are baked in at pinned Hugging Face commits and the image runs offline, as a non-root user, on the CPU by default; a CUDA build is a build argument. The image is about 4 GB unpacked and 2.6 GB compressed, 2.3 GB of it the two encoders' weights. The CI job `bodypart` tests the rules, the head and each entry point on a synthetic manifest with stand-in encoders, without torch or weights; the release publishes the image.

- Record 43's rulings, over the four slices. A model's proposals are staged at the threshold its card names (`threshold`, added to `contracts/model/v1`); `nils run --threshold P` raises it for a run and is refused below it, and a card that names none has every proposal asked as a review item. A newer run of a model supersedes what its earlier runs proposed and nobody took on the stacks it proposes again: those `<axis>:model` items are closed as superseded and their uncommitted staged decisions withdrawn, and a stack the new run does not propose keeps its earlier proposal, carried out of a superseded group into an item of its own and staged again where the group was; a committed decision and an item an unfinished campaign asks stay. A card may name several encoders (`encoders`, in the order a head concatenates their features); the edge is a small table, `model_encoder` (migration 61, each head's one encoder backfilled as its first), and `model.encoder_model_id` keeps the first. An output may be the run's own file (`level: run` in `contracts/job/v1`), and one of kind `model` names its `card`: the runner hashes the artifact, checks the card names that digest, fills `trained_on` with the label set the run was given (and refuses a card that names another), registers the model in state registered with the card's encoders, and keeps the artifact as the run's derivative of kind `model`, which a later run given `--model` mounts at `/inputs/<input>`. `results.json` may carry `seeds` (per stack a suggested value and its margin) and a `selection`, apart from `proposals`; the runner keeps them as the run's one derivative of kind `seeds`, and `nils pipeline seeds <run> --save NAME` saves the suggested stacks as a selection a campaign starts from. `stacks.json` carries each stack's `orientation`, its `body_part` and `technique` as the registry holds them now, and its slice count. A run whose container exits 0 while units failed or went unreported, or a file it made was refused, ends `partial`, not `done`. The release a run's bids input was made by is marked `purpose run_input` (migration 62, the releases earlier runs name marked too), which `nils release --history` and `GET /api/releases` leave out unless `--runs` or `runs=true` asks for them. Embeddings a run writes are kept under the cache's key (stack, encoder, preprocessing version), the encoder registered from the card its results carry, so a second run of the same key registers nothing. The derivative kinds gain `seeds` and `model`, which only a run writes, and the scope `run`, a run's own file with no subject. The body-part descriptors take the final contract: `bodypart-train`'s head is a run-level model output, its card lists the chain's encoders and a threshold (0.70 by default); the image digests stay placeholders until the release publishes the image.

### Fixed

- What wave 43's proof on a real corpus found in the body-part loop. The `nils-bodypart` image has no entry point any more, so a descriptor's command line, which names the program, no longer ran it twice (`nils-bodypart nils-bodypart embed`, which exited 2); a contract test holds each command line against the image's entry point. The runner passes the engine process's own uid and gid to podman as `--user` beside `--userns keep-id`, never the group of a shared (setgid) working place, which keep-id does not map, so an image's own `USER` no longer wins and a run's outputs belong to the engine's user, not a sub-uid; before, the next run could not hard-link the embeddings the cache held (EPERM), and the rootless podman test now runs an image with a `USER` twice, the second run given the first's embeddings. Training no longer fails below about a hundred labels with the tuning on: the PCA sizes it tries are capped by the smallest split any fit sees, the nested one the temperature is fitted on included, and 16 and 32 join v0's sizes where fewer than two of those fit; `bodypart-train` takes `pca_components`, and `bodypart-infer` takes `allow_pickle` (off by default), so a forest or an SVM head whose card checks can be inferred through it. `nils campaign answer` and `nils campaign close` read the actor from `NILS_ACTOR` as the other verbs do, so an agent at the keyboard is recorded as an agent and what it settled is staged (R6), and a model names its registered model. A container that fails as a whole, with no `results.json` to say more, raises one `pipeline:qc` item for the run rather than one per unit (three failed runs had left 2,023). The runtime probe gives podman 30 seconds rather than 10, and one that does not answer in time is reported as unknown for now, not as "not rootless", and is not kept. A run podman itself refuses (exit 125) names the image store podman looked in and the `HOME` it ran with, and the pipelines guide says how a scratch `HOME` hides the store. A campaign made from grouped review items, such as a model's confidence bands, asks one item per stack not yet decided, each closing into that stack's decision, and the group stays open until its last member is decided; a member someone decided on their own while the campaign was open keeps that decision, and the close reports its item as skipped; the proof's campaign had 3 items for 239 stacks. `nils explain` names the campaign behind a decision it closed and every answer's author, so an agent that rated shows beside the person who closed and committed. `pipelines/nils-bodypart/` has a README.

- A pipeline run's container can no longer reach past its own output folder or its own stacks (record 43 review). The engine never reads or writes through a link a container planted: `results.json` and a card are read only where they resolve inside the output folder, the seeds are written, created new and with `O_NOFOLLOW`, into a folder of the engine's own beside the output (`<run>.nils/nils-seeds.json`), and every derivative row names its file where it really is, never a link. A unit takes only the files its own templates name, a run-level file that leads out is refused like any other, and each refused file is a `pipeline:qc` review item with status `refused` (unit `run` for a run's own file) while the run goes on, `partial`. An embedding is read whole, every value finite, before it is kept, and only by an encoder the run was given or the output declares (`encoders` on an embedding output, the weight digests the image bakes). An input is linked once and never copied onto its own link, a file is one row whatever names point at it, a `*` never stands beside a unit's word in a path template, a folder whose name holds `:` or `,` is bound through its parent and a file directly in a source root binds the root, both said in `summary.scope`. A run's proposals count only on the stacks of its selection and by the models it was given or made; the rest are dropped and counted (`out_of_run`), and a newer run supersedes only its own stacks. The container is given one read-only bind per folder that holds the selection's files instead of whole source places, falling back to the roots past 2,000 folders, which the run's `summary.scope` records; its derivative inputs are linked into its own inputs folder (a hard link, a copy across filesystems) instead of a bind of the whole derivatives tree; the mountpoint of a nested input (`/inputs/labels`, `/inputs/<model>`) is made before the run, since a real runtime cannot make one inside the read-only `/inputs`; and a mount path holding `:` or `,`, which the runtimes' mount syntax splits on, is refused. The stacks and derivative inputs are read from the registry a few hundred stacks at a time.

- Two selections that bind different values of a parameter, such as two lists of ids, no longer share a hash. The content hash leaves a parameter's value out, as rule 13 says a question's hash does, and a saved selection stored it, so promotion's match of a handle to the selection version that holds its ask, and the run cache of `POST /api/ask/run`, could take one for the other. A saved selection now keeps the bound hash, of what it selects with its values (equal to the content hash when none is bound), which `nils ask validate` and `POST /api/ask/validate` report beside `hash`; the run cache keys on it and promotion matches by it.

- A staged decision no longer moves the epoch. A decision written staged is not in force, yet its audit row advanced the epoch it had just recorded as its drift signature, so every staged decision had drifted from itself and a commit straight after it was refused without `--anyway`, as was every staged decision once another was staged after it. A stage now records its audit row without advancing the epoch, and the commit that puts it in force does, as it always has. A model's answers are staged by the batch (record 42 R6), so without this none could be committed as it was written.

- `nils explain` reads a decision made for a whole group. It looked a stack's decisions up by the stack, its series, its subject and its origin, and a group decision, which is the answer to a grouped question, was none of these, so the value showed as decided with no why and no committer. It is found through the group's membership now.

- The author of a decision is the verified actor, never the request body. `POST /api/review/{id}/apply` read `author_kind` from the JSON body and took `person` when it said nothing, so a token acting for an agent could write a decision at a person's rank, the highest, and v1 would have recreated the unmarked-author problem that left 4,692 model predictions in v0's archive looking like a person's. The kind, and a model's version, now come from `X-Nils-Actor` as the token allows it, and a person at a keyboard when there is no actor. A body may still say `author_kind` and `model_version`, so an older client keeps working where they agree with the actor, and one that says anything else is refused with 403 rather than recorded under a name the caller did not prove. The header cannot raise what the token proves either: a token whose `act` claim names an agent may call itself that agent or a model it runs, never a person, and never leave the actor absent, which reads as a person (record 42 S1). Nor can it rename the actor or choose the model: under an `act` claim the header's `name` is the claim's subject or is refused, and a model acting is the one the issuer bound into the claim as `act.model`, so an agent's token that carries no model cannot answer as one.

- A Canon FASE series is not RESOLVE. The normalizer carried v0's rewrite of `fase` to `resolve`, and `fase` stood among RESOLVE's words as well as HASTE's, so Canon's single-shot fast spin echo was called readout-segmented diffusion. No series in the group's archive is named with the word; the rewrite and both keywords are gone. HASTE does not keep it, because `fase` is the word for phase in Danish, Norwegian and Dutch and the pack cannot tell a Canon series from another by its manufacturer, so a series named for a phase would have been HASTE; a Canon FASE series is now the technique its header says, and a site that writes it can give HASTE the word through an overlay (`lists.technique.HASTE`).

- The ask reads YAML under the 1.2 core booleans. A plain `n`, `y`, `yes`, `no`, `on` or `off` was read as a boolean under the 1.1 rules, so a count bound as `n`, which is what a person and a small model call a count more often than anything else, could not be named in an option: `share {of: n, over: people}` was refused as `share names {of, over}` through the draft door while the same document as JSON ran. Only `true` and `false` are booleans now, and a name written as one of those is refused at its path in words saying it was read as a boolean and should be quoted (kineuro/nils#98). A boolean option is checked the other way round: `adjacent`, `strict` and `key` on a clause, an axis's `each` and a pinned source's `pin` hold `true` or `false` or are refused at their path, because `adjacent: no` now arrives as the text `no` and was read as the option's default, so it meant adjacent.

- A share measure names its denominator before it runs. Strict validation accepted `{share: {of: n}}` on `out` and the run answered `500 share names {of, over}`. The validator now refuses a measure that names no column, a share with no `over` and a percentile with no `p` at `out.measures[i]`, as it refuses a share clause in a `bind` without its denominator, and a measure that fails at run is a refusal with its path rather than a server error. The ask specification now points at `share` for a share inside a group: a `count {set: people}` in a group's `bind` counts per group, so dividing by it gives 1.0 on every row (kineuro/nils#99).

- The draft door leaves an inline list of literals a list. Its repair pass took any array inside a clause whose first element was a string for a clause written without its options map, so `["in", {}, ["field", {}, "name"], ["ms-cohort-a", "ms-cohort-b"]]` became a clause called `ms-cohort-a` and was refused as `ms-cohort-a is not an op of the language`, while the same document as JSON ran. An array in an argument is repaired as a clause only when its first element is an op of the language or an alias of one (kineuro/nils#101).

- A BIDS sidecar keeps every number the converter wrote. A release reads dcm2niix's JSON to add a field and writes it back, and the JSON reader's default number parsing misread some 17-digit decimals by one unit in the last place, so a slice timing of `212.91890726713459` left as `212.9189072671346`. The engine's JSON reader now parses every number to the nearest double (serde_json's `float_roundtrip`).

- The ask gate can see a stack the pack ruled out. The one predicate the ask describes in words for stacks, "not excluded", was missing until record 35, and the gate passed identically with and without it because `nils synth` wrote no excluded stack. The generator now writes one beside the first study of one background subject in six, the scanner's screen capture of the MPRAGE, written after every other row so that every earlier answer is unchanged, and the gate's new `ruled-out-stacks` fixture, with four of the existing ones, fails when the predicate is dropped.

- The MRI pack calls an EPI RESOLVE only when its sequence name says so. The technique `MS-EPI` (label `RESOLVE`) was also reached by segmented k-space beside an EPI readout, a combination v0 carried, and Siemens writes `SK` in SequenceVariant on every EPI it makes, single shot or not. Read against the printouts of five scanner protocols, that combination called 2,916 single-shot diffusion stacks and 462 BOLD stacks RESOLVE, and every 3D GRASE ASL series too; none was. The value now comes from the readout-segmented sequence's own name (`*re_b`) or its words, so the diffusion series are `DWI-EPI`, the BOLD series and their motion-corrected copies `BOLD`, the ASL series `ASL`, and a gradient-echo EPI that nothing else names `GRE-EPI`.

- The MRI pack takes flow compensation from ScanOptions and not from a name. `FlowComp` was also reached by the words `flow comp`, `flowcomp`, `gmn` and `fc` anywhere in the text, and a 3D SPACE protocol named `... 0 flow comp`, whose printout says flow compensation is off and whose ScanOptions hold only `PFP`, was FlowComp on 87 acquired stacks and 125 reformats of them. The value now comes from the scan options alone, `FC` as Siemens writes it and `FC_SLICE_AX_GEMS` or `FC_FREQ_AX_GEMS` as GE writes it per axis; a site whose names are reliable can still give it words through an overlay (`lists.modifier.FlowComp`).

- The perfusion-weighted image of an ASL series is not a t test map. Siemens writes `TTEST` in the image type of the perfusion-weighted image its 3D GRASE ASL computes, which is the label-control subtraction and the image the series exists for, and the construct `TTestMap` took the token at its word on 249 of them. `TTestMap` now requires the stack not to be ASL, and means a statistic over a series everywhere else.

- A series' own body-part evidence beats the body part its exam was registered as. The MRI pack's body-part rules read one text that joined the series' names with `BodyPartExamined`, and a spine word anywhere in it decided, but `BodyPartExamined` is written from the exam's registration and says the same thing on every series: on five scanner protocols, brain series in exams registered as a spine were spine (36 stacks and 6 reformats) and spinal series in an exam registered as a brain were brain (10). The pack now reads three kinds of evidence in turn and the first that says anything decides: the series' own words (`anatomy_text`, which no longer holds `BodyPartExamined`), then a spine receive coil, then the stated body part (`stated_anatomy_text`, a new normalized text of `BodyPartExamined` alone). A head and neck coil decides nothing, since it received brain and spinal series alike, and neither does a coil named for the spine and the head or neck together, such as a head, neck and spine coil, which images all three. Where neither the series' names nor its coil say anything, the stated body part still decides, so 42 of those 52 stacks keep their wrong answer: nothing in their headers tells them apart.

- A plane resliced from a susceptibility acquisition is a reformat. The MRI pack's SWI route tried every output it knows and then fell back to the processed SWI image, so the MPR planes the console writes from a susceptibility EPI, whose image type says `MPR` and names no SWI output, were the construct `SWI` and a scanner-derived image, 375 of them on five scanner protocols. The route now takes `MPR` before its last resort, so those planes are the construct `MPR` and the disposition `reformat`, as every other MPR is; their provenance stays `SWIRecon`, which the name gives first.

- The MRI pack's intent rules no longer test for a construct nothing writes. `FatFraction` is declared on the construct axis and was never written, in v0 or here, and the Dixon and dual-echo field map rules of the intent cascade each tested for it where it could never be. The two tests are gone and the value stays, because a vocabulary is a contract; `nils pack shape` still reports it unreachable, with the reason.

- A longhand rule's `requires` gates it. The loader read `requires` on a value of an axis file and skipped it on a rule written longhand, which is the same rule in its long form, so a longhand rule fired where its author said it must not and nothing said so. It is honoured now, is checked like any other condition (a rule decided before the passes may not require an axis decided after them), and any key a longhand rule does not have is refused with its file, line and path instead of being ignored.

- What the scanner computed and wrote as an image of its own is scanner derived. The motion-corrected copy of a BOLD run (`MOCO`, which Siemens writes on an ORIGINAL image) and the perfusion-weighted image of an ASL series (`SUB`, the label minus the control) reached no disposition rule, because neither has a construct or a provenance that says so, and both were the acquisition. The printouts switch both on as products of the scanner; a new disposition rule reads the two tokens, so the run and the label and control images stay the acquisition and the copies are `scanner_derived`.

### Changed

- `nils review apply --as model` names the registered model with `--model MODEL` (its id, digest or `name@version`) instead of a free-text `--model-version`, which is kept as another spelling of `--model`, and the version recorded is the registered model's.

- The MRI pack is version 0.3.0, because the rules the five scanner protocols proved wrong now give other answers: RESOLVE, FlowComp, the t test map of an ASL series, the body part, the reformats of a susceptibility acquisition, and the disposition of what the scanner computed. Its vocabulary is unchanged, so a question asked of 0.2.0 means the same words here.

- The MRI pack is version 0.4.0: `body_part` has the value `chest` (record 43 R3), which v0's image classifier predicted and the body-part pipeline proposes. No rule writes it, as v0's keyword detector had no chest words, so `nils pack shape` names it unreachable by a rule and a chest reaches a stack only as a model's proposal a person commits. A BIDS release names it `acq-Chest`. Every other value and rule is as it was.

- An install fetches Kvasir at `v1.0.0-alpha.8` and the assistant at `v1.0.0-alpha.26` (record 47): Kvasir serves a model card and takes a model server as a backend, and the assistant reads Kvasir's pi-messages door at `/v1/pi`. `nils update --all` moves both.

## [1.0.0-alpha.37] - 2026-09-23

### Fixed

- An install survives a reboot. `nils setup` wrote the units of Kvasir and the assistant as `ExecStart=/usr/bin/env node ...`, which looks Node up on the service manager's PATH, and at boot the user manager's PATH does not yet hold `~/.local/bin`, where a Node of an account's own is linked. Both services exited 127 five times and stayed down until somebody started them by hand, while after a login the same units ran, which is why no test ever saw it. Setup now finds the Node it builds them with, the first Node 22 or newer on its PATH, and every unit names it by that absolute path, as the engine, the desk, llama.cpp and the supervisor were already named; the path is kept as it was found, so a Node replaced behind its link is the one that runs. The install records it as `node`, so that an update run from a PATH with no Node on it, as a service's can be, writes the same one again, and `nils update --all` writes every unit again as it always has, so an install made before this is mended by its next update. Where no Node is left, neither on the PATH nor at the recorded path, the update says so and the unit falls back to the manager's PATH, and the supervisor's install report carries `node` with its path, whether it is still there and what to do if it is not. Units in containers are unchanged, since the image has its own Node.

- `nils ask run` finds the installed pack as `nils classify` does. It was the one verb that demanded `--pack-dir`: `digest`, `classify`, `explain` and the other `nils ask` verbs that answer a question looked for the pack where it is installed, and `ask run` exited 2 before reading its document, so the exact command line of a run over an archive stopped working the day the flag became required and a stored script or a saved ask that ran yesterday failed today. Without the flag it now looks where the others look, `NILS_PACK_DIR` first and then the registry's own packs and the installed ones, and `--pack-dir` still overrides that and is still an error when it names a directory with no pack in it.

- `nils explain` names the review items it counts. The one command whose whole job is saying why a stack was judged the way it was ended on `2 review item(s) were raised for this stack`, with no axis, no losing value and no way to tell which of the queue's questions those two were: a stack carrying two conflicts showed the seven axes it decided and neither conflict. Each item that holds the stack is now its own line, by its id and kind, its status, and what it asks of this stack, read from the stack's own evidence: `review item 12 technique:conflict (open, one of 132 stack(s)): technique MDME over SE`, followed by the rule that decided it and the rule it overruled. A weak answer says its value, its confidence and the threshold it fell below, a missing value says so, a decision that overrides a rule says both answers, and a split says the reason and how many one-image stacks it made. An earlier run's superseded questions are left out. `nils explain --json` and `GET /api/explain/{stack}`, which answer one document, carry the same items under `review`, beside the `review_items` count they already had.

- A `run-` index means a repeat, and two different acquisitions never share one name in silence. BIDS gives `run-` to one acquisition measured again, and the release wrote it wherever two stacks of one subject, session and datatype came out with the same filename. Measured over a corpus of 702 named stacks, 403 of them took a `run-` index and 270, 67 per cent, sat under a name that covered more than one acquisition; over a legacy archive of 437,069 classified stacks, only 18 per cent of colliding names are repeats at all. A `run-2` that is really a different echo time is a claim nothing downstream can audit, because a validator passes it and a reader believes it, and the groups are small, so it has always looked exactly like an ordinary rescan. The index is now issued only where the two stacks are measurably the same acquisition, and the test is declared rather than hidden, so a person can argue with it and two releases agree: two different series, every axis the pack decided equal, the coverage not differing, the receive coil equal, the echo time, repetition time, inversion time, flip angle and b value within two per cent, the slice thickness and spacing within five, and the averages, echo train length, acquisition matrix, image size, scan options, scanning sequence and sequence variant equal exactly. A value only one side recorded is not a difference, because an absence is not a measurement; a whole group is one acquisition only when every pair in it is. Where a name covers more than one acquisition, no name is written at all: the stacks go to `sourcedata/` under the informative names of the descriptive layout, which are unique, and one review item per name says which stacks they are and what differs between them, in the pack's own words, which is also the evidence a new axis would need. A group whose suffix admits no `run-` at all is handled the same way, where before the second stack silently rewrote the first. The report says the three numbers behind every index the tree carries, `shared_names`, `repeats` and `not_repeats`, `nils release` prints them, and the tree's README says what its own `run-` indices are worth, because a silent change here is what caused the problem.

- A `run-` index means a rescan: the same stack, in the same place, made again at its own moment. Inside one session a rescan is the same protocol run again, so the test above now asks for everything to agree and only the time to differ, and three things it let through are refused a shared name and asked about instead. Measured over one research cohort of 143 subjects in a legacy archive, every group of more than ten series that agreed on the physics and the axes was readout-segmented diffusion stored one series per gradient direction, 148 stacks in three groups, and in a second cohort of 43 subjects 496 stacks in nine; 386 pairs in the first cohort shared one acquisition time, one acquisition written twice rather than made twice; and two stations of one prescription agree on every parameter, the slice count and the extent included. So the test now also compares where the stack sits: the centre of its images' positions in three dimensions, where the part along the slice normal is held to the wider of a millimetre and half a slice step, because two stacks closer than that sample the same positions, and the part across the plane of the slices to 5 mm, because a rescan re-centred on the same anatomy moves a few millimetres and a second station most of a field of view, which is what tells the stations of a sagittal spine apart when every `SliceLocation` agrees; a registry digested before keeps no position per image, and there the centre along the normal from `SliceLocation` is compared alone; the moment it was made, two stacks with one earliest acquisition date and time being `acquired at the same moment`; the diffusion gradient directions themselves beside their number, every b value and the phase-encoding direction; the temporal position and the number of temporal positions; the whole `ImageType` as a set of tokens; the series description, the sequence name, the body part examined and the contrast administration beside the protocol name, folded for case and whitespace only, so the counter a scanner appends to a re-run step is a difference and not a spelling; the slice orientation, as a label and as cosines within a hundredth; the modality, the scanner and its field strength; the pixel spacing and bandwidth; and the echo numbers, the number of images, the 2D or 3D acquisition type and how the series was split. A value only one side recorded is still not a difference. What passes is numbered in the order it was made, by the earliest acquisition time of each stack, then the series number, then the stack's id, where before it was numbered by series id. An instance gains the `ImagePositionPatient` it was read with, the fingerprint gains `slice_centre_mm`, `centre_x_mm`, `centre_y_mm`, `centre_z_mm`, `earliest_acquisition_date`, `earliest_acquisition_time`, `series_number`, `dwi_gradients`, `temporal_position` and `temporal_positions`, a series gains the `SeriesNumber` the scanner wrote, the schema is version 50, and the fingerprint's revision moved to 3, so the next `nils fingerprint` derives every stack again without `--force`; a series digested before keeps no number until its files are read again, and its stacks are ordered by id after the time. No text reaches a name: a pair its texts alone separate is refused and asked about, and the text never reaches a filename, the report or a review item.

- A release report names the categories it applied. A release has recorded which categories of element it removed since there were categories to record, because "de-identified" is not a property a file can carry without saying under what rule, and it recorded them on its row alone: not in the report it prints, not in the JSON one, not in `/api/releases`. So the sixth category landed, the accession number and the device serial number started leaving the archive, and the only thing a person reading a release could see was that two more tags had moved. The report now names them, in the order they were given, and where fewer than all six were applied it names the rest too, because what a release left in is the half of that sentence an operator has to see: `categories patient, ids` and `not removed trial, provider, institution, times; those elements leave the file unchanged`. `nils release --history --json` and `/api/releases` answer with the same names, read off the release row, so the report and the door cannot say different things about one release.

- `nils classify` counts stacks as stacks and items as items. The line a person reads first said `review items 830, 99.3% of the stacks, as 58 question(s)`: 830 is a count of items, 836 is a count of stacks, and the share was one divided by the other. Only 546 of those 836 stacks, 65.3 per cent, carried anything for a person at all, because one stack can raise a conflict and a weak answer and a split all at once and be three items in that total. The run now counts the distinct stacks its questions stand on, from the members it wrote, and says three numbers against the three things they count: `stacks to review 546, 65.3% of the 836 classified` and `review items 830, on those stacks, as 58 question(s)`. The share is stacks over stacks, the denominator is printed beside it, and every one of the three can be read back out of the registry. The JSON report carries the new count as `review_stacks`.

- A release removes the accession number, and the other direct identifiers its categories had never named. The five categories were carried from v0 tag for tag, and `(0008,0050)` was in none of them, nor `(0018,1000)`: a release wrote the hospital's own identifier for the examination, and the serial number of the machine that made the images, through to the released file verbatim, on every file it has ever written, while the change list it records said nothing about either, so nothing a reader could open said the elements had even been there. An accession number in the hands of somebody who can reach the hospital's systems undoes everything else a release did. A sixth category, `ids`, is the home those elements never had: 47 elements measured against DICOM PS3.15 Annex E's basic profile, holding the accession number and the issuer that says whose numbering it is, the study, admission and service episode ids with their issuers, the placer and filler order numbers, the machine's serial number, its own UID, the gantry, the unique device identifier and the stations, AE titles and locations the step was scheduled and performed at, the identifying, study, acquisition, image and visit comments and the description of the constraint on the patient's data, which are where a name, an accession number or a telephone number gets typed, the patient's birth name, alias, photograph, responsible organisation, issuer qualifiers, ward and institution of residence, the intended recipients of the results, who entered the order, from where and on which telephone, the bare person name of the content items, and the retired results and interpretation elements an old archive still carries. It goes with the other five by default, because the safe set is the one nobody had to think about, it is named `ids` where a run picks its categories, and the categories a run applied are recorded on the release as they always were. The change list names each element it removed and how many files it came out of, so a reader sees that an element was handled rather than absent by luck.

- The set is considered rather than complete, and what is left in is left in on purpose. The study and series descriptions, the protocol name and the sequence name stay: they are the evidence a pack classifies on and the only thing that lets a released tree be read or judged again, and they were read at digest long before a release writes. Dates stay under the date policy and UIDs under the UID remapping rather than joining a category, because two policies on one element is how one of them comes to be forgotten. Beside the categories the engine now holds one list of its own, what may never survive a release: the direct identifiers across every category that holds one, written out rather than derived from the categories, since a list built from what the code removes can only ever agree with the code, which is how an accession number was written through for as long as it was. A test writes files carrying every element on that list, releases them, and fails with the element's number if one comes out the other side or its value is still in the bytes. What the pseudonymiser removes is unchanged, so the tag policy it serves is the one a client already read.

- A release counts the stacks whose file will not say whether their pixels carry text, and no longer keeps them. Wave 3 section 8.4 says a stack whose `BurnedInAnnotation` says yes, or whose image type carries a token that means a picture of a screen, raises a review item and is not written, and that where the tag is absent the release says how many stacks it could not judge. The code held that third answer as well, and `BurnedInAnnotation` is absent on most of the series of a real archive: a default release of a selection reaching 772 stacks wrote 174 of them, most of its subjects released nothing at all, and the number a release was supposed to print was the number of stacks it was keeping. The tag saying yes and the screenshot token hold a stack exactly as they did; an absent tag does not. Such a stack is written and counted, and the count is in the report, under `could not judge` beside the stacks held back, and in the release's own row, as `unjudged` beside `burned_in`, with `policy.on_unknown` beside them saying what was done with them, since the same number reads one way under each setting. `--on-unknown` is unchanged in spelling and gains a default: `write`, which writes and counts, and `hold`, which holds every unjudged stack and files a question about each, as the default did. A site that wants what it had asks for `hold` on the command line or at the release door; a site that does not now releases what it selected and reads one number about what nobody can judge. A dataset released before this and released again under the new default gets its unjudged stacks as stacks added to the next version, and the stacks it already wrote are rewritten once, because the run's own policy is part of what decides a file's bytes.

- A stack held back by a release is asked about once, and not again by every later release. A release is re-run whenever anything upstream of it changes, and the file says the same thing about its pixels every time, so a question per held stack per release is the same question filed again: two releases of one selection filed two queues of identical rows, hundreds each, and a queue nobody can read is a review spine that has stopped working. A stack that already carries a `release.burned_in` or a `release.unjudged` item, answered or not, raises no second one, whatever the release. What recurs is the count in the report and in the release row, which is where a fact about a whole run belongs. Nothing closes or rewrites the items already filed.

- The body-part rules read the value before anything removes its words. The MRI pack's normalizer drops `brain` and `head` as boilerplate, which is right for the axes that tell techniques and constructs apart and wrong for the one axis those two words are the answer to: on a run over an archive of 836 stacks, 607 of the 636 whose `BodyPartExamined` says `BRAIN,HEAD` came out with no body part at all, while `SPINE` and `NECK`, which are not on the removal list, decided 71 of 71. Nothing was flagged, because an axis with no value is a question only where the pack says a value is always expected, so it read as success. The pack now derives a second text, `anatomy_text`, from the same six fields with the same folding and none of the removals, and every clause of the body-part rule set reads it: the stated body part decides, whatever the description says, and a description that mentions anatomy is no longer blinded by a word dropped for somebody else's benefit. The removals stay exactly as they are for `search_text`, so no other axis moves, and the damaged and accented spellings still fold to the gap the keyword lists are written with. The pack's own corpus carries the six cases that say so, including the one that shows the boilerplate is still boilerplate where it was meant to be.

- A neck a rule can name is a neck the axis can produce.
